# CEDIA — SSOT plan

This is the **single authoritative plan and spec** for the CEDIA project. Superseded
standalone plans/specs are retired; HANDOFF.md and READMEs are navigation aids. If product
requirements change, this file changes; supporting records do not become another spec.

**Implementation reading contract:**

- §0, §2.1–§2.8, §3.A–§3.D, §5–§8 and §11 define the current target. §2.8/§8.2 specify
  the full pinned-OMP scope; §8.1 defines D/W/N/F. Follow §8 dependencies and track §10 item 70.
- §3.1–§3.5 and §3.H preserve measurements/reference history. Where their appearance or
  mechanism differs from the consolidated screen/settings contract, §3.C–§3.D/§6.4 govern.
- §9 records dated implementations. §10 items 1–68 and the requirement graph retain IDs and
  earlier acceptance descriptions; reconcile them through item 70 before touching that work.
  They neither revive obsolete renderers/relay choices nor certify today's build.
- Wayfinder tickets are decision rationale after consolidation, not a parallel queue to
  restart. Their old open/assigned labels do not reopen the completed product interview.
- “Proposed” file/API names in the current contract identify planned implementation seams,
  not unresolved product choices. Resolve routine details locally; record any material
  deviation in this plan. New upstream features and optional research are separate intake.

Read order for a new session:

1. [`AGENTS.md`](../../AGENTS.md) — project rules and invariants, applied every turn.
2. This file — §0 for the definition; §2.2–§2.8 for ownership/capabilities; §3.C–§3.D
   for task/screens; §6.4–§6.5 for settings/remote; §8 for execution; §10 for open work.
3. `docs/maintenance/evidence/<receipt>/` — the runtime receipts this plan cites.

---

## 0. Product definition

**CEDIA is an owned Agentic-IDE forked from Code-OSS: the most universal and versatile
workspace for coding tasks and projects.**

- CEDIA defines its own product spec — surfaces, interaction, architecture, visual design —
  on top of the Code-OSS base it forks. It copies no other product's spec.
- The harness is **OMP and only OMP**. Brand, icons and product name are CEDIA's. CEDIA ships
  no Tab engine, no cloud agents and no private models.
- References: **Code-OSS** for the editor base; **OMP** for execution, transcript and the
  agent surfaces (plan / goal / subagent / MCP / approval schema / queue).
- The product target is the complete applicable pinned-OMP surface in §2.8, a full
  Code-OSS IDE and same-Mac remote control. §8.1 F defines completion; D/W/N are
  intermediate deliveries. Native desktop windows use Electron, not an all-SwiftUI UI.

## 1. Reference precedence

| Layer | Reference |
|---|---|
| Product, surfaces, interaction, window chrome, colour / geometry / motion | **CEDIA workspace contract** (§3 measurements as data, §5 decisions, §10 for what is open) |
| Behaviour only OMP has (plan / goal / subagent / MCP / approval schema / queue) | OMP contract |
| Execution and transcript ownership | **OMP** — one owner, never a second |

When the layers disagree, the CEDIA workspace contract wins — and appearance always uses
CEDIA's token layer (§3.3). `AGENTS.md` is the entry point and owner of project working rules;
this plan owns product requirements. READMEs and evidence do not override either owner.
The owner answers linked from §10 item 69 have been consolidated into the current contract.
New explicit owner instructions supersede that baseline when given and must be reconciled
here. Unadopted research remains optional; dated evidence applies to its original revision.
Item 69 records planning closure; item 70 owns implementation status.

## 2. Architecture

```
CEDIA.app  (Code-OSS fork, CEDIA brand, pinned ea1912fd…)
├─ Agents window   ← the base's window identity (profile/restore/close lifecycle);
│      renderer = the standalone Cedia agent bundle (`vs/cedia/agent/index.html`, patch 0056)
│      sidebar (New thread · Search · Projects) · composer · slim status bar
│      right dock (Changes / Browser / Terminal / Files / Git / Device)
├─ IDE window      ← the normal workbench (Explorer / editor / LSP / debug / terminal)
│      dock `cediaComposerDock` = the same bundle in compact form (`agent-ui/ide.html`)
└─ one CEDIA host  ← lifecycle · journal · artifacts · relay · devices · git · PTY terminals
        │
   OMP — the only harness, owner of execution + transcript
```

Rules:

1. The Agents surface is a **native window of the base** — window identity, profile, restore
   state and close/quit lifecycle are Code-OSS's — and since patch `0056` that window renders
   the **standalone Cedia agent bundle** (`vs/cedia/agent/index.html`) instead of
   `sessions.html`; the main-process bridge is `registerCediaAgentWindowBridge`
   (`patches/desktop/0056`, `apps/macos/src/agent-window-main.ts`). Window chrome policy
   (menus, zoom, traffic lights, status bar) is owned by one main-process module for both
   windows (§3.A/§3.B; §10 item 55).
2. Both windows render **one agent surface**: the same bundle (`index.html` standalone,
   `ide.html` in the IDE dock). Its adapter (`apps/macos/agent-window/src/cedia-adapter.ts`)
   reads sessions/composer/transcript from the host `/v1` API, and OMP answers the host. The
   extension's native `chatSessionsProvider` surface has no renderer left since `0056` and
   retires with §10 item 56.
3. **OMP is the only harness.** Registering or using `copilot` / `claude` / `codex` harnesses is
   forbidden, as is wiring GitHub Copilot auth, sign-in or BYOK. The base's
   `src/vs/platform/agentHost/**` is Copilot-bound and serves only as a shape reference.
4. The IDE window: Code-OSS owns buffers, undo, LSP, debug and extensions.
5. The host owns lifecycle, journal, artifacts, relay and devices. The iPhone client is a
   projection of the same session, never a second owner.
6. OMP owns execution and transcript. The UI never creates a second agent loop.

### 2.1 Selected direction and integration boundaries (2026-09-23)

The owner selected question 49A: retain the CEDIA/Code-OSS application foundation and
the AI-primary/full-IDE two-window shape, and redesign the Synara–OMP integration and
shared settings boundary. The topology above describes the retained implementation;
the table below defines the responsibility split for that selected direction. It does
not claim that the existing adapter, settings, or remote clients already fulfill it.

| Responsibility | Single owner | Reuse and adaptation boundary |
|---|---|---|
| Desktop windows, editor buffers/undo, language services, debugger, extensions | CEDIA-branded Code-OSS application | Retain the IDE and two-window foundation; reconcile close/background/Quit and explicit task-to-IDE handoff with the accepted behavior |
| Agent conversation and task-management presentation | Shared Synara-derived agent UI | Reuse components and applicable workflows in the primary window and IDE Agent Chat; native iPhone remains a separate client of the same task services, not a promised shared renderer |
| Agent execution, tools, conversation history, model/provider catalog and tool-permission decisions | OMP | One execution owner per task; preserve shared CLI configuration and credentials. A running CLI session must be attached to or explicitly handed off, never independently executed by two owners |
| Project/task associations, worktree lifecycle, command coordination, artifacts and client synchronization | One CEDIA application service boundary | Reuse existing host services and suitable Synara service implementations behind that boundary. Importing an upstream implementation replaces the corresponding owner; it must not create parallel authoritative task stores or hosts |
| App appearance/layout/notification preferences and capability filtering | One CEDIA settings service | Present the Synara-led settings UI with working shared read/write paths; renderer localStorage may cache values but must not create divergent window-specific authorities. Account for device-specific preferences explicitly |
| Persistent OMP settings/auth, IDE settings, and keybindings | Their existing OMP / Code-OSS / keybinding-file owners | One settings center routes reads and writes to these owners. It does not copy credentials or mirror all settings into a second store. Distinguish app theme from editor syntax/font, task model from persistent defaults, and category reset from data deletion |
| Paired devices, remote application commands, terminal lifecycle and notification delivery | CEDIA host and its remote-client boundary | Evaluate the existing remote/terminal services for reuse. OMP collab is a candidate for OMP session projection, not proof of a complete device, model-selection, new-task, or terminal protocol. Tailscale transport is selected in §6.5; availability remains gated in §10 |

Synara remains the source of applicable application experience, not an additional harness.
Reusing its services is preferred where compatible with these ownership boundaries; the
specific modules to import, adapt, or replace require source and behavior verification.
Do not implement a second application backend solely to make generic upstream API names
appear supported, and do not drop an applicable feature merely because the current adapter
does not implement it. Record its capability status until a real path is verified.

This direction preserves the owner's upstream policy: pin a working integrated version,
evaluate relevant Synara/OMP/Code-OSS changes, and enable updates only after the affected
workflow passes. There is no automatic feature-parity or zero-maintenance promise. User-
facing identity, settings help/reset/search, and updater routing use CEDIA; required upstream
attribution is preserved. Planning closure is recorded in §10 item 69; implementation
readiness and blockers remain under item 70.

### 2.2 Integration design (2026-09-23)

The following design implements the accepted product choices; new interface/file names are
proposed implementation targets, not claims about the current build. Existing supported
paths are retained. Reuse Synara components and bounded services at their owning seam; do
not start its generic provider manager, auth store, or independent session server beside OMP.

| Boundary | Retained source / proposed change | Contract |
|---|---|---|
| UI to application | `apps/macos/agent-window/src/cedia-adapter.ts`, shared desktop bridge, `apps/host/src/router.ts` | Translate UI actions once to the CEDIA protocol. Derive visibility from an enumerated capability result, not optimistic booleans or empty fake catalogs. Both desktop windows use the same adapter. |
| Application to harness | `packages/omp-adapter/src/client.ts`, `apps/host/src/service.ts` | OMP owns the loop, tools, transcript and session file. Host metadata references that file; its bounded event journal is a client projection. Preserve native OMP command/error semantics. |
| Task commands | `packages/protocol/src/index.ts`, host command/store paths | Preserve command ID, device identity, incarnation and payload hash. Serialize acceptance per task; reconcile a command by ID after timeout. `outcome_unknown` is not permission to replay a mutation. Reject stale incarnations with refresh/reconciliation instructions. |
| Project and workspace | host `store.ts`, `workspaces.ts`, `git.ts`; IDE bridge | CEDIA owns project/task/worktree associations; Git owns repository facts. One task records its source commit, effective cwd and retained restoration revision. Never infer workspace identity from whichever window currently has focus. |
| Shared preferences | proposed host `settings.ts` and `capabilities.ts`; protocol types and versioned OMP settings bridge | One revisioned CEDIA app-preference store in the host state directory; atomic validated patches and stale-revision rejection. Renderer storage is only a cache or explicitly device-local state. Mac task drafts have one shared device-level owner across both windows (§3.C), while other devices retain separate drafts and layout state. OMP, Code-OSS and keybindings remain separate authoritative owners reached through this service boundary. |
| Remote clients | `apps/ios/src/core/{api,transport,relay}.ts`, host auth/router/remote | Web and native clients project the same host tasks. Controller tokens never become owner tokens. Pairing/revocation are Mac-owner operations; capability results constrain all entry points, not just menus. |
| Terminal ownership | existing OMP terminal registry and window-specific shells | Keep IDE workbench terminals private. The remote Terminal panel controls identified host-accessible terminals on the Mac; it does not claim to attach to arbitrary IDE/Electron PTYs. The remote user shell is a separate host-owned PTY in the task cwd, with multiple views of that same PTY rather than a clone per client. |

**Feasibility gates, owned by the implementation slices in §8:**

- Running CLI-session attachment: prove a supported OMP attach/control path that preserves
  the live owner. If unavailable, show the session as externally running/read-only and allow
  explicit continuation only after that owner exits and an exclusive acquisition succeeds.
  Do not label this fallback as complete running-CLI continuity. Escalate the missing feature
  for owner review; do not copy the transcript into a new task to claim success.
  Source check at vendored OMP v18.1.18 confirms the current adapter always spawns its own
  `rpc-ui` process. OMP collab already preserves a live host/guest owner and is a bounded
  reuse candidate, but CEDIA has no guest adapter and collab lacks the complete application
  command set. Its default public relay must not silently replace the selected Tailscale path.
  The existing CEDIA lock covers cooperating CEDIA-launched processes, not arbitrary CLI
  writers; an external-session handoff needs a proven shared ownership mechanism before
  enabling same-file continuation. An idle-looking session file alone is not proof of release.
- Shared OMP configuration: map each setting to a supported OMP read/write/reload path.
  A file-level integration, if required, must preserve unknown fields, use atomic replacement,
  detect concurrent CLI edits, and verify readback. Changes requiring a new session are marked
  accordingly; they must not silently restart a running turn. The inspected OMP Settings
  class supplies `get/set/flush/reloadFromDisk`, but generic settings RPC is absent. R2's
  concrete target is a versioned, allowlisted CEDIA extension to OMP RPC that uses those
  methods, returns effective layer provenance and verifies persisted readback. Ordinary
  `set` writes the global layer; project/overlay values are read-only through that API
  (specialized model-role commands are separate). Do not present a global write as a
  project write. A CLI config broker can support global changes if explicitly marked
  pending for running sessions; it does not establish live reload. Persisted-file conflict
  detection remains required even with the RPC bridge.
- Permissions: enumerate actual tool-policy enforcement before exposing editable policy
  controls. Forward OMP's existing request once; retain independent editor-buffer protection
  where it prevents data loss rather than duplicating tool authorization. OMP owns
  `tools.approvalMode`/`tools.approval`; current CEDIA editor/host effects also have their
  own authorization hook. R1/R2 must classify those checks and remove duplicate broad
  prompts while retaining CEDIA path/device and unsaved-buffer guards. This is a target,
  not evidence that today's build already presents exactly one prompt.
- Remote: Tailscale is selected in §6.5. Existing Paseo wire compatibility does not establish
  a working private-network gateway; the gateway and enrollment paths still require integration. Native iPhone, cellular, and locked-device delivery
  require their own evidence; a web export or mocked socket is insufficient.
- Applicable upstream features with absent OMP or host support stay on the capability-status
  page. The implementation does not replace OMP or silently add another paid/external service
  to satisfy a feature. Material dependencies return to the owner with concrete alternatives.

### 2.3 Applicable capability inventory (source audit, 2026-09-23)

Status vocabulary: **retain** means an existing path is reusable, **adapt** means integration
work is required, **pending** means an applicable capability lacks a proven owner/API or
runtime path. None means a new packaged acceptance pass. This inventory covers the current
vendored feature families; future upstream additions go through §8's intake policy.
Vendor paths below are under `apps/macos/agent-window/vendor/synara/`; desktop adapter means
`apps/macos/agent-window/src/cedia-adapter.ts`. One family can have different readiness by surface.

| Feature family | Source and selected owner | Integration treatment / acceptance boundary |
|---|---|---|
| Chat/composer/model/effort | Vendor `apps/web/src/components/chat/`; adapter → host model catalog → OMP | Retain components; adapt queued/pending-model state. Only OMP executes; model-provider tabs come from its catalog. |
| Transcript/tools/approvals/presentations | Adapter; host service/journal; OMP frames and session file | Retain projection; adapt one-response ordering and error states. OMP owns history and tool authorization. |
| Projects/tasks/search/history | Vendor Sidebar/routes/search; host store/router/workspaces | Retain one project/task index; adapt worktree lifecycle and CLI discovery. Search/index results do not imply complete historical pagination/import. Unsupported import/replay APIs need explicit implementation. |
| Attachments/files/mentions | Vendor composer utilities; desktop staging/files; adapter prompt builder | Retain image/context conversion; adapt immutable unsaved snapshots and cross-window target selection. OMP RPC accepts text/images; generic file context is an authorized CEDIA snapshot conversion with size/type handling, not an OMP-native file upload. Distinguish slash skills from unsupported provider/plugin mentions. |
| Skills/slash/compaction | Vendor slash UI; adapter `get_available_commands` and `compact` → OMP | Retain session discovery/compaction; pending sessionless settings catalog and persistent management. Empty catalog stubs cannot certify availability. |
| MCP/hooks/plugins | OMP config/capability paths; vendor discovery/external-MCP contracts | Adapt verified OMP facilities; pending settings discovery/write/reload. Synara plugin/provider marketplace is not automatically an OMP plugin system. |
| Plan/goals/subagents/sidechat | Vendor PlanSidebar/sidechat components; OMP events/fork; CEDIA lineage projection | Retain fork/projection; adapt native OMP collab/subagent operations only where advertised. No independent Synara agent manager. |
| Full IDE and Agent Chat | Code-OSS + `agent-ide-webview.ts`/`ide-bootstrap.ts`, shared bundle | Retain editor/LSP/debug/extensions; adapt explicit task handoff, snapshot attachment and conflict UI. |
| Review/Git/turn diffs/rewind | Vendor diff/Git UI; host `git.ts`; desktop review modules | Retain live working-tree diffs and verified Git paths; adapt missing actions. Per-turn file snapshots and file restoration remain separate from OMP conversation rewind. PR hosting integration is pending a real authorized source. |
| Terminal | Vendor terminal components; desktop `agent-window-terminal.ts`; OMP/host terminal registry; mobile VirtualTerminal | Retain measured separate shell roles. Adapt remote interactive transport/checkpoints and a separate host-owned user shell in the task cwd; AI terminals are separately selectable. Arbitrary IDE terminal attachment is not claimed. |
| Browser | Vendor BrowserPanel; desktop `agent-window-browser.ts` and native-browser bridge | Retain user-operated browser tabs/navigation. Agent browser tools are pending an OMP-accessible handle/bridge; a visible browser does not prove automation. |
| Simulator / Device pane | Vendor simulator services already used by `agent-window-device.ts` | Retain bounded compatible service reuse behind the CEDIA bridge. Gate on actual Xcode/helper availability; this is separate from paired remote phones. |
| Artifacts / previews | Host `artifacts.ts`, desktop preview and mobile artifacts core | Retain host-owned metadata and previews; preserve task/path/type identity and unavailable-file errors. |
| Automations / schedules | Vendor automation routes/contracts; adapter mutation APIs currently unsupported | Pending. Reuse UI; eventual scheduler dispatches through the same CEDIA→OMP owner. No alternate harness or always-running hidden service; dependency/permission policy must be verified before enabling. |
| Local servers / project scripts | Vendor Environment/server/script UI; adapter empty/unsupported methods | Pending host process ownership, discovery and stop semantics. A discovered URL is not authorization to stop an unrelated process. |
| Usage / statistics | Vendor usage components; generic usage/stats APIs unsupported | Pending OMP/provider-backed facts. Display unknown rather than zero; do not import Synara account controls; native OMP provider account/usage operations belong to O03. |
| Voice / speech-to-text | Deferred by the owner during the scrutinize review, 2026-09-23 | Outside the current implementation and acceptance scope in every language and client. Keep recording/transcription controls inactive and hidden. Revisit later; earlier local-processing choices remain historical direction, not an R1–R8 dependency. |
| Notifications | Vendor completion toast logic; CEDIA notification/lifecycle boundary | Retain in-app activity where backed. OS/background delivery is pending; current desktop bridge reports unsupported and native iPhone delivery is unverified. Require category/preferences and generic-content gates. |
| Kanban / PR lists / Studio / Spaces / note/recap workflows | Vendor routes/contracts; no complete current CEDIA backing | Pending applicability-to-owner mapping at upstream intake; task-oriented workflows stay on capability status when applicable. No second project/task store. Features requiring a new external account/service return for a concrete dependency decision. |
| Settings / onboarding / shortcuts / updates | Vendor settings components; CEDIA settings service, Code-OSS and OMP owners | Adapt §6.4; retain keybinding bridge. CEDIA release/update identity only. Do not reuse Synara's provider installers, update endpoint or telemetry by default. |
| AppSnap / Safari cookie vault / other harnesses / cloud engines | Generic upstream integrations | Excluded from this product direction. Preserve legally required attribution and inert original migration data; exclude normal routes/search/reset/background effects. |

This inventory replaces the assumption that an absent OMP RPC automatically makes every
application feature out of scope. App services such as project views can be CEDIA-owned;
agent execution and transcript must still remain OMP-owned. New external dependencies are
explicit gates, not hidden implementation detail.

### 2.4 Turn acceptance, OMP queue and pending model contract

R3 extends the versioned OMP bridge and the host projection; it does not introduce another
agent loop or a host-side queue drainer. The following identifiers and operations are new
protocol targets, not existing API claims.

- The host serializes mutations per task and assigns each submitted turn intent a stable
  `turnIntentId` linked to the existing `commandId`, payload hash, device and incarnation.
  Persist the intent before dispatch. OMP accepts that identity with prompt/follow-up and
  echoes it in queue, turn-start and turn-end events. CEDIA projects those events; OMP alone
  decides when an accepted queued turn executes. Steer remains input to the current turn,
  not a separate queued turn. Queries and streaming must not wait for a whole turn to finish.
  A model/tool `turn_end` is not necessarily a submitted intent's completion: one OMP
  agent run can process multiple follow-ups. Explicit submission boundaries identify
  the completed and newly running intent batches. In `one-at-a-time` mode each batch
  contains one follow-up; in `all` mode the consumed batch runs and settles together.
  CEDIA must not invent one model request per queued message or finish a tool round early.
  Transcript presentation follows OMP's message order, not enqueue timestamps: a queued
  user's original timestamp can precede the preceding submission's assistant response.
  Preserve those timestamps while carrying authoritative order through the UI projection.
- Transport/RPC acknowledgment means accepted, not turn completed. Store the command receipt
  separately from the turn projection: `prepared`, `queued`, `running`, `completed`, `failed`,
  `cancelled`, `needs_continue` or `outcome_unknown`, with an OMP event cursor and actual model.
  A `follow_up` ACK cannot produce a completed-turn badge. Host acceptance order and OMP
  queue order are recorded and checked; no renderer-local queue becomes authoritative.
- OMP's versioned queue snapshot exposes intent identities/order and whether each has started.
  The bridge persists pending-intent metadata through OMP's own session facilities, without
  making CEDIA's journal a second transcript. Pending work restored after actual process
  interruption is paused (`needs_continue`), never drained at startup. Explicit Continue
  reconciles identities before releasing verified pending work. A missing identity/outcome
  stays unknown; never infer non-execution from a missing host ACK or resend automatically.
- The latest OMP-acknowledged pending model/effort revision, committed in the same serialized
  critical section as OMP dequeue/start, governs that next turn. Host receipt alone is not
  model acceptance: the UI shows awaiting acknowledgment until OMP records the revision.
  A change still in transit when a turn starts applies to a later turn and must not be
  presented as that turn's active model. OMP validates and applies it at this boundary,
  emits the resulting revision/model, and leaves the current turn unchanged. If invalid,
  pause pending turns and show the error until the owner corrects or cancels the selection.
  A UI `agent_end` callback followed by `set_model` is insufficient because OMP may already
  have started its follow-up. Keep the deferred-model action unavailable until this bridge
  boundary is verified; expose that as a failed required desktop gate, not a substitute model.
- Stop interrupts the active turn and pauses unstarted queued work; it does not discard its
  text or immediately launch the next queued turn. Resume/Continue is explicit after Stop,
  while normal completion may advance OMP's queue. Cancelling a queued item is an identified
  OMP operation; report a race with an already-started turn instead of pretending cancellation.
- Reconnect to a healthy host reads its command/OMP snapshot without creating another owner.
  After host loss, CEDIA uses the existing lock and lifecycle rules in §2.7. If no authoritative
  queue/start evidence can be recovered, preserve the submitted content and show unknown;
  any deliberately new submission gets a new identity and requires explicit user action.

R3 tests the complete adapter→host→OMP bridge path with isolated fixtures: two clients queue
messages, pending-model changes before/after turn start including delayed host→OMP delivery,
duplicate intent, invalid model,
Stop/cancel races, and crashes before dispatch, after ACK and before/after turn-end evidence.

### 2.5 Shared Mac draft revision and Send contract

Use a host-owned `drafts` record keyed by Mac device identity and stable draft/task identity;
main-process and IDE bridges forward to this owner. New-task drafts have stable IDs before
session creation; promotion to a task preserves the draft identity. Add proposed host
`drafts.ts`, store migrations, protocol types and owner-only local draft routes. Remote
clients continue keeping their own unsent drafts and cannot read the Mac draft store.

1. Read returns `revision`, content and attachment references. A patch must include
   `expectedRevision`; a database transaction updates only that revision and broadcasts the
   new one to both Mac windows. On conflict, preserve the losing text locally and offer
   compare/reapply or use-latest. Do not auto-merge or silently overwrite either version.
2. Send names draft ID/revision and the normal command ID. In one host transaction, verify
   revision/payload hash, reserve an immutable submission snapshot and its command intent,
   and uniquely claim `(device, draftId, revision)`. Two sends of that same revision resolve
   to the same command; different content for that identity is rejected. Dispatch happens
   after the transaction and follows §2.4's unknown-outcome rules, not a cross-process atomicity
   claim. Keep submission receipts long enough to reconcile reconnect/restart.
3. Clear the submitted draft only after authoritative acceptance and only if its revision
   still matches; a concurrent newer edit survives. Failure keeps the snapshot accessible;
   uncertain delivery shows pending reconciliation, not an invitation to create another Send.
4. Import existing app-side draft files and legacy `cedia.drafts` extension state once.
   Preserve both conflicting originals as recoverable local drafts with source labels; do
   not guess which is newer from unrelated counters. After validated migration, retire their
   writes and renderer-localStorage authority. Attachment blobs remain bounded referenced
   assets; never copy credentials or turn unsent drafts into OMP history.

R2 implements storage/migration and bridge allowlists; R3 implements the Send reservation;
R4 verifies two real independent renderers, delayed stale writes, send-versus-edit, repeated
Send and app restart. Existing single-renderer write-chain tests are necessary but insufficient.

### 2.6 Archive, cleanup and restoration contract

Archive initially changes visibility while retaining the workspace. Automatic cleanup is
separately capability-gated and may only enable after this complete protocol passes. R3 adds
persistent workspace metadata: task/project/repository identity, worktree root and actual cwd,
source commit, task branch, integration target/ref and observed commit, restoration ref/SHA,
cleanup generation/state and last failure. New-task creation accepts a validated base ref,
resolves it to a commit before worktree creation and records that commit; a branch label is
not proof the requested base was used. Selected dirty copying remains explicit and conflict-aware.

- Start `archive_requested` under the task/workspace mutation guard. Refuse active turns;
  block new task writes/terminal creation/handoffs during the cleanup attempt. Revalidate
  dirty tracked/untracked/ignored data, active user/AI terminals and open/unsaved IDE work.
  An unavailable IDE check, external change or any uncertain condition means `retained`.
- Define automatic integration proof as the retained task commit being an ancestor of the
  recorded integration target commit, verified through Git. Clean working-tree status alone
  is not integration. Squash/cherry-pick equivalence is not guessed; retain until explicit
  later integration proof or a separately authorized cleanup operation exists.
- Before removal, create and verify an immutable `refs/cedia/archive/<task>/<generation>`
  pointing at the actual clean task HEAD, preserve the task branch, and durably store the
  restore receipt with state `prepared`. This ref protects its objects from ordinary Git
  garbage collection. Persist evidence before calling `git worktree remove` without force;
  never `rm -rf`, drop the branch, or clean unknown files to make the operation pass.
- Recheck guards immediately before removal. Successful removal advances to `removed`;
  refusal leaves `retained` with a reason. On crash, inspect the recorded ref and actual
  worktree registration/path. A missing worktree with a valid receipt may finish the recorded
  transition; an existing worktree is revalidated, never deleted solely because state says
  `prepared`. An orphan ref is harmless and retained for reconciliation. External mutations
  cannot be universally locked, so any detected race aborts cleanup.
- Continue validates the receipt and restores that SHA into a managed worktree (reattach the
  preserved branch only if it still points at that SHA and is free; otherwise use a new
  restoration branch without moving the existing branch). Preserve OMP session identity and
  record the new workspace association before allowing commands. If the OMP cwd/session path
  cannot be resumed safely, keep continuation blocked rather than silently fork. Missing refs,
  moved repositories or occupied paths produce actionable recovery without overwriting them.

Test initial archive retention; integration ancestry/uncertain squash; dirty and ignored
files; IDE/PTY vetoes; failures before/after ref creation, receipt persistence, removal and
completion; repeated cleanup; changed primary/task branches; and exact-revision restoration.

### 2.7 Application, host and runtime lifetime contract

Electron main is the sole CEDIA application lifecycle coordinator. Add proposed
`apps/macos/src/app-lifecycle.ts` using the existing gateway launcher and Code-OSS lifecycle;
add an owner-only host shutdown/adoption handshake in host CLI/server/service. This is R1's
lifecycle sub-slice, required before remote is available. Do not add a second login daemon.

| Event | Required transition |
|---|---|
| Login | Register the same app as a macOS login item in background mode. Electron main starts/adopts one compatible host; show status/Open/Quit without opening work windows or executing saved work. |
| Close either/both windows | Close views only. Main, host, OMP owners and host user shells retain their identities. Native panel/IDE-only resources may dispose without terminating task owners. |
| Deliberate Quit | Enter `quitting` and reject new starts/mutations, including remote ones. If work/PTYs are active, offer Stop-and-quit or Cancel; handle IDE dirty buffers as well. Cancel reopens admission. On confirmation, pause queued work, stop/await OMP and user-shell exit, persist status, close gateway, receive host shutdown ACK, then allow app exit. Failure remains visible and does not report successful Quit. |
| App crash, host survives | A host with useful active work or remote access may survive as crash recovery. On restart main adopts it only after owner-token, app/host protocol, state directory and live process identity checks. Renew an app lease/generation rather than treating a stale PID or any healthy listener as ownership proof. |
| Host crash, OMP child survives | Do not spawn a competing executor. Without a proven attach transport the session is `recovery_required`/externally running and control is blocked until the prior owner exits and the exclusive lock can be acquired. Do not kill unrelated OMP processes or claim a stdio child can be reattached. |
| Execution stopped / reboot | Restore metadata/transcript and mark interrupted/needs-continue; explicit Continue only. No automatic queued turn or terminal input replay. |
| View/network disconnect | Reconnect to the still-healthy host/session and reconcile command IDs/events; this is not execution interruption. |
| Update | Apply the same admission, dirty-buffer, stop/exit and host compatibility checks as Quit before switching the app/state pair. A busy/refused update retains the working build. |

An app-managed host is distinct from a deliberately manual `cedia-host serve`: incompatible
or independently managed instances are reported for explicit resolution, never silently
adopted or stopped. Adoption preserves one host/OMP owner and cannot bypass versioned state
migration. R1 tests the handshake and failure states; packaged close/Quit/crash/login checks
must pass before the desktop checkpoint, and repeat with paired remote clients before web
or native availability. Pure `shouldStopHost` tests alone do not satisfy this gate.

### 2.8 Complete pinned-OMP integration contract

**Meaning of complete.** CEDIA is a desktop Electron/Code-OSS application with real Mac
windows and a full IDE, not an all-SwiftUI implementation. “Fully integrated with OMP” means
one OMP runtime owns every agent action and transcript; all applicable capabilities of the
qualified OMP revision have a working, discoverable CEDIA path, documented scope and passing
acceptance. It does not mean all controls are visible at once, that every phone is a full
IDE, or that unconfigured external services are available. D/W/N are intermediate delivery
checkpoints; **F is the full pinned-OMP integration target**. Missing bridges block F even
when an earlier checkpoint can ship. A shell escape hatch alone is not graphical integration.

**Audited baseline.** OMP v18.1.18 at `00085d4e7dfdcfbf302c122fa2682b410a0f43d1`, plus
`patches/omp/0001-cedia-rpc-bridges.patch`, SHA-256
`c7286164c78b543501e23519769cd275764e463e8645157093846cdedc508b6e`, is the source baseline.
The working runtime tree includes that patch; it is not pristine upstream. The
[coverage receipt](evidence/omp-complete-scope-2026-09-23/) inventories command, UI, tool,
settings and CLI registries and supplements them with SDK/service paths not exposed by RPC.
These snapshots are evidence, not another plan or a runtime certification. OMP upgrades
regenerate the inventory and qualify new/changed/removed capabilities before claiming F
for the new revision; old evidence cannot silently certify new behavior.

**One integration seam.** Extend the existing OMP subprocess and tracked patch where RPC
is missing. Keep `packages/protocol` as the client wire owner, `packages/omp-adapter` as the
OMP wire translation, and `apps/host` as authenticated application transport. Do not start
an SDK session, TUI automation process or Synara server alongside the existing session to
reach an unavailable operation. Reuse OMP command/service handlers; extract their UI-neutral
operation only where a TUI-only dependency prevents reuse. CEDIA presentation translates
select/input/editor/approval requests without reproducing OMP execution or authorization.

The following **O01–O12 coverage families** partition implementation responsibility; §8.2
names the files, ordering and tests. All are required for F, subject only to the explicit
platform/dependency dispositions below. “Required” includes effective discovery and honest
unavailability when a user has not configured an optional service; it never permits calling
a missing CEDIA bridge “unconfigured” to pass F.

| ID / OMP capability | Single owner and bridge | CEDIA surface and required behavior |
|---|---|---|
| O01 — execution and control | OMP prompt/steer/follow-up/aside, queue, abort/retry, modes and run-state; §2.4 serialized intents | Both composers expose Send/Steer/Stop and a searchable command menu for advanced delivery/control. Carry queue/start/settle identity, cancellation and notices through web/iPhone. Intermediate `agent_end` with `isTerminal=false` remains working; no premature completion. |
| O02 — sessions and history | OMP SessionManager/session services for list/resume/branch/tree/fork/handoff/name/pins/import/export; host references identities and workspace | Task History and command menu support full pagination, tree/branch selection, side-conversation ancestry, fork, export and deliberate import. Distinguish conversation rewind, OMP checkpoints and CEDIA file restoration. Never overwrite a live externally owned session or treat foreign-harness transcript import as execution of that harness. |
| O03 — providers, models, auth and usage | OMP ModelRegistry/AuthStorage/session model roles, thinking/auto effort, service tiers/fast mode, OAuth account selection and native usage reports | OMP settings and task model picker show actual providers, custom endpoints/models, roles, entitlement, effective model and pending change. Usage shows units/source/reset time and unknown values. Native OMP credit actions are owner-only, explicit-confirmation actions, not imported Synara account controls. No new subscription or redemption is automatic; the O03 runtime policy guard below overrides inherited auto-redemption for CEDIA-controlled sessions. |
| O04 — effective configuration | OMP settings schema/layer resolution, specialized config files and current session overrides; §6.4 versioned settings bridge | Common Settings > AI/OMP has Basic and searchable Advanced views. Every schema key has owner/scope/default/provenance/editability/reset/reload metadata; no raw writable “everything” JSON escape. Global writes use OMP; project layers without a supported writer remain source-linked/read-only until their guarded file writer is implemented. Show file conflicts and pending-restart state. |
| O05 — tools, requests and artifacts | OMP built-ins, dynamic tools, host tools, code-mode/eval namespaces and extension UI/request lifecycle | Both chats render text, structured args/results, progress, errors, rich media/artifact references and cancellations for every advertised tool. Expand details on demand. Ask/select/input/editor/permissions have one response winner and preserve cancelled/timed-out states remotely. Unknown future payloads remain readable with a version warning, never fake success. |
| O06 — skills, instructions and extensions | OMP discovery, slash/templates/custom TS commands, context/rules/system prompts, MCP manager, hooks, plugins/marketplaces and loader | Settings > AI/OMP catalogs plus composer discovery share provenance, scope, warnings and refresh state. View/edit source through the IDE; enable/disable/install/remove/update only through OMP's supported manager. MCP tools/prompts/resources/auth/transports reconnect through their OMP owner. Show executable-content/package changes and request explicit action; no generic Synara installer or duplicated registry. |
| O07 — planning and agent organization | OMP Plan/Goal/guided-goal/loop/Todo/Vibe/Prewalk, task agents/AgentHub/work pools/IRC, Advisor and async jobs | Contextual Plan/Progress/Agents views show actual native state, budgets, outcomes, ancestry and pending user decisions. Pause/resume/cancel/message and plan approval call native operations. Sidechat/fork, subagent and collab are distinct identities. Plain “make a plan” prompting does not satisfy native Plan-mode acceptance. No second goal loop/agent manager. |
| O08 — CLI continuity and collaboration | Existing OMP owner plus versioned local attach endpoint/shared ownership protocol; OMP collab semantics | Discover terminal sessions; attach to an instrumented live owner without launching a second executor, then detach without stopping it. For older/uninstrumented CLI, show external-owner state and require a verified explicit handoff before continuation. Controller permissions, select/editor responses and AgentHub observations follow the owner. Tailscale remains CEDIA remote transport; public OMP rooms are not silently enabled. |
| O09 — context and memory | OMP context breakdown, compaction/shake/drop-images, retry/fallback/TTSR, session and configured memory backends/autolearn | Context drawer shows counts, effective sources, compaction/retry state and bounded controls; Memory settings show current backend and its supported inspect/edit/clear actions. Preview destructive context/memory actions, keep unrelated config/session history intact, report backend errors. Existing shared configuration is preserved; external-backend credentials/provisioning stay explicit. |
| O10 — browser, computer and native interactions | OMP Eval/browser/computer/native bridge; CEDIA owns identified WebContents/IDE/device resources it supplies | Keep user browser and agent-controlled tabs clearly identified. Explicitly attach a task-scoped tab handle; then OMP may navigate/read/interact/capture through the scoped bridge. Expired/closed/cross-task handles fail. OS permission requests stay on Mac; remote clients can see the reason and permitted results but cannot bypass platform consent. Source-linked native integrations use the same request/result contract. |
| O11 — execution environments and jobs | OMP bash/python/eval/notebook/LSP/SSH and job/terminal lifecycle; Code-OSS owns editor language/debug facilities | Transcript/job details expose kernel/job/session identity, progress, output, cancel and reconnect. Separate OMP tools from the host user shell and IDE terminal. Discover language/server/SSH/helper readiness without silently installing or connecting; expose supported configure/start/stop actions via the owning service. Notebook/media outputs use bounded artifact previews. |
| O12 — diagnostics, storage and maintenance | OMP config/import/plugin/usage/database/diagnostic CLI services; CEDIA owns packaged runtime updates | Advanced > Diagnostics offers redacted status, validation and export; storage/import/rebuild actions identify affected owner/data and preserve originals on failure. Native OMP CLI-only administration gets a typed owner-only bridge or a deliberate source/config editor workflow, not command-string interpolation. CEDIA validates and installs its packaged OMP revision as one release; no independent self-update can replace a running pinned runtime. |

**Native commands that cross application boundaries.** `/wt`, `/move`, directory grants,
Git commit and session deletion route through the same CEDIA workspace/dirty-buffer guards
as their buttons, then call the appropriate OMP service; a slash command cannot bypass task
identity, explicit commit/push intent or restoration rules. Native security scans/findings,
GitHub tools, local search, image generation and TTS output retain their OMP paths and use
O05 result/artifact presentation. TTS output does not enable deferred microphone/STT/live
voice input. Transcript sharing, remote collab rooms and external account/back-end setup
show destination and scope before an explicit owner action; no background public upload or
relay activation follows from merely opening a catalog. CLI benchmark/gallery/rendering and
ACP frontends are developer tools, not additional agent modes in the primary UI.

**Surface and policy dispositions.** AI window and compact IDE chat use the same command
catalog, state projection and contextual details. Advanced options open the shared Settings
window instead of crowding the composer. Web/native clients must display tool results,
artifacts, native requests, plans/goals/agents/jobs, context state and permitted task controls
for O01–O11. They also support bounded text/image/file-context attachments at F. Persistent
config, auth, executable package installation, account/credit changes, workspace destruction,
platform consent and maintenance remain Mac-owner operations with explicit “Manage on Mac”
state; this permission boundary is not a missing feature. Secret values never enter catalog
metadata, logs, search indexes, exported diagnostics or client state.

A per-operation disposition is one of: **integrated**, **dependency unavailable**,
**platform presentation equivalent**, **owner-only**, or **explicitly excluded**. Until
implemented it is **integration missing**, which blocks F. Dependency-unavailable requires
an implemented path, a tested readiness probe, an actionable reason and an applicable
unavailable-state test; mock evidence alone does not certify actual enabled operation.
TUI rendering, terminal keybindings, gallery/developer fixtures and protocol hosting modes
(RPC/ACP/print) have CEDIA equivalents or CLI-only developer roles rather than duplicate
application modes. OMP model providers named Codex/Claude/etc. are allowed; execution of
those alternative harnesses and Synara account/reset/update backends remains excluded.
OMP-native tool/service operations must not be excluded just because they share those names.

**Product invariants versus shared terminal configuration.** Apply a session-level CEDIA
policy layer without rewriting shared OMP files: no automatic credit redemption, no automatic
restart/replay after app recovery, no automatic packaged-runtime replacement, and the selected
sleep/lifecycle behavior. Surface the stored value, effective value and CEDIA override reason.
In particular `codexResets.autoRedeem=yes` may trigger native background redemption after
usage fetches or limit handling. The tracked OMP bridge must guard both automatic redemption
and sweep paths before subscribing or fetching usage, survive reload/reconnect and apply
before qualifying a CLI attachment as CEDIA-controlled. An unguarded live owner is inspect-only
with a truthful limitation. Only a separately confirmed owner action can redeem; usage display
alone never grants permission. Preserve terminal-only preferences when detaching rather than
silently overwriting the user's configuration. Runtime updates/plugin changes that would
remove the policy layer invalidate the negotiated capability until re-qualified.

**External and adjacent scope.** Speech-to-text is owner-deferred across all clients. New
cloud agents/Tab engines/AppSnap/Safari vault are excluded. Native APNs remains deferred
under the selected cost policy. Extra Synara Kanban/Studio/Spaces/PR hosting/scheduling and
Search/Orca/Ghostty replacements do not define OMP completeness; they keep the separate
§2.3/§6.6 intake status. Native OMP jobs, memory, computer use or slash operations are not
removed merely because a similarly named Synara screen was deferred. Platform-specific or
optional backend integration can pass its unavailable case, but an enabled claim additionally
needs an actual compatible environment; record untested backend/platform combinations in
the release qualification manifest rather than claiming universal runtime verification.

**Capability protocol, proposed additions.** Extend the R1 response with per-operation
metadata and retain typed operation schemas at the owning bridge. A descriptor identifies
`id`, O01–O12 `family`, source revision, bridge version, permitted surfaces/principals,
argument/result schema version, availability/reason, configuration scope, reload timing and
verification receipt. Typed operation handlers are explicitly registered; discovery metadata
never grants permission, and there is no arbitrary `eval`, shell or SDK method route.
Subscribe to OMP command/tool/model/config changes and replace the catalog revision atomically;
reject stale capability revisions before mutation. OMP's dynamic extension/MCP tools and
provider models are enumerated at runtime, so the audited static list is not a hard-coded
allowlist of third-party names. The adapter reports unknown or unsupported wire versions and
preserves inspectable output; it does not assume an empty catalog means “everything loaded”.

## 3. Workspace surface contract (two windows: §3.A and §3.B are the contract)

> **Baseline 2026-09-22, amended by the 2026-09-23 owner decisions.** CEDIA owns its spec. **§3.A (IDE window)** and **§3.B (agent
> window)** below are the contract; every other block in §3 (the retired Cursor capture,
> §3.1–§3.3 measurements, §3.4–§3.5) is **input data** — values and history CEDIA keeps,
> never an authority to re-measure against. Synara is the agent window's *composition*
> reference for the retained build (§3.H); §2.1 defines the selected direction for broader
> application integration. The palette is CEDIA's in both windows. Current rules:
>
> 1. IDE authority = a CEDIA-owned spec (Cursor measurements demoted to input data).
> 2. Agent scope = render everything OMP/host backs with real data (§3.B scope rule).
> 3. Theme = one shared application theme, editable from the common Settings center in
>    either window; code syntax/font can differ. The retained `workbench.colorTheme`
>    mapping is implementation history, not a requirement to keep Appearance read-only.
> 4. Window chrome = one main-process policy module for both windows.
> 5. Status bar = present in **both** windows.
> 6. Terminal = distinct current owners: Electron-main shell in the Agents window,
>    workbench shell in the IDE, host registry for OMP terminals exposed to iOS (§10 item 64).
>    The expanded remote-terminal target is not evidence of convergence already implemented.
> 7. Git = the host git service is the single implementation behind every Cedia git
>    surface; the stock SCM view stays as an upstream feature (§5).
> 8. One owner per model selection, project creation, git and keybindings; terminal roles
>    remain explicit under §2.2, without duplicating a live PTY for another view.
> 9. The IDE dock is the compact form of the agent bundle — one surface spec, responsive
>    rules only (§3.A dock row).

### 3.A IDE window contract

| Region | Element | Contract (v1) | Backed by |
|---|---|---|---|
| Window | identity | normal Code-OSS workbench; default CEDIA chrome/editor anchors are `#141414`/`#181818` dark and `#F3F3F3`/`#FCFCFC` light; independent syntax themes may alter editor rendering without changing app chrome | `apps/macos/src/cedia-theme.ts` + `cedia-theme.test.ts` (§11 gate 1) |
| Window | menu set | full workbench menus | Code-OSS |
| Window | status bar | present; carries `cedia.showAgents` plus host/model/session status entries | extension status bar |
| Window | chrome policy | zoom, traffic lights, title bar and menu policy come from one main-process module shared with the agent window | §10 item 55 |
| Layout | workbench parts | Explorer / editor / panel / debug as Code-OSS; both splitters carry accessible names | patches `0036`/`0037` |
| Dock | `cediaComposerDock` | **compact form of §3.B's surface**: same bundle (`agent-ui/ide.html`), routes, components and tokens; responsive rules only (thread sidebar hidden via `isIdeEmbeddedRuntime`, header compact <700px, tour insets collapse). No second chat UI; the webview task shell must be gone (§10 items 10, 63) | `agent-ide-webview.ts` + `ide-bootstrap.ts` |
| Dock/inline | selection actions, inline edit, focus/prefill handoffs | reach the host through the guarded `sendCommand` path; a refusal is shown as a warning, never silent | §10 item 63 |
| Models | selection state of record | one state: `GET /v1/models` → session `set_model` (+ `set_thinking_level`); the control drawn anywhere is the bundle's picker | §10 item 56 (retires the workbench LM-picker path) |
| Projects | creation flow | one flow: host `POST /v1/projects` (+ `/v1/workspace-suggestion`); IDE entry points register through it when a Cedia surface needs the project | §10 item 59 |
| Terminal | integrated terminal | the user's private dev shell: upstream workbench behaviour, xterm.js, not an agent surface | §5 (terminal-roles row) |
| Git | SCM view | stock upstream feature, kept; not a Cedia surface | §5 |
| Keybindings | system | one system: `Cedia/User/keybindings.json`; context-scoped CEDIA bindings with common editing/conflict reporting; `backlog/command-map.md` supplies baseline evidence and current context vocabulary, not a second policy | §10 items 57/69 |
| Theme | authority | shared CEDIA application theme; code syntax/font separately selectable; editable through the common Settings center | §2.1; §10 items 54/69; §11 gate 1 |

### 3.B Agent window contract

Retained renderer: the standalone bundle (`vs/cedia/agent/index.html`) in the base's sessions-window
identity (patch `0056`); composition = Synara (MIT, vendored, **presentation only** — data
always flows through the adapter): layout, elements, geometry, surface typography and motion
come from the port (§3.H owner decision, 2026-09-19). **Palette = Cedia's own**: every colour,
including type colour, comes from the shared application-theme authority in §3.A
(execution = §10 item 54); editor syntax themes do not replace application chrome. Cedia-authored chrome in this window (status bar,
handoff controls) uses CEDIA's type and motion scales (§3.3–§3.4).

**Scope rule (owner, 2026-09-22: "build everything OMP has and supports").** A surface renders
**iff** OMP or the host backs it with real data. Planned applicable capabilities that are
not ready are hidden from normal work menus/settings and listed on a separate capability-status
page. Other harnesses and out-of-scope features are excluded rather than promised there.
An implemented action temporarily unavailable in the current state may show a specific reason.
`list → []` fakes and `unsupported()` behind a live-looking control are defects (§11 gate 4).

| Region | Element | Contract (v1) | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| Sidebar | Search (⌘K / ⇧⌘P), New thread, Projects + Add project | rows over host projects/sessions | backed — keep |
| Sidebar | Automations | absent from normal navigation until supported; track applicable planned capability on the capability-status page | §10 item 69 |
| Sidebar | Kanban, Pull requests, Plugins, Studio, Spaces | absent from the retained working UI; classify applicability and backing before enabling or listing as planned; current absence alone does not remove applicable Synara workflows from the selected target | §10 items 60/69 |
| Header | IDE, Environment, chat actions | handoff `openIde {cwd, path, line}`; Environment rows only where backed | backed |
| Environment panel | top-right card opened from the title-bar `Environment` control | rows only where backed (Changes · Local · branch · Repository · Open in IDE); a row with no source stays absent (§9 rule) | backed — keep |
| Status bar | slim bottom bar (new) | bundle-drawn: host state · model · session status · branch, painted with `statusBar.*` tokens | §10 item 55 |
| Composer | input, send/steer/queue/abort by state | host commands; §3.C state machine | voice is explicitly deferred; no microphone control in this delivery |
| Composer | attachments | images reach OMP (`images[]` on `prompt`); other references become a labelled attached-context block — the payload the native path already proved (§10 item 34) | §10 item 61 |
| Composer | mentions | `/` = real skill/slash list from `get_available_commands`; `@` = file/workspace refs → attached context | §10 items 61/62 |
| Composer | model + effort | one picker component: host catalogue, provider tabs, stars, effort ladder → `set_model` / `set_thinking_level` | backed — keep |
| Transcript | messages, tool cards, approvals/questions, presentations, edit + rewind (tail; any-message = §10 1b) | host journal projection + `uiRequests` | backed — keep |
| Right dock | Changes · Browser · Terminal · Files · Git · Device · Sidechat | each pane on its host bridge; Git on the host git service incl. the currently-throwing actions (item 58); Sidechat = OMP fork; Changes shows the working-tree diff today, turn diffs pending §10 1c/35 | backed (Git = item 58; turn diffs = §10 1c/35) |
| Settings | common center with General, AI/OMP, and IDE categories | frequent supported controls first, Advanced/search for the rest, explicit scope; Appearance editable from either window | target; retained controls inventoried under §10 item 69; theme per item 54 |
| Settings | Skills, MCP connections | render only when their capability is real (§10 items 62/43); until then track on the capability-status page | pending |
| Settings | imported AppSnap/Safari/Profile/Usage/Worktrees controls | no generic upstream settings without a supported owner; applicable usage/worktree functions follow §2.3 and §6.4, while unrelated services stay excluded | §10 items 60/69 |
| Onboarding | welcome → tour → project → done (OMP-only card) + the gateway card (external MCP pairing, kept per §9) | as landed 2026-09-20; the gateway card's MCP capability tracks §10 item 43 | backed; gateway = pending (item 43) |
| UI state | task-completion toasts · split view · shared Mac drafts | view layout/toasts are local; draft revisions and Send reservations belong to the host (§2.5), shared by both windows | existing projection retained; cross-window concurrency requires R2/R3/R4 |
| Feedback / What's New | — | the feedback endpoint is cut (no third-party POST); release history = a Cedia changelog or cut | §10 item 60 |
| Theme | appearance | edit the same CEDIA application theme as the IDE through shared Settings; no independent per-window theme owner | §10 items 54/69 |
| Shortcuts | sheet + editing | reads and writes the real `keybindings.json` through the extension bridge; no second chord vocabulary | §10 item 57 |

Acceptance for both windows: §11 gates 1–4, with geometry/colour measured from the real DOM or
compositor (gate 2) and the scope rule above as the honesty test (gate 4).

### 3.C Task and workspace behavior (owner decisions consolidated 2026-09-23)

These rules consolidate the accepted interview answers. They are target behavior; §9's
historical receipts do not prove them. A task is the CEDIA association to one OMP session,
its workspace and results. A turn is one run inside that session. A model provider supplies
models to OMP; it never chooses another harness. A remote client controls the Mac's task.

| Trigger | Required behavior and failure handling |
|---|---|
| New Git task | Default to the project's configured primary branch and record its selected commit; permit another branch before Send. Concurrent tasks receive separate worktrees. Do not implicitly fetch/pull. Optional selected uncommitted-file copying preserves the source and reports copy conflicts. |
| New non-Git task | Work in the original folder with a visible shared-folder label. Admit only one CEDIA file-mutating task per folder; do not initialize Git. This guard cannot lock out external tools. |
| Select a task in the AI window | Change that window only. IDE task/cwd stays pinned until explicit Open in IDE or Review. Show task and workspace identity in each surface so disagreement is understandable. |
| Open in IDE / Review | Target the chosen task's actual worktree. Before displacing unsaved work offer Save / Discard / Cancel; Cancel retains the IDE target. Review opens that task's diff. Completion presents a summary and Review action without stealing focus. |
| Send / Plan / Steer / Stop | Act is the default; explicit Plan and natural-language restrictions apply. Send while running queues a following turn under §2.4. Steer is a separate action for the active turn; Stop interrupts it and pauses unstarted queued work until explicit Continue. No second loop and no automatic commit/merge/push. |
| Change model/effort while running | Show actual running selection and a distinct pending selection. Apply/validate the pending choice before the next turn; a rejected choice remains actionable and does not silently fall back to another harness. |
| IDE Send to AI | Explicit target task plus visible attachment preview. Capture file/selection identity and immutable text snapshot; unsaved content is labelled and not implicitly saved. Tools still read disk unless given that snapshot. |
| AI modifies a file with an unsaved IDE buffer | Preserve both versions, show comparison, and require explicit selection/manual integration. Never silently merge, overwrite the buffer, or save it. Default editor saving is manual; Auto Save remains an opt-in IDE setting. |
| Continue a terminal-created session | Preserve OMP session identity and original cwd. Acquire/attach to its sole owner through §2.2's gate. Mark non-isolated/shared workspaces and apply CEDIA's concurrent-write guard. |
| Commit / merge / push | Execute only after the corresponding explicit user action or instruction for that task. A successful turn, passing checks, or Archive is not authorization. |
| Archive | Refuse while running; offer explicit Stop or wait. Keep history and task result. Automatically remove a worktree only after integration is established, no tracked/untracked/ignored user data remains, no terminal/execution uses it and no unsaved/open IDE work is displaced. When any condition is uncertain, retain it and explain why. |
| Continue after eligible cleanup | Keep the branch and verified immutable restoration ref (§2.6). Restore that revision and the original conversation, never current primary-branch HEAD. Missing restoration data is reported. Restoring files does not restore terminated processes or unrecorded buffers. |
| Interrupted work after crash/reboot | Reconcile authoritative process/session state and restore saved history/status. If execution stopped, show Interrupted and wait for explicit Continue; never automatically replay a turn, queued mutation or terminal input. A disconnected view reconnects to its healthy host. Host failure with a surviving OMP child follows §2.7: attach only through a proven path, otherwise block until safe ownership handoff; never spawn another owner. |
| Login to macOS | Start CEDIA in the background automatically (53A), with visible status/Open/Quit controls and selected remote connectivity. This occurs after user login, not before disk unlock. Do not automatically replay previous tasks or open windows merely because login started the application. |
| Close desktop windows | Keep the application and active tasks available in background, with visible status/Stop/Open controls. |
| Explicit Quit | If active, offer stop-and-quit or cancel; wait for the actual stop/exit result. If idle, quit subject to IDE unsaved-document handling. Do not leave an independent execution daemon behind. |
| Unsent draft ownership | For the same task, the AI and IDE windows on one Mac share one draft (57A). Other devices keep separate drafts. Revision-aware edits and a single successful Send prevent stale-window overwrites or double submission; sending elsewhere does not erase another device's draft. Drafts are not OMP transcript entries until sent. |
| Compose a new message while Mac is unreachable | Preserve an editable draft on the client (54A). After reconnect, refresh the task context and require explicit Send; there is no automatic offline outbox. Reconcile previously sent commands separately by their existing IDs. |
| Mac sleep/offline | Respect system sleep settings. Report the Mac unavailable; do not promise wake-on-demand or continued work during sleep. Reconnect to actual state when reachable. |
| Open remote Terminal | Open or reattach to the task's user shell on the Mac, identified separately from AI tool terminals and using the task cwd. Viewing/selecting an AI terminal is explicit; opening the panel does not send input into an AI tool process. Closing the panel detaches the view, while explicit terminal Close follows its active-process confirmation. |
| Concurrent desktop/mobile control | Host accepts commands in one order and resolves each approval once. A losing/stale response refreshes to the settled result. Disconnect preserves drafts and shows an uncertain command outcome without automatic resubmission. |

### 3.D Screen and navigation contract

Keep Synara's composition and component language, CEDIA identity and one shared app theme.
Product controls use English; user chat, paths and content may use Thai or other languages.
The following arrangements specialize the accepted direction without creating another renderer.

| Surface | Primary layout | Contextual / secondary controls | Required states |
|---|---|---|---|
| AI window | Sidebar: Search, New task, Projects and task rows. Main: task header, conversation, composer. | Header Open in IDE and Environment; right dock opens one requested Changes/Files/Browser/Terminal/Git/Device/Sidechat panel. Settings and Capability status are separate destinations. | New draft with explicit project/branch; connecting; running; queued; awaiting input; stopped; error; completed with Review; archived. Each state identifies the actual task/workspace/model. |
| IDE window | Full Code-OSS Explorer/editor/LSP/debug/extensions and workbench terminal. Compact Agent Chat shows its pinned task's transcript, composer, attachments, approvals and status. | Open AI window for full management; explicit task switch and Send to AI. Avoid a second permanent global-task sidebar inside the chat. | Unsaved-buffer conflict, workspace-switch decision, failed command, disconnected host and pending model are visible without losing editor work. |
| Settings | One destination from either desktop window; General, AI/OMP and IDE categories; frequent controls first, supported Advanced controls and search. | Scope badges; category Reset preview; device enrollment in General > Remote; Capability status links outside working settings. IDE specialist controls may open the actual workbench settings editor. | Loading/failed read; saving/saved/failed write; conflicting revision; applies-next-session; unavailable capability. No success toast before owner readback. |
| Web / iPhone | Project/task list, task conversation and composer, with status and approvals. | On-demand review/diff and a separate user shell in the task cwd; AI terminals selectable separately; project/model selection and New task. Device connection status stays reachable. Full desktop IDE is not reproduced on the phone. | Pairing, reconnecting, offline, revoked, stale task, awaiting approval and command outcome unknown. No optimistic duplicate turn after reconnect. |
| Capability status | Applicable features grouped as available, integration pending, or upstream blocked, each with a concise reason. | Implementation/evidence details belong in developer documentation; normal users see what works and what is unavailable. | Other harnesses, cloud engines and unrelated services are excluded entirely. A temporary restriction of an implemented action is explained in-place. |

The target has no permanent empty panels and no unsolicited IDE retargeting. Search, deep links,
onboarding and keyboard commands must obey the same capability/branding boundary as navigation.
Notifications cover completion, failure and requests for input, with category switches; generic
lock-screen content is the default. Detailed content is opt-in. Delivery while the client is
suspended is a separate integration gate, not implied by a connected foreground client.

**Input data (not the contract):** the retired Cursor capture and tables that follow, §3.1–§3.3
measurements, and §3.4–§3.5 history.

```text
Cursor Agents (window)
├─ Title bar: close / minimize / fullscreen; no editor tab, no editor title actions
├─ Sidebar (single column)
│  ├─ Hide Sidebar · Go Back · Go Forward
│  ├─ New Chat ⌘N · Search ⌘K · Automations · Customize
│  ├─ Projects (+ New Project)
│  ├─ Repositories (+ Customize Sidebar, Open Workspace)
│  ├─ session rows: grouped by project, row = state + name + relative time
│  ├─ Getting Started card (Skip step, Connect Slack)
│  └─ Account menu · Settings
├─ Main
│  ├─ Header: IDE · Chat actions · Show Apps
│  ├─ Session transcript (when there is work)
│  └─ Composer: project popup · branch combo · environment ("This Mac") ·
│              input (placeholder "Plan, Build, / for skills, @ for context") ·
│              toolbar: Add agents/context/tools · reasoning popup · voice input · Send
├─ Recommendation rows (Dismiss where present): Plan New Idea ⇧Tab · Multitask ·
│  Run in Cloud · Build from a design · Deploy my prototype · Start with a plan · Debug an issue
└─ Notifications (alt+T)
```

| Region | Element | Behaviour to match |
|---|---|---|
| Window | menu bar | the Agents window's menu set: `Cedia · File · Edit · View · Window · Help` (no Selection/Go/Run/Terminal) |
| Sidebar | New Chat / Search | shortcut hints that actually resolve; Search opens search, it is not a permanent field |
| Sidebar | Automations / Customize | entry points to real product capability |
| Sidebar | Projects + New Project | separate from Repositories |
| Sidebar | Repositories + Customize Sidebar / Open Workspace | manage repo groups and open a workspace |
| Sidebar | session rows | state + name + relative time, grouped by project |
| Sidebar | recommendation card | dismissible/skippable, never permanently stuck |
| Sidebar | Account + Settings | entry points to account and settings |
| Header | IDE | switches to the editor window (a different window) |
| Header | Chat actions / Show Apps | task context menu and apps entry |
| Composer | project / branch / environment | name the real workspace per task before sending |
| Composer | input + IME | same placeholder; Enter sends, Shift+Enter newline |
| Composer | toolbar | agents/context/tools, reasoning, voice input, send/stop by state |
| Recommendation rows | idea rows + Dismiss | create a draft from the text; dismiss per row |
| Panels | Changes · Files · Browser · Terminal · preview/artifacts | per task, carrying workspace identity |

### 3.1 Measured values (light theme, empty draft, 1710×1073, default zoom)

| Element | Measured | Note |
|---|---|---|
| Sidebar width | 255px | there is a "Resize sidebar" splitter, so it is adjustable, not constant |
| Sidebar row inset | 8px left/right | the selected row's fill spans x8..246 |
| Sidebar row box | 30px | token `--ui-sidebar-menu-button-min-height` = 28px → **token and rendered box disagree**; trust the rendered value |
| Sidebar row pitch | ~30.7px | measured from text-row spacing |
| Sidebar row fill (selected) | `rgb(223,224,225)` on chrome `rgb(236,237,238)` | — |
| Composer card (empty draft) | **608 × 106px** including a 1px border, centred in the main pane | x679..1286, y506..611 · 423px margin each side |
| Composer card border | 1px `rgb(234,234,234)` | all four sides |
| Composer card fill | `#FCFCFC` = `editor.background` = **lighter than the page behind it** | token: `--prompt-input-container-bg` = `--cursor-bg-input-surface` = `color-mix(in srgb, var(--cursor-base) 6%, transparent)` |
| Composer shadow | none | token `--prompt-input-container-shadow: none`; never add elevation |
| Composer radius / editor | radius 18 (`radius-4xl`) · editor min-height 36 (`spacing-9`) | matches `--prompt-input-border-radius-expanded` / `--prompt-input-editor-min-height` |
| Idea rows (empty state) | 4 rows below the card, occupying y624..793 (~42px/row) | separator inset 12px from the card edge; mode pills sit below the card |
| Type scale the agent CSS actually uses | 11/12/13/14 (tokens) **plus 16/17/18/20px classes** | `.ui-osj86m{font-size:18px}` → an 18px heading is not outside Cursor's own scale |
| Motion | instant/fast/normal/slow = 50/100/150/200ms · `--cursor-easing-out-cubic` = `cubic-bezier(0.215, 0.61, 0.355, 1)` | values declared in the bundle (real animation timing not yet observed) |

**Colour measurement rule (read this before chasing ghosts).** Cursor's chrome and chat
surfaces are **translucent (glass)**, not opaque. `screencapture -l <windowID>` composites them
against a transparent backdrop and reads roughly 7 units darker (chrome reads
`rgb(236,237,238)`) while a full-screen capture of the same display reads `rgb(250,250,251)`.
In one full-screen capture containing both apps, Cursor's sidebar and Cedia's read identically.
The ~7-unit delta is a **capture artefact, not a product difference**. To close §11 gate 2, use a
full-screen capture containing both apps, or treat the theme file as the base and verify against
a full-screen capture. Never compare colours with `screencapture -l` alone.

### 3.2 AX correction pass (2026-09-14)

§3's tree and table above were summarised from an earlier pass. This pass pulled the
**accessibility tree of the running `Cursor Agents` window** (Computer Use → `@oai/sky`; raw dump
at [`evidence/cursor-agents-ax-2026-09-14/cursor-agents-ax-tree.txt`](evidence/cursor-agents-ax-2026-09-14/cursor-agents-ax-tree.txt))
and found the following corrections. Where they disagree with §3, **these win**:

- **No in-window menu bar**: `Cursor | File | Edit | View | Window | Help` is the macOS menu bar
  (an AX sibling of the window), not drawn in the window, and there is **no title bar band** —
  the traffic lights float over the sidebar itself.
- **The right side is one panel, not a list of panels**: `Panel editor-panel-group` is headed by
  `Tabs` + an `Open new tab menu` button + `Enter Full Screen` + `Hide Apps`, with
  `Changes · Browser · Terminal · File` laid out as tab-strip entries. `Show Apps` / `Hide Apps`
  toggles this panel (storage key `cursor/glass.rightPane`) and it is about 608px wide — the same
  as the composer.
- **The empty composer has no Send button**: the toolbar holds only `Add agents, context, tools` ▾,
  `High` ▾ (reasoning) and `Start voice input`; the row above is the project popup + branch combo
  (`main`) + `This Mac`.
- **Recommendation rows carry subtitles and not every row has Dismiss**: `Plan New Idea ⇧Tab` ·
  `Multitask` · `Run in Cloud` (no subtitle) · `Build from a design — Turn a frame into working UI
  in this repo` + Dismiss · `Deploy my prototype — Put it on a live link anyone can open` +
  Dismiss · `Start with a plan — Align on implementation before writing code` · `Debug an issue —
  Find root causes and fix tricky bugs`. `Run in Cloud` denotes a cloud runtime option (it appears
  in Cursor's own source as a label in the Autopilot PR menu), not a full cloud-agent entry point.
- **Real sidebar order**: Hide Sidebar · Go Back (disabled) · Go Forward (disabled) ·
  New Chat ⌘N · Search ⌘K · Automations · Customize · Projects (+ New Project) ·
  Repositories (+ Customize Sidebar ▾, Open Workspace ▾) · sortable project groups ·
  Getting Started card (Skip step n of m, Connect Slack) · Account menu · Settings ·
  `Resize sidebar` splitter. There is **no** `Sessions` label and no pet.
  **Corrected again from a real screenshot (2026-09-15)**: in an actual Agents window screenshot
  (3420×2224) the sidebar ends at `Repositories` + repo rows — **no Getting Started card and no
  Account/Settings row** — while `Go Back/Go Forward` are in the title bar, not the sidebar, and
  the `Repositories` heading has two icons (filter, add) rather than a `Customize Sidebar ▾`.
  The AX list above therefore describes another pass or another state, not this empty home. The
  user chose Cursor's rendering over the spec draft, so both blocks were removed and a test pins
  their absence.
- **Colours read from the real screen (dark)**: chrome/sidebar `rgb(35,35,37)` · main pane
  `rgb(27,27,27)` · composer card `rgb(34,34,34)` (lighter than its background) · right panel
  `rgb(26,26,26)`; the composer is 608 wide and ~107 tall, centred in the main pane at the
  1710×1073 rig (consistent with §3.1, which was measured in light).
- **Still unverified**: running/approval/error states, the dark half of the light-theme pair, and
  real animation timing.

### 3.H The agent window's UI reference is Synara, under MIT (historical decision, 2026-09-19)

§3.A is the contract for the **IDE window** (the Code-OSS workbench); §3.1-3.3 are its input
data (measured from Cursor 3.20.x, kept as values, not as a target — corrected 2026-09-22). The **agent window** is a deliberate, owner-directed
deviation: its look is the reference app Synara, whose repository
([`Emanuele-web04/synara`](https://github.com/Emanuele-web04/synara)) is **MIT** (Copyright (c) 2026
T3 Tools Inc. and Emanuele Di Pietro; no NOTICE, no extra terms), so its components can be reused
with the copyright and permission notice kept.

Historical port boundary, superseded where it conflicts with §2.1–§2.3 and §3.C–§3.D.
In particular, the renderer-only import restriction and Cursor-parity requirement below
are not current target rules. Compatible Synara services may be adapted behind the one-owner
boundary; the shared CEDIA theme is editable. The original decision was:

1. **One execution owner stays**: OMP, reached only through `apps/macos`'s extension and the host.
