   Synara's own server, provider adapters, contracts and updater are **not** taken. Its UI is a
   React renderer (`apps/web`, Vite) whose components are taken as *presentational* code and fed by a
   Cedia adapter, so no second process can own a session, an approval or a credential.
2. **Two windows with two references**: the IDE window keeps Cursor parity (§3); the agent window
   takes Synara's composition - hero, composer footer, provider-rail model picker with stars,
   environment picker, sidebar sections - and its visual language *inside that window only*, so the
   workbench tokens the rest of the product uses are untouched.
   **Owner clarification, 2026-09-19:** *what* comes from Synara is the UX, the UI, the elements,
   the layout, the model picker and every chat-box element; the **palette** stays Cedia's own - the
   same theme the IDE window uses. So the port copies Synara's composition and structure rather
   than moving its colours onto this window, and the agent window is deliberately not repainted
   with a second palette (the extension clears any it wrote earlier, `apps/macos/src/extension.ts`).
3. **Vendored, not submoduled**: the pieces we need (about 2-3k lines: `ChatComposerFooter.tsx`,
   `ProviderModelPicker.tsx`, `ComposerModelPicker*.tsx`, `ModelStarButton.tsx`,
   `ComposerEnvironmentPicker.tsx`, `ComposerEffortSliderCard.tsx`, `ChatEmptyStateHero.tsx`) are
   copied into a Cedia-owned folder with their original headers, and the MIT notice is added to this
   repository's third-party notices. A submodule would drag in their Effect-based toolchain and
   contracts, which is the second-owner risk this point exists to avoid.
4. **Elements with no data are still left out**: Kanban and Pull requests have no OMP surface, and
   the permission control waits for OMP's `permission` command shape (measured 2026-09-19: the bridge
   has `permission` and `cedia_native_permission`). Both are tracked in §10 rather than faked.

Two historical measurement sources, both recorded in
[`evidence/ui-cursor-parity-lock-2026-09-14/`](evidence/ui-cursor-parity-lock-2026-09-14/):

   `/Applications/Cursor.app/Contents/Resources/app/extensions/theme-cursor/themes/cursor-dark-color-theme.json`,
   named `Cursor Dark Anysphere v0.0.3`. Values are read from the file, not from a screenshot.
2. **CUA/AX capture of the Cursor Agents window** (1224×768 logical, Cursor 3.20.17, dark) for
   the geometry and IA the theme file does not carry.

Dark palette (historical input; CEDIA-owned tokens and §3.A govern the current target):

| `sideBar/activityBar/statusBar/titleBar/panel/editorWidget/terminal/tabsBackground` | `#141414` | `--cedia-panel` |
| `tab.activeBackground`, `dropdown.background` | `#181818` | raised panel surfaces |

**Default CEDIA palette relationship:** chrome (`#141414`) is **darker** than the editor/transcript
(`#181818`). This is what separates Cursor from the Code-OSS default (chrome `#191A1B`, lighter
than editor `#121314`). Independent IDE syntax themes remain permitted under §6.4. The default mapping lives in `apps/macos/src/cedia-theme.ts`
(`CEDIA_DARK_ANCHORS`, `CEDIA_WORKBENCH_COLORS`).

Light palette (added 2026-09-14 after a runtime check — Cursor on this machine runs in light,
while Cedia was forcing dark chrome over a light workbench, which was a real parity gap; Cursor
ships five themes: `cursor-dark`, `cursor-dark-hc`, `cursor-dark-midnight`, `cursor-light`,
`cursor-light-colorblind`):

| Key | Cursor Light value | Used in Cedia as |
|---|---|---|
| `editor.background` | `#FCFCFC` | `--cedia-bg` |
| `sideBar/activityBar/statusBar/titleBar/panel/editorWidget/terminal/tabsBackground` | `#F3F3F3` | `--cedia-panel` |
| `foreground` | `#141414` | `--cedia-text` |
| `descriptionForeground` | `#141414BD` | `--cedia-muted` |
| `panel.border`/`sideBar.border` | `#14141414` | `--cedia-border` |
| `input.border` | `#14141433` | `--cedia-control-border` |
| `focusBorder` | `#14141433` | `--cedia-focus` |
| `button.background` / hover | `#2778C1` / `#246AAB` | `--cedia-accent` |
| `textLink.foreground` | `#0064B0` | `--cedia-link` |
| `list.activeSelectionBackground` / `list.hoverBackground` | `#14141414` | `--cedia-selected-bg` / `--cedia-hover-bg` |
| `input.background` | `#FCFCFC` | `--cedia-input` |

**Light invariant:** chrome `#F3F3F3` (243) is still **darker** than editor `#FCFCFC` (252) — the
relationship is not inverted. The palette is selected from `window.activeColorTheme.kind` via
`cediaThemeKindFromVscode()` and repainted on `onDidChangeActiveColorTheme`.

**Follow the OS like the reference does:** Cursor sets `window.autoDetectColorScheme`, so its
chrome follows the machine's light/dark setting. Cedia sets it too, but only when the user has
not set it themselves (checked with `globalValue === undefined`, not truthiness, so a deliberate
`false` is never overridden). The `dark-hc`, `light-colorblind` and `midnight` palettes were
added later on 2026-09-14 — see §11 for their status.

**First screen without a folder:** the Agents window can start with no folder open, and then it
cannot write workspace settings. The palette used to be skipped entirely, leaving the first
screen (New task) in engine colours. Fixed by falling back to global scope when there is no
folder, plus `isCediaWorkbenchPalette()` to distinguish the palette Cedia wrote from values the
user set, so Cedia's own footprint is not read back as "the user chose this" and repainting stops.
Verified at runtime: the first screen gets `#F3F3F3` / `#FCFCFC`.

**Agent design tokens of the reference** (measured from the real bundle, 2026-09-14). Cursor ships
its agent-window design system inside `workbench.desktop.main.js` as 405 CSS custom properties
named `--cursor-*`, so Cedia's tokens can be compared directly instead of guessed from a
screenshot:

| Group | Reference token | Value | Cedia | Match |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| font-size | `--cursor-font-size-xs/sm/base/lg` | 11/12/13/14 | `--cedia-font-*` | ✅ |
| line-height | `--cursor-line-height-*` | 14/16/18/22 | `--cedia-lh-*` | ✅ |
| height | `--cursor-height-*` | 20/24/28/32 | `--cedia-height-*` | ✅ |
| radius | `--cursor-radius-xs/sm/base/lg/xl/2xl/3xl/4xl/full` | 2/4/6/8/12/14/16/18/9999 | `--cedia-radius-*` | ✅ |
| control radius | `--cursor-radius-base` | 6 | `--cedia-control-radius` | ✅ |
| composer surface | `--conversation-surface-border-radius` → `radius-xl` | **12** | `--cedia-composer-radius` | changed 10 → **12** |
| conversation type | `--conversation-font-size` → `font-size-lg` | 14 | `--cedia-font-lg` (transcript body) | ✅ |
| focus ring (dark) | `--cursor-stroke-focused` = `--cursor-focus` 15% | `#F0F0F026` | palette `focusBorder` | ✅ exact |
| spacing | `--cursor-spacing-1/1-5/2/2-5/3/4…` | 4,6,8,10,12,16,20,24,28,32,40,44,48 | `--cedia-space-*` | ✅ (Cedia names by px, reference by step) |
| duration | `--cursor-duration-instant/fast/normal/slow` | 50/100/150/200 | `--cedia-motion-*` | corrected to match |
| easing | `--cursor-easing-out-cubic` | `cubic-bezier(0.215, 0.61, 0.355, 1)` | `--cedia-motion-curve` | corrected to match |

**Motion correction (fixing a regression from the previous pass):** an earlier pass changed the
motion tokens to equal `DEFAULT_MOTION_TOKENS` in `ui-a11y.ts` so the two sources agreed
(instant 0, drawer 180/120, curve `cubic-bezier(.2,0,0,1)`) — but that set is **not** the
reference's. The reference values are instant 50, feedback 100, surfaceIn 150, surfaceOut 100,
drawerIn 200, drawerOut 150, curve `cubic-bezier(0.215,0.61,0.355,1)` (`--cursor-easing-out-cubic`).
Both `ui-a11y.ts` and the CSS fallbacks in `webview.ts` were corrected to the reference values.
`--cursor-easing-out-quint` (`cubic-bezier(0.16,1,0.3,1)`) is kept as `MOTION_CURVE_STRONG` for
a future emphasis transition, with no unused CSS. D15's intent still holds (mode switch commits
at 0ms, reduced-motion zeroes durations, no decorative motion); use this table for the numbers.

**Agent surface colour (checked 2026-09-14 — confirmed no change needed):** the reference sets
`--cursor-sidebar` = `--cursor-editor` = `#181818` and `--cursor-chrome` = `#141414` in dark,
which looks like the agent sidebar should be `#181818` (lighter than Cedia's). But the code that
actually paints the surface reads
`r.style.background = "var(--glass-chat-surface-background, var(--cursor-bg-chrome))"` — i.e.
**`--cursor-bg-chrome` = `#141414`**, the same as the `sideBar.background` Cedia uses.
`--cursor-sidebar` has no consumer that paints the chat surface. Cedia therefore keeps its current
value, and this question is closed.

**Inactive/unfocused state (fixed 2026-09-14):** the reference theme does not declare
`statusBar.inactiveBackground`, so its status bar keeps its colour when the window is unfocused —
but the engine Cedia pins ships its own default themes (`Light 2026` / `Dark 2026`) that **do**
declare it, so Cedia once showed the engine colour (`#FAFAFD`) instead of the palette colour in
an unfocused window. Fixed by adding the inactive/unfocused keys the engine declares and the
reference does not (`statusBar.inactiveBackground`, `statusBar.inactiveForeground`,
`activityBar.inactiveForeground`, `panelTitle.inactiveForeground`) to all five palettes. Verified
at runtime: the IDE window's light status bar reads `rgb(243,243,243)` across its full width
(previously `rgb(250,250,253)`).

**Shell scale audit (2026-09-14):** every px value in the shell's CSS was checked against the
reference's scales **by property kind** (radius against radius scale, type against type scale)
and off-scale values were fixed: `border-radius: 11px` (`.attention`) → `var(--cedia-radius-full)`
(a pill badge); `border-radius: 5px` (`.pane-chip`, `.pane-draft`) →
`var(--cedia-control-radius)`; `border-radius: 1px` (`.pane-chip` chevron) →
`var(--cedia-radius-xs)`. A test enforcing the invariant that no border-radius escapes the scale
`[0,2,4,6,8,12,14,16,18,9999]` lives in `apps/macos/test/webview.test.ts`.

**Composer geometry (corrected 2026-09-14).** Cursor names its prompt-input tokens directly, so
they compare one-to-one:

| Measured | Reference token | Value | Cedia | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| radius (expanded) | `--prompt-input-border-radius-expanded` | `radius-4xl` = **18px** | `--cedia-composer-radius` | changed 10 → 12 → **18** |
| editor min-height | `--prompt-input-editor-min-height` | `spacing-9` = **36px** | `--cedia-composer-editor-min` | was 44px → 36px |
| editor max-height | `--prompt-input-editor-max-height` | **200px** | `--cedia-composer-editor-max` | was 180px → 200px |
| editor padding | `--prompt-input-editor-padding` | `spacing-2 spacing-3` = **8px 12px** | `.composer textarea` | was 12px all round |

**18px confirmed by measurement, not just by token:** that token has two definitions (default
`radius-4xl` = 18, variant `embedded` = `radius-base` = 6), so the real corner curvature was
measured from the reference window — the composer box's left edge insets 14px at the topmost row
and reaches the inner edge about 18 rows down, which matches r≈18 (first-row inset formula
r−√(r−0.25): r=18→13.8, r=16→12.0, r=12→8.6, r=6→3.6). The composer's real size in the reference
window (1710×1073) is **604×104px**, centred in the main pane with ~424px margins.

**Still open (needs a picture to decide):** the shell's headings use `font-size: 18px;
line-height: 26px` (`.task-title`, `.empty strong`, `.settings-page h2`), but the reference's type
scale is only xs/sm/base/lg = 11/12/13/14 and `--conversation-font-size` is also 14 — there is no
18px step. Changing headings to 14px is a visible change, so it stays unfixed until an image
comparison is possible.

**Open items that must not be changed without evidence:**

- **The agent window's chrome reads ~7 units darker than its theme.** A colour-accurate capture of
  the real reference window (`screencapture -l <windowid>`) reads sidebar `rgb(236,237,238)` and
  composer surface `rgb(245,245,246)` while the transcript area reads `rgb(252,252,252)` =
  `#FCFCFC` **exactly** (no shift). The delta is therefore in the chrome, not the whole window,
  and it is **not** a colour-management shift as previously believed (that explanation was
  withdrawn). The cause is unconfirmed — the light `--cursor-*` values are set at runtime and
  cannot be read from the bundle, and fitting an overlay formula to both surfaces does not agree —
  so the palette is **not** changed. The provable method: read the values the reference sets at
  runtime, or compare two windows in one same-scale image. (Measurement note: Computer Use
  screenshots are shifted ~7 per channel — never use them for colour comparison.)
- `--cursor-accent` = `#599CE7` (blue) while the reference's VS Code theme sets
  `button.background` dark = `#81A1C1` (grey-blue) and light = `#2778C1` (blue). The agent UI's
  accent and the theme's accent therefore disagree with each other; Cedia uses the theme file's
  value. A capture of a real button is needed before deciding.
- `--conversation-block-gap` = `0px` and `--conversation-text-inset` = `0px` (one definition
  each) but **no consumer was found in the bundle**, so the reference's effective message-block
  spacing cannot be concluded; Cedia uses its own spacing.

**The gate (retired 2026-09-20).** The external cross-check (`check:cursor-parity`) read another
product's installed theme files; CEDIA owns its palette now, so the script is deleted. What it
once caught stays pinned by a test: the dark palette had set `titleBar.inactiveForeground` to
`#F0F0F05C` (36%) instead of `#F0F0F099` (60%). Token truth lives in
`apps/macos/src/cedia-theme.ts` and is locked by `apps/macos/test/cedia-theme.test.ts` with no
external reference.

### 3.5 Real sidebar IA (locked from the reference capture)

New Chat / Search / Automations / Customize are icon-prefixed rows · no persistent search field
and no scope chips in the sidebar · one Projects heading with a trailing `+` · a New Project row ·
a Repositories heading ending in filter buttons, followed by repo groups and task rows (status
dot, title, worktree icon, relative time) · a compact inline selector row above the composer
(project / branch / environment, each with a chevron) · the home column, mode pills and idea rows
share one left edge · idea rows are icon + title + muted description with a divider per row.

**Superseded for the Agents window (2026-09-19).** The rows above are *Cursor*'s Agents window —
the reference §3 locks for the IDE window. The Agents window's sidebar is the Synara port's now
(`apps/web/src/components/Sidebar.tsx` at `3333343`): `Search` is an icon button in the sidebar's
header rather than a row, the primary navigation is `New thread` / `Automations` (the reference's
`Kanban` and `Pull requests` have no OMP source), and `Projects` is a header whose trailing action
is `Add project`. Cursor's `Customize` row and `Repositories` section are gone from that window;
both commands stay registered, so the command palette and the IDE window still reach them. §9's
"The Agents window's sidebar is the reference's" carries the receipt.

## 4. CEDIA-owned surfaces (driven by OMP)

CEDIA's own design; they live in the same window:

- approvals/questions per OMP's schema (select/multi-select/input/editor + scope/cwd/tool)
- plan/goals/queue/subagent lineage + token budget
- models/providers/MCP/skills/hooks catalog exactly as OMP advertises it
- worktree/branch bring-back receipt + artifacts (MIME/hash/buildId)
- pending applicable capabilities → separate status page; hide from normal work menus;
  out-of-scope cloud agents and other harnesses are excluded, not future-feature promises

### 6.1 Patch mechanism constraints (measured, not theoretical)

`prepare-desktop.ts` proves "already applied" with `git apply --reverse --check` **per patch**,
plus a whole-set stamp (`desktop/.prepared.json`, ignored) carrying the manifest digest.
Four structural consequences, each hit in practice:

1. **A new patch that edits a file created by an earlier patch cannot verify.** If a later patch
   touches a file a previous patch *created* (e.g. `0021` creates `agentHomeUtilityEditor.ts`),
   the creator's reverse-check fails because the file no longer matches its post-image. Fold that
   work back into the patch that creates the file. **A patch that only edits base files can be a
   separate patch normally** (`0027` editing `editorTabsControl.ts` and `0028` editing
   `build/next/index.ts` both pass).
2. **`git apply` writes the worktree, not the index.** After applying earlier patches, `git diff`
   still compares against the base commit (a base-relative diff), which does not match the state
   the patch will really be applied to. This stays invisible until files are shared: when
   `sessions.common.main.ts` was also edited by `0005`, the apply failed because the patch tried
   to delete an import `0005` had already removed. **`git apply --index` the earlier patches
   before re-cutting.**
3. **Re-cut from the tree only for files that patch itself creates.** Files an earlier patch
   already edited, or a later patch will edit, must keep their original hunks instead of a
   regenerated section. Real case: `0022` creates `agentHomeNav.ts` but also edits
   `sessions.desktop.main.ts`, which `0023`/`0024` edit further — regenerating the whole file
   list from the working tree pulls `0023`/`0024`'s work in, and those two then fail to apply.

4. **Overlapping patches defeat per-patch reverse-check, so the stamp owns idempotency.** A later
   patch may rewrite lines an earlier patch added (0011's model-picker block was extended by
   0043/0045): on a fully patched tree that earlier patch then fails *both* reverse-check (its
   post-image is gone) and forward-check (its pre-image is gone), and no per-patch proof can
   pass. `prepare-desktop.ts` therefore skips the loop entirely when `.prepared.json` matches
   the manifest digest, and writes the stamp only after a full successful apply. Reset
   (`git checkout -- .` + `git clean -fd -e node_modules -e .build`, then re-run) deletes the
   stamp with everything else, so a reset tree always re-prepares from the base.

| **OMP runtime gate** — exact `omp/18.1.18` comparison in `apps/host/src/service.ts`, three smokes and `prepare-omp-runtime.ts` | **fixed (2026-09-15)**: `isSupportedOmpVersion()` in `packages/omp-adapter/src/types.ts` accepts the baseline or a newer patch in the same minor (accepting this machine's `18.1.22`) and rejects other minors, older versions or non-version strings. The host still probes with the same env it spawns with; `prepare-omp-runtime` still pins exactly because it builds the shipped artifact | Receipt [`evidence/s2-omp-version-gate-2026-09-15/`](evidence/s2-omp-version-gate-2026-09-15/) · the repo's smokes pass against a real OMP 18.1.22 · `apps/host` is green across the set |

### 6.4 Settings, migration and branding contract

**Target owner routing.** The proposed host settings service owns CEDIA app preferences,
replacing competing writes to `synara:app-settings:v1` and extension `cedia.productPrefs`.
Use `GET /v1/settings` and revision-checked `PATCH /v1/settings` as proposed contracts:
responses identify revision, supported field metadata and redacted values; patches name
expected revision, category and changed supported values. Unsupported key/scope is rejected;
stale revision produces a conflict and fresh read, never a silent last-writer overwrite.
These routes do not exist in the current host. Global app/OMP/IDE preference mutations
are owner-only; controllers receive only the redacted values/capabilities required by their
surfaces and may write their own device-local display preferences. Per-task model/control
commands retain their existing controller authorization. A successful app patch is atomic; writes to
external owners are separately acknowledged/read back, not falsely presented as one cross-
store transaction. Broadcast invalidation to both windows and refresh on reconnect.

Each metadata row must carry key, English label, scope, read/write owner, default,
reset category, migration disposition and capability condition. The table below is the
allowlist for initial integration. A new upstream setting has no effect until classified.
`Fresh` means initialize the stated new-design default and report the discarded preference;
`Preserve` means retain a compatible value/reference without copying its authoritative store.
Defaults described as proposals are reviewable engineering choices, not new owner answers.

| Category / fields | Scope and authoritative read/write path | Default / reset / migration |
|---|---|---|
| General > Appearance: app theme (proposed `appTheme`) | CEDIA app preference → shared main/workbench/bundle token propagation | Proposal: System, with CEDIA Light/Dark choices. Reset Appearance only; Fresh. IDE syntax selection never changes this value. |
| `uiDensity`, `chatWidth`, `chatFontSizePx` | CEDIA app preference; responsive renderer projection | Comfortable, standard, 12px (11–18); Fresh; Appearance reset. Merge duplicate productPrefs density into this one key. |
| `uiFontFamily`, native font smoothing | CEDIA app preference, capability-gated desktop rendering | System font; smoothing follows existing macOS platform default; Fresh. No font-setting control until renderer actually consumes it. |
| Chat code font (`chatCodeFontFamily`) | CEDIA app presentation; distinct from IDE font | Default monospace; pending verified consumption, hidden until wired; Fresh. |
| `terminalFontSizePx`, `terminalFontFamily` | CEDIA app-owned terminal presentation, not IDE terminal configuration | 12px (10–22), default monospace; Fresh. IDE terminal retains its own settings owner. |
| Reduced motion / high contrast | CEDIA app preference with OS accessibility inputs | Proposal: follow OS by default, explicit override in Advanced; Fresh. One path replaces duplicate productPrefs flags. |
| `timestampFormat` | CEDIA app preference | Locale; Preserve if supported; General reset. Does not change English product labels. |
| `sidebarProjectSortOrder`, `sidebarThreadSortOrder`, `showChatsSection` | CEDIA app preference; project/task identities stay host-owned | Manual project order, updated task order, Chats shown; Fresh layout. |
| `sidebarNavOrder`, `hiddenSidebarNavItems` | CEDIA app preference, filtered through capability registry | Default supported route order, none hidden; Fresh. Unsupported/deep-linked routes cannot reappear due to old values. |
| Environment presentation | CEDIA app preference: `environmentPanelDefaultOpen`, `showEnvironmentRepository`, `showEnvironmentEditor` | Closed by default; repository/editor visible when backed; Fresh. |
| Pending Environment presentation | Usage/PR/recap/pinned/instructions/notepad and `showAutomationRunThreads` | Underlying capability gates precede controls. When enabled: existing schema defaults (usage/PR/recap/pinned/run threads true, instructions/notepad false). Fresh; no active effect while pending. |
| Layout restoration / startup | CEDIA app preference for restore policy; base owns actual window geometry and per-window open panel | Automatic background start after macOS login (53A); explicit opening can restore windows. Fresh layout. Login itself opens no work window and replays no task. Device-local dimensions do not synchronize to phone or resize another window. Remove duplicate sidebar-width/panel-position authorities. |
| Composer Send behavior | Product invariant plus contextual keybindings | Active-turn Send queues; Steer separate. Normalize legacy `followUpBehavior=steer` to queue with migration notice. No preference may silently reverse the accepted command meaning. |
| `composerEffortSlider`, `enableAssistantStreaming`, `diffWordWrap` | CEDIA presentation preferences; actual effort remains OMP session state | Slider true, display streaming true, wrap false; Preserve compatible behavior values. Streaming toggle changes rendering, not OMP execution/transport. |
| `confirmThreadDelete`, `confirmThreadArchive`, `confirmTerminalTabClose` | CEDIA app preference; safety/lifecycle guards stay mandatory | True / false / true; Preserve. Archive/dirty/active-use checks cannot be disabled through these preferences. |
| Notifications | CEDIA per-device preferences; OS permission/delivery state owned by platform | Proposal: completion/error/input categories on once delivery permission is granted; in-app toasts on. Generic lock-screen text default; details opt-in. Preserve compatible switches; never treat a preference as OS permission. |
| General > Shortcuts | `Cedia/User/keybindings.json` via `agent-window-keybindings.ts` and workbench resolver | Familiar IDE defaults; Preserve compatible bindings. Preview resets only chosen CEDIA bindings/category; retain unrelated extension/user bindings and report context conflicts. |
| General > Remote and devices | Host `auth.ts`/`remote.ts`, owner-only enrollment/revocation | Remote off until explicitly configured/enrolled. Devices/tokens are actions and credentials, excluded from Reset. Existing enrollments require explicit migration compatibility validation; do not silently revoke or activate. |
| AI/OMP > Providers and authentication | `/v1/providers` and host `provider-auth.ts` → OMP auth/config | Discover current shared OMP state. Preserve references; no credential copying. Sign-out/removal is an explicit action, never category Reset. |
| AI/OMP > Model catalog / favorites | Catalog: `/v1/models` → OMP; favorite IDs: CEDIA presentation preference | Catalog read-only; favorites initially empty, compatible IDs preserved. Missing models show unresolved selection rather than another harness. |
| Task model/effort / mode | OMP session via host command path, not app settings | New-task persistent default comes from OMP when configured; Act default with explicit Plan. Preserve existing session selection; pending model rule in §3.C. No category Reset alters active sessions. |
| AI/OMP > persistent defaults, custom models, skills, MCP, hooks, tool policy | OMP's actual config scopes/read-write-reload path verified in R1/R2 | Preserve existing global/project config. Show effective source/scope and restart/reload timing. Only verified editable keys appear; reset uses OMP's supported defaults with shared-CLI-impact preview, never deletes auth or skill files. |
| Project > primary branch / workspace | Host project metadata + Git facts; proposed project-setting fields | Detected/configured primary branch, worktree isolation for new concurrent Git tasks. Preserve explicit branch choice. Generic `defaultThreadEnvMode=local` cannot disable this rule. |
| IDE > save, syntax/font, language/debug/extensions | Code-OSS configuration service with User/Workspace scope | Manual Save for fresh profile; Preserve compatible existing IDE preferences with report. Expose scope and use original settings UI for specialist controls. Reset removes only selected explicit overrides and never uninstalls extensions or deletes files. |
| General > Updates | CEDIA release/update owner; upstream intake governed by §8 | Notify when a validated compatible update is available; installation is an explicit owner action. No automatic install or restart on Quit. Preserve the current working app until update/rollback gates pass. |
| Onboarding/version metadata | CEDIA application lifecycle, not editable preference | Preserve compatible completion marker; initial new-design tour can explain changes. Excluded from category Reset. |

The active registered task's composer reads `/v1/sessions/:id/models` from its
existing OMP owner, with a task-scoped query cache. This read never starts another
runtime. Local drafts and settings use `/v1/models`; a registered task may fall
back to that metadata only on an explicit no-live-runtime response, never after
a failed live read. Session extension models must not leak into another task or
global configuration, and removed models are revalidated against the same owner
before selection.

**Explicit exclusions:** all non-OMP harness binary paths, homes, endpoint/password/socket
settings, custom-model arrays, provider enabled/hidden/order/install/update fields; generic
`textGenerationProvider`/writing-model defaults; Synara accounts and their cloud/reset-credit controls,
AppSnap/Safari vault, alternate branded icons, non-Mac titlebar settings, and automatic
`resumeChatsAfterQuit`. OMP is fixed as the harness rather than an editable `defaultProvider`.
Simulator auto-open is hidden until its device capability is real; propose off by default
so it does not open a panel unsolicited. PR diff-color controls wait for real PR support.
Profile identity fields are not needed for personal device authorization and stay hidden
unless a concrete local display use is implemented. Advanced never bypasses these exclusions.

**Reset/migration rules:** one registry drives visible controls, search/deep links, validation,
reset preview, import and background effects. Reset identifies category/scope/affected keys;
authorize only that set and report individual owner failures. It never clears history,
workspace files, credentials, enrolled devices or arbitrary IDE settings. Persistent OMP
changes explicitly warn that terminal OMP shares them. Migration preserves source data,
projects/history/session identities, OMP references and compatible IDE settings; appearance
and layout are Fresh as the owner selected. Unsupported/unknown legacy data stays inert in
the private source backup with a redacted report, not an active quarantine settings store.
Do not import legacy secrets into reports. `getSettings` synthetic defaults and unsupported
`updateSettings` are replaced before these controls are advertised as working.

**Theme seam:** CEDIA's app-theme value selects chrome tokens shared by both windows. The
IDE may retain Code-OSS theme machinery to render syntax, but effective chrome overrides
come from the app theme. Verify Light/Dark/System, arbitrary installed syntax themes and
restart in R2; no observer may write a syntax change back as an app-theme selection.

**Branding seam:** visible names, accessibility labels, icons, onboarding, About, help,
release notes and updater destination are CEDIA-owned. Keep upstream source identifiers
and required license notices; do not mass-rename internal Synara symbols. Source scanning
must distinguish provenance from user-facing leaks, and runtime checks must cover deep
links, old profiles, error messages, network effects and both windows.

### 6.5 Remote design and remaining decision gates

**Confirmed owner choices 50A/51A (2026-09-23):** Tailscale private networking
is the primary remote connection. The owner accepts installing/signing in to Tailscale on
the Mac and participating devices; CEDIA retains its separate QR/code enrollment and device
revocation, without adding a CEDIA account. Prefer supporting services with no additional
service charge. If an essential feature requires payment, retain it as a documented pending
capability rather than silently buying a service or dropping the target. This cost policy
excludes the model usage the owner already pays for; it is not a new spending authorization.

**Speech-to-text deferred (owner, scrutinize review, 2026-09-23):** all voice input,
including Thai/English, desktop/remote capture, engine selection and performance work,
is outside this delivery. R1–R8 and current acceptance do not depend on it. Keep existing
voice routes/controls inactive rather than adding an engine or cloud fallback. Earlier
52A/55A/56A decisions and source research are retained as rationale for a later feature
review; they do not authorize or require current speech work. Shared text-draft ownership
(57A) remains in scope independently of voice.

| Selected design | Implementation boundary | Prerequisites / proof |
|---|---|---|
| Private Tailscale access | Tailscale Serve terminates tailnet HTTPS and forwards to a dedicated CEDIA gateway on localhost, serving the same-origin web client and authenticated controller routes. Funnel/public exposure is excluded. Forward through existing host/router without exposing the loopback owner token. Tailnet access is additional to CEDIA device enrollment. | Tailscale clients/account on participating devices; eligible Personal plan has no subscription charge per dated research. Existing host rejects browser Origin and non-loopback Host, so gateway is actual integration work. TLS, origin policy, enrollment and mobile transport must be verified end to end. |

Paseo relay remains an existing implementation/source-reuse asset, not the selected network
path. R6 must prevent its current default endpoint from activating during Tailscale pairing,
startup or reconnect. Do not silently fall back to either the official or a self-hosted
Paseo relay when Tailscale is unavailable. Preserve old enrollment/identity records during
migration, explain that the selected transport requires new connection setup, and do not
open the previous route as a side effect. Relay alternatives need a future explicit change
of direction; they are not extra required deployments or settings in this initial design.

**Remote web artifact and serving contract:** reuse the existing Expo/React Native web
renderer from `apps/ios`; do not port the full desktop Synara renderer into another web app.
`bun run --cwd apps/ios export:web` produces the export; a CEDIA build step copies that output
to `dist/remote-web` and packages it under `extensions/cedia/runtime/remote-web`. R6 owns this
build/asset-manifest path, static serving and transport wiring in `apps/ios/App.tsx` and
`src/core/{pairing,transport,api}.ts`; R8 checks its presence in a clean packaged app.

The localhost-only gateway serves the packaged web client at `/`, known assets and valid
SPA deep links; `/v1/*` remains an authenticated API namespace, never an HTML fallback.
Unknown API paths return structured errors. Add gateway-scoped enrollment/session/CSRF
routes without exposing the native owner endpoint. Resolve the web session to its paired
controller principal and proxy with that controller authorization, never a universal owner
credential. Serve only manifest-owned web assets, no workspace-directory static serving.
Use the existing bounded event polling/cursor contract initially; a new socket protocol is
not required. Do not cache authenticated API responses or transcripts in a service worker.

Web runtime uses same-origin HTTPS fetch and cookie/CSRF auth; native iPhone uses an explicit
validated tailnet HTTPS endpoint and SecureStore-held controller token. Adapt the existing
HTTP transport for these separate auth modes; do not store browser bearer credentials.
Replace relay-only offer parsing with a versioned transport discriminator. Preserve legacy
Paseo offers inertly; loading old enabled state cannot activate its relay. Pairing labels
show the actual transport and endpoint. End-to-end gates include fresh package assets,
reload/deep links, MIME/error handling, same-origin auth/CSRF, reconnect, revocation and an
actual web client controlling the Mac over a different network.

Pairing proposal: the Mac owner displays a short-lived, single-use enrollment code/QR;
acceptance creates a named revocable controller credential. Do not embed the owner token or
provider secrets. Display endpoint identity and device name before confirmation. Bootstrap
material is not persisted as a reusable QR; controller credentials use each platform's
appropriate private storage. For the web client, exchange enrollment for a revocable host-side session with a Secure,
HttpOnly, SameSite cookie scoped to the gateway, enforce the exact configured HTTPS Origin
and Host, and validate CSRF on mutating requests. Check WebSocket origins and device
revocation on active connections as well as new requests. Do not put reusable credentials
in URL queries or renderer localStorage. Trust Serve identity headers only from the
localhost proxy path and never as a substitute for CEDIA enrollment. Native clients use
revocable controller tokens over HTTPS; personal Mac builds retain the no-Keychain rule. Changing transport is explicit and shows which
devices must reconnect/re-pair. Disabling Remote closes remote access without cancelling
local work; revoking a device blocks further commands and invalidates its connected session.

Remote operation semantics are transport-independent: preserve task/command/incarnation IDs,
resume bounded events with full authoritative snapshot when history is truncated, refresh
models/project lists, settle approvals once, and never replay uncertain mutations. Terminal
input/resize/close target an identified terminal. The default remote terminal is the owner's
separate host-owned user shell in that task's cwd, not the AI tool's terminal. Multiple views
attach to its same terminal ID. AI terminals are separately labelled/selectable, and opening
the panel never injects keystrokes into them. Reconnect restores a checkpoint and marks
missing output. Closing a mobile panel detaches its view rather than killing the Mac shell;
active user shells participate in Quit and worktree-cleanup guards. A host restart reports
terminated shells honestly instead of replaying their commands.

**Native notifications are a separate gate:** foreground/in-app activity is not suspended-
app delivery. R7 must establish an actual platform delivery mechanism, signing/credentials,
privacy and any service cost before claiming locked-device notifications. If that requires
an additional service/account, present its concrete setup to the owner; do not silently add
it under the transport choice. The feature remains pending until this dependency is settled.
The free-first scope can show foreground/in-app status. Apple APNs delivery additionally
requires a provider connection, device tokens, signing and the applicable developer-account
capability; it remains deferred under 51A. Local notifications alone cannot deliver an
unexpected Mac event to a suspended iPhone. Native device installation/signing and retention
limits also need an actual-device receipt; web availability does not certify the native app.
These gates name missing evidence or user inputs; they are not permission to substitute a harness or to call the full product complete.

### 6.6 Browser and terminal source-reuse assessment (2026-09-23)

The owner requested evaluation of Search, `stablyai/orca` and Ghostty, not replacement of
the chosen application base. These recommendations guide later capability intake; they do
not add new D/W/N release requirements or authorize dependency installation. Pinned source
findings are in §13. Keep CEDIA branding, its shared settings and OMP execution boundaries.

| Project | Relevant value | Recommendation and boundary |
|---|---|---|
| Search (`driceroland/Search`) | Compact browser chrome, lazy tab creation/session restoration and explicit suspended states | Use as a UX/source reference. It is a native Swift/WebKit executable, not an Electron browser widget. Keep the existing Electron browser owner; do not add a second engine, its Keychain/password store, updater or extension system. Do not expose its Bench socket as CEDIA's agent API. |
| Orca (`stablyai/orca`) | Closest source reference for worktree-bound browser tabs, page snapshot/action flows, element-to-agent context and terminal interactions | Adapt bounded patterns/components only where simpler than existing CEDIA/Synara code. Orca is a complete Electron multi-agent orchestrator and already lists OMP support; this does not certify CEDIA's RPC/config/transcript contract. Do not import its agent launcher, settings owners, remote servers or telemetry. |
| Ghostty / libghostty-vt | Headless terminal parsing/state/checkpoints | Retain the existing optional `@coder/libghostty-vt-node` host path and verify packaged availability honestly. Keep xterm.js in visible Agent/IDE/web/iPhone terminals. The current public VT core is not a ready-made Electron GUI widget; a renderer swap is a separate measured project, not this integration prerequisite. |

**Browser improvement order (later capability, after the required manual browser):**

1. Keep tabs attached to the task and add an explicit Send page/selection/screenshot to AI
   action using the existing attachment/preview and draft target contract. Include source URL,
   task/page identity and capture time. Page text is untrusted context, never system policy.
   Reuse existing tabs/session/lazy-runtime behavior before adding another implementation.
2. Give OMP a typed browser bridge owned by CEDIA, using opaque task/page handles and an
   explicit automation-enabled page/profile. `snapshot → action → snapshot` carries a
   navigation generation; stale references fail instead of acting on a different document.
   Preserve cancellation, bounded results, existing permissions and an explicit user/agent
   control state. Current CEDIA tabs share a browser partition; task-associated tabs do not
   prove cookie isolation. Use separate automation profiles or explicit attachment to a
   user page, without automatic reuse/import of personal logged-in sessions.
3. Evaluate Orca-style element inspection/Design Mode after that bridge passes: selected DOM,
   computed style, screenshot and verified source-map location into an editable prompt.
   Missing or ambiguous source mapping stays unavailable rather than inventing a file path.
   Responsive viewport/console/network aids are subsequent scoped candidates, not another IDE.

Orca's `agent-browser` is an automation helper, not itself a model harness. It can be
considered only if a bounded comparison shows it is simpler than extending CEDIA's existing
Electron/OMP browser seam. Audit and pin its daemon/CDP ownership, binary packaging, version
compatibility, download/install behavior and shutdown before adoption; never expose a raw
CDP endpoint or all logged-in tabs through remote-controller authentication by default.
No helper or extra Chromium download is selected by this research.

**Terminal improvement order:** first deliver the already-required task user shell and
remote reconnect in §6.5, with visible separation from OMP tool terminals. Reuse existing
xterm tabs/splits/search/link/selection features where present; improve explicit Copy/Send
selected output to AI with task, cwd and terminal identity, without sending keys to the
shell or treating output as trusted instructions. Keep scrollback bounded and report gaps.
Do not add a second terminal settings center or automatically import Ghostty theme/config:
Orca's Ghostty feature imports appearance, not Ghostty's engine, and automatic appearance
import would recreate the settings collision this redesign addresses.

A future Ghostty-backed visible renderer must demonstrate a measured problem in the retained
xterm path and pass input/IME, selection, accessibility, search, images, resize, escape-sequence
and reconnect tests on each affected platform before replacing it. Existing Ghostty headless
checkpoints are not that proof, and optional native-load failure must not advertise checkpoint
support. No performance or memory improvement is claimed without a comparative runtime test.

## 7. Shared React presentation boundary

The current desktop agent surface is the Synara-derived React bundle described in §2/§3,
rendered in the standalone Agents window and compact IDE Agent Chat. Code-OSS continues to
own the editor and workbench. Reusing React does not require replacing the IDE shell or
maintaining a second native chat renderer.

| Layer | Source / boundary |
|---|---|
| Shared conversation/composer/task presentation | Vendored Synara `apps/web` components through the CEDIA adapter |
| Host/tool/session data | CEDIA protocol and host → OMP, never renderer-owned execution |
| Workbench integration | Main-process and extension bridges; desktop changes captured through §6.1's patch mechanism |
| Styling | Synara composition, CEDIA shared app tokens and responsive compact layout; scope styles to avoid workbench CSS collisions |

The 2026-09-15 React-island experiment and its old R1–R4 labels are historical, superseded by
the shared bundle. Its evidence remains in §9 and
[`cursor-agent-window-architecture-2026-09-15`](evidence/cursor-agent-window-architecture-2026-09-15/).
It creates no new requirement to restore `src/vs/sessions` React roots, chase Cursor pixel
parity, or adopt Untitled UI. The only current R1–R8 sequence is §8, tracked under §10 item 70.

## 8. Execution sequence for the consolidated direction

This is the final implementation sequence. Execution begins on an explicit owner request
to implement; that request does not need a repeated design approval. §10 item 70 alone
tracks its unfinished work. S1–S5 identify the historical delivery stages (remove
Copilot, provider integration, duplicate retirement, desktop surfaces, mobile continuity);
the dated implementations and old D1–D5 receipts remain in §9. Do not reintroduce a native
chatSessionsProvider or obsolete webview shell to satisfy those historical steps.

Each slice starts by checking its current sources and relevant §9 receipts, then writes a
failing boundary test for the missing behavior, implements the smallest owner-path change,
and records the resulting revision and checks. No slice may advance a capability to
available based solely on a schema check, adapter stub, or an earlier version's receipt.
Paths below are implementation ownership boundaries; newly proposed files are labelled.

| Slice / dependencies | Files and interfaces | Required deliverable and verification |
|---|---|---|
| R1 — ownership and capability boundary; first | Modify `packages/protocol/src/index.ts`, host `router.ts`/`service.ts`, desktop `cedia-adapter.ts`; create host `capabilities.ts`, proposed `apps/macos/src/app-lifecycle.ts`, host CLI/server adoption/shutdown handshake and tests | Implement §2.7 as the prerequisite for app/remote availability. A typed capability response includes availability, scope, reason and supported operations, consumed by navigation/actions/search/deep links. Audit OMP native operations, CLI ownership and settings reload gates in §2.2. Assert one OMP session owner and refusal of another harness. A fake-success/empty unsupported API is never enabled. |
| R2 — settings, branding and migration; after R1 | Create host `settings.ts` and `drafts.ts` plus tests; add store/protocol/main-bridge draft migration and CAS routes under §2.5; extend the tracked OMP patch at `rpc-types.ts`/`rpc-mode.ts` and adapter command types for the versioned settings bridge; modify protocol/router, desktop adapter, vendor `appSettings.ts`, `settingsNavigation.ts`, `settingsSearchIndex.ts`, settings route/panels; theme/keybinding bridges | Implement §2.5 draft CAS/broadcast/migration and §6.4 owner routing and revisioned writes, shared theme with independent syntax, category-reset preview and migration report. Fixture fresh and legacy profiles, unknown fields, conflicting writers, restart and failed owner writes. Capture both packaged windows updating from one theme/shortcut change. |
| R3 — task/turn and workspace lifecycle; after R1 and R2 draft storage | Host `service.ts`, `store.ts`, `workspaces.ts`, `git.ts`, `runtime-lock.ts`; protocol and tracked OMP queue/turn-boundary patch plus adapter; desktop task/composer handlers | Implement §2.4 intent/queue projection and pending-model boundary, §2.5 Send reservation and §2.6 durable archive/restore state machine; keep cleanup inactive until its full gate passes. Implement §3.C starting-commit/worktree rules. Tests must cover two tasks from one repo, copied selected dirty files, shared-folder admission, invalid pending model, retained branch/revision, ignored files, and active-terminal cleanup veto. Prove CLI attachment or keep that capability explicitly blocked. |
| R4 — full IDE and AI handoff; after R2/R3 | `apps/macos/src/agent-ide-webview.ts`, `ide-bootstrap.ts`, selection/editor/review modules, `agent-window-main.ts`; patch stack only when the actual workbench owner changes | Implement §3.D compact chat and explicit handoff. Test two independent Mac draft renderers and Send/edit races, task A in IDE while AI selects B, Cancel on dirty switch, unsaved snapshot attachment and external disk-write conflict preserving both texts. Verify language navigation, debugger, extensions, workbench terminal and keyboard focus still work in the packaged IDE. |
| R5 — checkpoint-required agent surfaces; after R1, use R2/R3 when needed | Shared vendor chat components and adapter; host/OMP bridges; existing browser/device/terminal/git owners named in §2.3 | Work through §8.1 checkpoint requirements and the §8.2 O01–O12 packets for F. Separate Synara-only additions do not expand a checkpoint. Reuse existing verified paths. Tool cards, permissions, skills, attachments, review and subagent lineage must show actual OMP outcomes. Validate no duplicate authorization and no unsupported live-looking control. Speech-to-text is deferred and must not gate this slice. Fix existing rewind idle-state defect; do not promise workspace rollback before a separate snapshot gate passes. |
| R6 — remote connection and device boundary; after R1/R3; Tailscale selected | Host `auth.ts`, `remote.ts`, `server.ts`, `router.ts`; proposed dedicated `remote-gateway.ts`; existing relay path stays inactive for the selected mode; mobile core transport/pairing/API plus Expo web export/static-asset packaging | Require §2.7 lifecycle proof before availability. Produce the selected §6.5 transport, short-lived enrollment, revocation and authenticated client access. Preserve loopback owner API restrictions. Test hostile origin/Host, expired enrollment, revoked device, command deduplication, reconnect/new incarnation and uncertain outcomes. An actual remote-network device check is required before availability. |
| R7 — remote control and native iPhone verification; after R4/R6 and R1 lifecycle | `apps/ios/src` views/core and tests; host terminal state/lifetime; repeat R1 desktop lifecycle with connected devices; notification integration only after its delivery gate | Complete remote task/project/model/approval/diff/terminal controls. Test two clients answering one request, foreground/reconnect behavior, window-close background continuation, explicit Quit and sleeping/offline host, auto-start after login and explicit-send offline drafts. Compile native iOS and verify on a physical iPhone using cellular; suspended/locked notifications require a separate proven delivery path. |
| R8 — integrated release and upstream maintenance; after relevant slices | `scripts/build-cedia.ts`, `scripts/check-packaged-shell.ts`, `patches/desktop/manifest.json`, vendored `upstream.json`, docs lock/notices and §13 evidence | Record the exact CEDIA revision, upstream versions, patch digest and app build. Verify fresh and migrated profiles against the claimed D/W/N/F checkpoint and §11 matrix, including packaged remote-web assets for W/N. Do not call partial-checkpoint evidence full-product acceptance. Adopt upstream updates only after affected gates pass; keep the previous working build and its compatible state backup until then. |

### 8.1 Fixed delivery checkpoints

R1–R8 are implementation slices, not a promise to ship every upstream capability in one
release. Apply R8 verification to each named checkpoint below. Required entries cannot be
hidden or reclassified as pending to pass that checkpoint; failures block its release.
Required OMP remainders advance to F through §8.2 rather than remaining indefinitely
deferred. D/W/N may ship earlier; they cannot be described as complete OMP integration.
Extra Synara application workflows stay in the separate intake scope. Speech-to-text is
explicitly outside D/W/N/F by owner request.

- **D — integrated Mac workspace:** one app/two windows, create an isolated task, execute
  through OMP, queue/steer/Stop/change pending model, use tools/approvals, open the correct IDE
  workspace, preserve unsaved edits, review results, and continue history. Common settings,
  draft CAS/Send, migration and lifecycle tests are mandatory. Archive may retain worktrees;
  cleanup and external-live-CLI attachment stay unavailable until their separate protocols pass.
- **W — same-Mac remote web:** D plus the packaged Expo web client, Tailscale gateway,
  enrollment/revocation, task/project/model/send/stop/approval/diff/artifact controls and the
  separate host-owned user terminal. Prove concurrent Mac/web control, reconnect and closing
  both windows with actual off-LAN web access. R1 lifecycle proof is a prerequisite.
- **N — native iPhone:** W plus native build/signing/install and physical-iPhone cellular,
  keyboard/terminal/WebView, secure credential storage and foreground-notification evidence.
  APNs/locked-device notifications remain separately deferred under the existing cost policy.
- **F — full pinned-OMP integration:** N plus §2.8/§8.2 O01–O12, complete registry-to-
  surface/test mapping, qualified CLI continuity, advanced settings/management and all
  applicable native OMP controls/tools. Every integration-missing entry blocks F. Platform
  equivalents and optional dependency states must meet the explicit §2.8 evidence rules.

| §2.3 capability family | Required checkpoint / explicitly deferred remainder |
|---|---|
| Chat/composer/model/effort | D; W/N must preserve the same semantics and identities. |
| Transcript/tools/approvals/presentations | D for actual OMP tools/requests/history; W/N for remote projection and one-response resolution. |
| Projects/tasks/search/history | D for CEDIA-managed tasks, stable project/workspace selection and complete paginated retained-history reads; W/N for task/project selection and new tasks. OMP-native import and qualified live CLI attachment are required for F (O02/O08); uninstrumented external owners follow §2.8. |
| Attachments/files/mentions | D for authorized text/image context and explicit unsaved snapshots; remote upload/attachment UI is required for F (O05), while remote text commands remain required. |
| Skills/slash/compaction | D for actual session skill/slash discovery/use and compaction. Sessionless catalog and management are required for F (O06). |
| MCP/hooks/plugins | D must preserve existing OMP configuration/execution and show honest effective status for supported fields; native OMP catalog/marketplace and graphical management are required for F (O06). No synthetic empty success. |
| Plan/goals/subagents/sidechat | D for explicit Plan instructions, actual OMP subagent status/lineage and verified sidechat/fork semantics. Native Plan/Goal/AgentHub/Advisor controls are required for F (O07/O08). |
| Full IDE and Agent Chat | D including language navigation/debug/extensions/terminal and the same compact chat; phone is not a second full IDE. |
| Review/Git/turn diffs/rewind | D for working-tree diff, explicit commit/merge/push and safe conversation rewind. OMP checkpoint/rewind and safe cleanup/restore are required for F (O02/R3). PR hosting and additional snapshot engines remain separate app-feature intake. |
| Terminal | D retains working Agents/IDE/OMP roles; W/N requires a separate host user-shell owner and reconnect without duplicate PTYs. Renderer replacement is not required. |
| Browser | D retains working user navigation/tabs and correct task context; OMP browser/computer integration is required for F (O10). No browser-engine replacement is required. |
| Simulator / Device pane | Later; actual Xcode/helper availability and independent acceptance required. |
| Artifacts / previews | D for task-bound metadata and supported previews; W/N for authenticated bounded fetch/diff/preview. Unsupported formats remain explicitly unsupported. |
| Automations / schedules | Separate app-feature intake; native OMP jobs are required for F, while a new CEDIA schedule service is not an OMP-completeness prerequisite. |
| Local servers / project scripts | OMP-native process/job control is required for F (O07/O11); additional Synara server-management UI remains separate intake. |
| Usage / statistics | D preserves backed task/context usage and honest unknowns; provider-wide accounting/statistics are required for F when the OMP provider exposes them (O03). |
| Voice / speech-to-text | Deferred by the owner; inactive boundary in D/W/N/F. |
| Notifications | D in-app completion/error/input; W/N connected foreground behavior. OS background delivery/APNs is later; foreground success never certifies suspended delivery. |
| Kanban / PR lists / Studio / Spaces / note/recap workflows | Later applicability/integration intake, outside D/W/N. No competing project/task store. |
| Settings / onboarding / shortcuts / updates | D common settings/branding/auth ownership, category reset/migration and manual validated-update policy; W/N gets only permitted controller/device controls. |
| AppSnap / Safari vault / other harnesses / cloud engines | Excluded, not deferred capability promises. |

Each required entry has a corresponding §11.1 scenario and must record source/fixture and
packaged/device evidence at the checkpoint. A later upstream feature does not automatically
enlarge a release already under verification. Reopening these boundaries requires an
explicit plan change rather than changing a capability flag to make acceptance pass.

**Commands for execution, not tests claimed by this document:**

```sh
node scripts/ci-validate.mjs
bun test packages/omp-adapter packages/relay apps/host apps/macos/test scripts/lib
bun test apps/macos/agent-window/test
bun run --cwd apps/macos/agent-window typecheck
bun run typecheck
bun run test:mobile
bun run --cwd apps/ios typecheck
bun run --cwd apps/ios export:web
bun run build:agent
bun run package:mac
bun run check:packaged
```

Run targeted suites during each slice and the broader relevant suites at integration.
The isolated agent-UI typecheck includes its vendor tree; root typecheck excludes it.
The web export/package-asset checks are mandatory for W/N, not proof of native readiness.
Native iOS build/device and provider-backed smoke commands require the appropriate device,
credentials and existing authorization at that step; mock-only tests remain provider-free.
Do not automatically run live provider/relay scripts as part of the isolated gate.

**Migration and rollback:** before a schema migration, create a private versioned backup
and record source/schema versions. Migrate into a separate candidate state, validate counts,
references and OMP session identities, then atomically select it; retain the original on any
failure. Preserve OMP-owned config/auth and unsupported source records. Fresh app appearance
is deliberate and reported, not data loss hidden as successful migration. Restarting a
migration must be idempotent. Rollback stops candidate execution before returning to the
last compatible app/state pair; never run two owners on a shared OMP session or blindly
restore a stale backup over sessions modified after migration. Newer state without a safe
downgrade path keeps the previous copy intact and presents an explicit recovery choice.

**Update installation policy (owner confirmed):** notify about validated compatible CEDIA
updates and install only when the owner selects the action. Do not install automatically on
Quit or restart the app underneath active work. Resolve active work and unsaved buffers
through the existing lifecycle rules before installation; a refused/busy update leaves the
current build usable. Upstream components are integrated/tested as a CEDIA release rather
than independently auto-updated into an unverified combination.

**Upstream intake:** keep the Synara-derived presentation delta and adapter boundary
reviewable; retain upstream names/licenses internally. For each proposed upstream revision,
classify feature additions against §2.3, review provider/settings/updater/egress changes,
run affected contract tests and packaged workflows, and record adoption or deferral in the
existing plan/evidence. Upstream OMP support may replace an adapter implementation only
when it preserves the same owner/config/session contracts. It does not remove the full-IDE,
mobile, migration or acceptance obligations automatically.

### 8.2 Full OMP completion packets (F)

These packets refine R1–R8; they are not a second sequence or a second unfinished-work
list. §10 item 70 owns status. Reuse completed behavior after re-verification; each packet
starts with its failing boundary fixtures and ends with source, packaged and applicable
remote evidence. Proposed files below do not yet exist unless already present in the tree.
Every audited registry record maps to one O-family; supporting families may share a test.

**Shared wire contract (O04/R1 prerequisite).** Add protocol types and validation in
`packages/protocol/src/index.ts`, translate only registered operations in
`packages/omp-adapter/src/{types,client,ui,host}.ts`, and add a host-owned
`apps/host/src/omp-capabilities.ts` module for metadata/projection. Reuse R1 `capabilities.ts`
as the aggregate rather than creating another feature-state store. The proposed OMP bridge
advertises `cediaCapabilitiesVersion: 1`; new requests `cedia_get_capabilities` and
`cedia_control` are explicitly negotiated alongside existing CEDIA bridges. `cedia_control`
is a discriminated union whose `operation` has a schema and handler in a static registration
table; its payload cannot name an arbitrary JS function, executable or source text.
Use OMP's existing RPC operations wherever they already express the action. Host admission
continues to use the existing authenticated command envelope and §2.4 idempotency, not a
new unauthenticated endpoint. Session-scoped commands carry task/session/incarnation and
capability revision; owner-only config/maintenance operations use a separate registered
principal check. No credential is embedded in a capability descriptor.

```ts
// Proposed shared metadata; operation-specific requests/results retain separate typed schemas.
type OmpCoverageFamily =
  | "O01" | "O02" | "O03" | "O04" | "O05" | "O06"
  | "O07" | "O08" | "O09" | "O10" | "O11" | "O12";
type OmpCapabilityState = "available" | "dependency_unavailable" | "integration_missing";
interface OmpCapabilityDescriptor {
  id: string; family: OmpCoverageFamily; capabilityRevision: string;
  ompRevision: string; bridgeVersion: number; schemaVersion: number;
  surfaces: Array<"agent" | "ide" | "web" | "iphone">;
  principal: "owner" | "controller";
  state: OmpCapabilityState; reason?: string;
  scope: "session" | "project" | "global" | "device";
  apply: "immediate" | "turn_boundary" | "reload" | "new_session";
}
```

The allowlisted descriptor/handler table also stores source identity, default/reset policy
where relevant and test/receipt IDs. Discovery metadata is not execution authorization.
Dynamic third-party tools use OMP's existing tool schemas and transport; they do not become
arbitrary new privileged `cedia_control` operations. The same family/surface mapping powers
capability status, command discovery and the F coverage check.

| O04 — schema, config and inventory; R1/R2 first | Proposed `apps/host/src/omp-capabilities.ts`; R1 `capabilities.ts`, R2 `settings.ts`; `packages/protocol/src/index.ts`; tracked patch to OMP `modes/rpc/rpc-types.ts`, `rpc-mode.ts`, config Settings APIs; proposed `apps/host/test/omp-capabilities.test.ts`, `omp-settings.test.ts` | Unknown operation/key/scope denied; advertised operation without handler fails coverage; stale config or capability revision conflicts; global/project precedence and effective readback survive CLI edits/restart. Generated controls classify every audited schema key, including TUI-only equivalent/read-only keys. Changing a future key cannot silently alter running sessions. |

Runtime update remains CEDIA-owned. Regenerate all inventories and fail for uncovered operation/setting/tool/event, missing renderer or unqualified enabled claim. Run each packet's suites, §8 build/typecheck/package checks and §11 packaged/remote scenarios on one attested revision. Record exact supported/optional-unavailable combinations. |

**F proof is finite and inspectable.** The proposed `scripts/check-omp-coverage.ts` compares
source registry sets, the protocol handler/descriptor table, renderer/request coverage and
acceptance records. Each source entry has an O-family, disposition/reason, authoritative
handler or presentation equivalent, applicable surfaces and a test ID. Reject missing,
duplicate, orphaned or stale-hash mappings. Compare static tool and slash aliases as well as
primary names; test runtime additions/removals with fixture custom providers, MCP tools,
extensions, skills and templates. Settings/config source changes invalidate the classification
until reviewed. SDK/service source hashes and the §2.8 semantic supplement catch capabilities
outside those registries; an unchanged count does not prove an unchanged contract.

Run owner-only configuration/maintenance tests with isolated agent directories and local
fixtures. No model/network call is part of G0. Enabled browser/native/provider/backend claims
require a separate authorized real environment receipt; unavailable-state tests establish
only their named unavailable case. Persist a release qualification manifest with source and
patch tree, UI/client revisions, capabilities, tested environments and receipts. F requires
D/W/N and all applicable O01–O12 gates; no `integration_missing` entry may remain, and no
feature can be reclassified as optional solely to turn the gate green.

## 9. Landed work ledger (append-only registry, no authority over §3-§8)

This section records what landed and when, with a receipt for each. It is a registry, not a spec:
if an entry contradicts §3, §5, §6 or §8, **those sections win** and the entry is what to fix.
Append new entries at the end; never rewrite an old one except to correct a factual error, and
say in the entry that you corrected it.

### S1a/S1b/S1c (2026-09-14) — Copilot removal

- `patches/desktop/0003` — product.json: allowlists `chatSessionsProvider`, removes
  `defaultChatAgent` (the source of the welcome/sign-in flow), removes Copilot from
  `trustedExtensionAuthAccess` and `builtInExtensionsEnabledWithAutoUpdates`. `product.json` now
  contains no Copilot references (`grep -c copilot` = 0).
- `patches/desktop/0005` — stops loading the Copilot chat session provider (desktop + web entry).
- `patches/desktop/0006` — removes all harness registrations (Copilot/Claude/Codex) plus the BYOK
  proxy, the Claude/Codex proxies, the pending-edit provider and Copilot API wiring;
  `providerConfigurations` is `[]`; `npm run typecheck-client` = 0 errors.
- `patches/desktop/0007` — removes the last production references to the Copilot harness (the
  Copilot-schema picker family, `sessionPluginBundler.ts`'s Copilot import, and the
  remote-agent-host contribution's load of the deleted picker).
- S1b deleted `node/{copilot,claude,codex}` (920 files), the whole `platform/agentHost/test` tree,
  `sessions/contrib/providers/copilotChatSessions`, and the picker families bound to
  Copilot/Claude/Codex schemas. The deletions are kept as **`removals` in
  `patches/desktop/manifest.json`** (18 entries) applied by `scripts/prepare-desktop.ts` rather
  than as a multi-megabyte deletion patch, so the list stays reviewable and every path is still
  tracked and confined to the checkout.
- **Reproduction proved**: restoring all files (102 Copilot files) → `bun scripts/prepare-desktop.ts`
  (7 patches + 18 removals) → the same Copilot-free tree, with `npm run typecheck-client` = 0 errors.
- **Correcting an earlier note**: an earlier summary said "the window is blank as expected because
  Caret's provider is not written yet". **Wrong.** The window was blank because the **workbench
  did not boot** after `patches/desktop/0003` removed `defaultChatAgent` from product.json — the
  base read it without an undefined guard in two places: `toDefaultAccountConfig()`
  (`workbench/services/accounts/browser/defaultAccount.ts`) and a module-level
  `assertDefined(product.defaultChatAgent, …)` in `welcomeOnboarding` (the latter killed the
  **entire IDE window**). Evidence: renderer exceptions captured over CDP —
  `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'chatExtensionId')` and
  `Onboarding requires a default chat agent product configuration.` The AX finding that the word
  `Copilot` was absent was therefore an artefact of nothing mounting, not proof of a clean surface.
- `patches/desktop/0009` — makes the `defaultChatAgent` readers tolerate its absence (accounts,
  extension gallery/deprecation, pack-uninstall, composer welcome copy, onboarding) without
  changing behaviour when product.json has a value.
- `patches/desktop/0008` — removes `Selection`/`Go`/`Terminal` from the Agents window's
  `src/vs/sessions/browser/parts/menubar.contribution.ts`; the IDE window keeps its menu set
  (verified in the real built bundle: `"mSelection"`/`"mGo"`/`"mTerminal"` are 0 occurrences in
  `out/vs/sessions/sessions.desktop.main.js` and still present in the workbench bundle).
- Routing: `apps/macos/src/extension.ts#openAgentsWindow` calls
  `workbench.action.openAgentsWindow` (the base's window, opened with `--agents`); it no longer
  switches mode in the existing window and no longer opens a webview.
- After rebuilding, the Agents window really mounts with title `Agents`, and its whole innerText
  contains no `Copilot`.
- **Measured twice** (same machine, same build, fresh profile, `--remote-debugging-port`):

| Window | time to first window | workbench mounted | steady RSS (sum over processes) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Agents (`--agents`) | 1.65s | 1.80s | 721MB / 7 processes |
| IDE | 1.48s | 1.61s | 642MB / 8 processes |

  Method note: RSS is the sum of every process's RSS in that profile (shared pages counted more
  than once). It compares Caret's two windows to each other, not to any other application.
- **Remaining naming debt**: 15 files whose names contain "copilot" but which are not the harness
  (`copilotCliConfig`, `copilotHome`, `copilotManagedSettings`, `copilotToolIds`, and the
  slash-command and prompt-syntax compat helpers). They register nothing and gate nothing.

### S2 provider and bridge (2026-09-14)

- `apps/macos/src/chat-sessions-map.ts` — a pure projection (uri/session item/turn plans/command)
  with 14 test cases in `apps/macos/test/chat-sessions-map.test.ts` (fixtures only, no provider).
- `apps/macos/src/chat-sessions.ts` — registers the participant + `createChatSessionItemController`
  + `registerChatSessionContentProvider`; feeds history from `state.ts`'s `applyEvent`; sends work
  with the `prompt` command through `CaretHostClient` and streams by reading events from a cursor
  (aborting when the token is cancelled). OMP still owns execution and transcript.
- `patches/desktop/0003` adds `chatParticipantPrivate` to `extensionEnabledApiProposals` and
  **`sessionsWindowAllowedExtensions: ["caret.caret"]`** — genuinely required: without the
  allow-list, an extension with `main` + contributed `views` is disabled in the Agents window
  (`extensionEnablementService#_isDisabledBySessionsWindow`).
- `patches/desktop/0010` — `contrib/providers/extensionSessions/browser/extensionSessionsProvider.contribution.ts`
  is a general bridge: it reads `IChatSessionsService.getChatSessionItems()` and exposes them as an
  `ISessionsProvider` (the model the sidebar actually uses), sending work with
  `chatService.sendRequest`, so no third-party provider is needed; registered in
  `sessions.desktop.main.ts`. Fail-safe: if items cannot be read, the provider returns an empty
  list and logs a warning — the window does not break.
- Not advertised, and therefore not shown as buttons: rename/archive/delete/fork/side-chat/
  multi-chat and the model picker (`capabilities` = false and `getModelPickerOptions` disables all)
  — these wait for the OMP binding to finish, per §5's "missing capability → disabled + reason".
- **Verified** (packaged app + `--agents` + CDP): the sidebar shows
  `treeitem: caret-ui-acceptance, 1` (grouped by the session's real workspace) and
  `treeitem: New task, updated 2 days ago, State: Completed`; clicking opens that session's chat
  view in the Agents window with a full composer and welcome — no errors in the exthost log and no
  read-only banner (the element exists in the DOM but is `display:none`).
- Receipts: [`evidence/agents-window-boot-2026-09-14/`](evidence/agents-window-boot-2026-09-14/),
  [`evidence/agents-window-sessions-2026-09-14/`](evidence/agents-window-sessions-2026-09-14/),
  [`evidence/agents-window-sessions-bridge-2026-09-14/`](evidence/agents-window-sessions-bridge-2026-09-14/).

### S2 live turn through the host API (2026-09-15)

- `smoke:omp:live` (`scripts/omp-live-turn.ts`) fires one real turn through the same path the
  Agents window uses (host HTTP API → `startSession` → command `prompt` → OMP → event stream),
  getting the exact instructed answer in 3.4s over 184 frames ending with `agent_end` and the
  command `completed`. Receipt: [`evidence/s2-live-turn-2026-09-15/`](evidence/s2-live-turn-2026-09-15/).
- **What the receipt corrected**: OMP does **not** send `prompt_result` for a real turn —
  `prompt_result` is reserved for a prompt that is scheduled but does not invoke the agent
  (`upstream/omp/docs/rpc.md` §9). The turn terminator is `agent_end` + `message_end`, and `start`
  **emits a new incarnation every time** (`service.ts:134`), so the post-start value must be used;
  the app side already did the right thing (`chat-sessions.ts:487-491`).

### S2 live turn from the on-screen composer (2026-09-16)

Receipt: [`evidence/s2-agents-window-live-turn-2026-09-16/`](evidence/s2-agents-window-live-turn-2026-09-16/).

- **Gate 1 (closed)**: `ChatServiceImpl.sendRequest` refuses every request with no *default agent*
  for its location (upstream that agent is Copilot Chat's participant, removed in S1), so every
  click ended at `sendRequest No default agent for location panel` before reaching Caret's
  provider. Fixed by making `caret.omp` the default agent: `patches/desktop/0003` adds the
  `defaultChatParticipant` proposal for `caret.caret`, `apps/macos/package.json` declares
  `isDefault: true` + `modes`, and `apps/macos/src/chat-sessions.ts` runs the real turn from the
  participant handler (`runTurn`, the same path as the session content provider). After a rebuild
  and repackage, the `No default agent contributed` symptom at boot disappeared and requests
  reached the Agents window's transcript.
- **Gates 2+3 (closed, 2026-09-16)**: Caret registers its own language model provider from OMP's
  catalogue (`apps/macos/src/omp-language-models.ts`, vendor `caret-omp`), because the extension
  host always attaches `vscode.ChatRequest.model` for a handler
  (`extHostChatAgents2.getModelForRequest`) and can only resolve models belonging to the
  requesting extension. Three things had to agree: (i) `patches/desktop/0010` declares the vendor
  descriptor (`deltaLanguageModelChatProviderDescriptors`), otherwise
  `Chat model provider uses UNKNOWN vendor caret-omp`; (ii) the picker identifier must be
  `<vendor>/<id>` to match what the extension host builds (`getVendorFromModelIdentifier`), hence
  the prefix in `0010` (sessions bridge) and `0011` (chat widget), with the extension stripping it
  before writing `set_model`; (iii) the provider must announce itself
  (`onDidChangeLanguageModelChatInformation`) because `registerLanguageModelProvider` does not
  resolve on its own — after announcing, the workbench logs
  `[LM] Resolved language models for vendor caret-omp` and the chip can pick a model.
  `patches/desktop/0003` adds the `chatProvider` proposal for `caret.caret`.
- **Result**: a prompt typed into the Agents window's composer (built and packaged from this
  revision) reached OMP and the answer flowed back into the transcript — evidence: a
  `Caret participant turn for session 94e8b9bd-…` line in the Caret channel, the session journal
  (`user: 'Reply with exactly this and nothing else: caret-ui-turn-ok-2'` /
  `assistant: 'caret-ui-turn-ok-2'`), and the transcript showing `Sent 11:23 AM` → `Completed 11:23 AM`.
- **Earlier same-day attempt** (`evidence/s2-live-turn-window-2026-09-16/`): typing worked (text
  landed in `.view-line`, Send became enabled, `.chat-submit-button` had no `disabled`) but
  **submit was never dispatched** — a CDP trusted click on the button (confirmed by a listener
  that `mousedown`/`click` reached the element), a CDP click on `.action-label`, and a real
  `Return` through Computer Use all produced no request to the extension, no new `prompt` command
  on the host, and the session still at 9, so **no provider credit was spent**. The important
  finding was that a session **with no turns** shows the base's welcome (`Build with Agent` /
  `Generate Agent Instructions`) rather than Caret's chat view; that was the real S2 blocker, not
  OMP or credentials. Chronology note: this attempt (10:18) happened *before* the 11:23 default
  participant work above. What remains true from it: **a session with 0 events still shows the
  base's welcome, not Caret's chat view.** Measurable test constraint: Monaco in this window uses
  the **EditContext API**, so `Input.insertText` and `Input.dispatchKeyEvent` cannot enter text —
  real keys through Computer Use are required, and the window must be raised to frontmost in the
  *same call* as the action (proved to work). `sky.paste` fails with a clipboard timeout when the
  app is not frontmost.

### OMP version gate (2026-09-15)

The old gate compared `omp/18.1.18` exactly in `apps/host/src/service.ts`, three smokes and
`prepare-omp-runtime.ts`. `isSupportedOmpVersion()` in `packages/omp-adapter/src/types.ts` now
accepts the baseline or a newer patch in the same minor (accepting this machine's `18.1.22`) and
rejects other minors, older versions, or non-version strings. The host still probes with the same
env it spawns with; `prepare-omp-runtime` still pins exactly because it builds the shipped
artifact. Receipt: [`evidence/s2-omp-version-gate-2026-09-15/`](evidence/s2-omp-version-gate-2026-09-15/).
