    in `CediaIdeAgentProvider.openIde`), deleting the route/view/state/rail above, and proving it
    with the agent-window suite + `vite build` plus a live receipt showing a dock file edit
    landing in the IDE window's real editor. Until then the route stays as an unadvertised
    fallback, not an offered surface (§5).

**Spec execution (opened 2026-09-22, from the §3.A/§3.B lock).** Each item closes with a receipt
from a packaged build unless stated otherwise. Items 54-64 are the work this lock adds;
items 65-67 are same-day follow-ups from the full-repository architecture review (host,
extension and patch-stack internals) and do not depend on the two-window contract.
**Execution order:** 54 + 60 first (bundle/extension files only, no desktop-patch rebuild, no
shared files) → 55 (re-cuts patches `0057`/`0058`, so it owns the §6.1 patch loop alone) → 57,
58, 59, 63 (independent of each other; 63a needs no prerequisite — the surface is already
unreachable) → 61 + 62 as one slice or coordinated (both edit `cedia-adapter.ts`) → 56 (only
after its residual-consumer check) → 64 last (its receipt rides the next `package:mac` build).
Receipts batch through packaged builds per §9 practice; coordinate on `cedia-adapter.ts` and
`patches/desktop/` the way §6.1 requires. Items 65-67 are internal-health work: 65 (host) any
time; 67 (patch stack) after 56 settles `0033`'s fate; 66 (extension split) only after 63's
deletions so dead code is not carried across.

54. **One editable application theme across both windows (re-scoped 2026-09-23).**
    Owner choices 4A/38A replace the former read-only Appearance requirement. Common Settings
    exposes one application-theme value from either window; code syntax/font remain separately
    selectable. Reuse the existing IDE/bundle token propagation where suitable, and define
    the durable value owner under item 69 before implementation. Closes when changes from
    either window persist and repaint both, syntax/font changes preserve app chrome, and
    fresh/migrated profiles pass a packaged-run check. The older read-only-theme receipt in
    §9 proves only that earlier behavior, not this revised requirement.

55. **One window-chrome policy.** Zoom, traffic lights, title-bar and menu policy move into a
    single main-process module used by both `BrowserWindow`s (patches `0057`/`0058` re-cut onto
    it), and the agent window gains the slim status bar of §3.B (host · model · session ·
    branch, `statusBar.*` tokens). Closes when both windows share zoom behaviour and menu
    source by construction (no per-window patch), and the agent status bar is measured on
    screen with live values.

56. **One model-selection state; retire the native chat surface.** After a residual-consumer
    check (quick-access/palette entries that still resolve the participant), retire the
    workbench LM-picker/provider path: `chat-sessions.ts`, the vendor cluster in
    `chat-sessions-map.ts`, `omp-language-models.ts`, and patches `0011`/`0013`/`0014`/
    `0040`-`0047` — together with the registration surfaces that exist only for it: the
    `chatSessions`/`chatParticipants` contributions and `enabledApiProposals` entries in
    `apps/macos/package.json`, and the proposal-allowlist rows in patch `0003`
    (`chatSessionsProvider`, `chatParticipantPrivate`, `defaultChatParticipant`; its
    Copilot-identity removals stay). This also **supersedes §10 items 45-49**, which were
    written against the native renderer — restate them per the §10 head rule before touching
    either side. Host `GET /v1/models` + `set_model` with the bundle picker becomes the only
    model control (§3.A). Closes when `registerCediaChatSessions` and those patches are gone,
    the suite is green, and a model change from either window is demonstrably one code path.

57. **One keybinding system.** The bundle's keybinding section reads and writes the real
    `Cedia/User/keybindings.json` through the extension bridge (today: path advertised, list
    empty, write rejected — `cedia-adapter.ts:253-258, 1471, 1516`); the bundle's private chord
    defaults sheet is deleted; `backlog/command-map.md`'s CONFLICT-GATE gains the agent-surface
    `when` vocabulary. Closes when a binding edited in the agent window appears in the workbench
    and vice versa, plus one conflict-gate run.

58. **Host git service is the single git implementation.** Build the 16 throwing methods
    (`native-git.ts:51-87`: stage/unstage/pull/stash/worktree/PR preparation/…) on the host git
    service so every Cedia git surface (right-dock Git pane, review, worktree receipts,
    Environment rows) reads one implementation; the stock SCM view stays as the §5 upstream
    feature. Closes when the pane's disabled actions run against a real repo with a receipt, and
    no Cedia surface shells out independently.

59. **One project-creation flow.** `CreateProjectDialog` / `project.create` → host
    `POST /v1/projects` (+ `/v1/workspace-suggestion`) is the only Cedia project flow; IDE entry
    points register through it when a Cedia surface needs the project; dead sources
    (`provisionFromGitHub`) stay cut. Closes when a project created in either window is visible
    in both from the same host record.

60. **Agent-window scope cut (§3.B rule).** Remove the surfaces with no OMP/host source:
    routes/nav for Kanban, Pull requests, Plugins, Studio, Spaces; Settings sections AppSnap,
    Safari vault/cookie import, Profile stats, Usage, Worktrees; the feedback POST to
    `trysynara.com` and its dialog; the Synara `whatsNew` changelog/release history; Synara
    public art (favicon / apple-touch / `synara.*`) and the ~85-file Synara copy/link sweep;
    rebuild `upstream.json` from the actual tree (it wrongly excludes `apps/server`, which the
    host imports, and the vendored test trio); make the `nativeApi.ts` and
    `deviceFrameSource.ts` WebSocket fallbacks **throw** so a missing `window.nativeApi` can
    never open a second data path. Closes when a fresh-profile run through every sidebar row and
    settings section shows no dead state, no `trysynara` egress, and `upstream.json` matches the
    tree.

61. **Composer attachments and mentions reach OMP on the bundle path.** Today
    `thread.turn.start` throws on attachments/mentions (`cedia-adapter.ts:1288-1289`) while the
    native path already proved the payload (§10 item 34): images → `prompt.images[]`, other
    references → labelled attached context; `@` refs resolve through the files bridge. Closes

62. **Skills/slash and compaction are real, not fakes.** `listCommands`/`listSkills` forward

63. **Extension consolidation (the surgery the decisions authorize).** (a) delete the
    unreachable task shell — `webview.ts`, `messages.ts`'s dead union half,
    `CediaTaskViewProvider`'s webview half, `cloneStateForWebview`, and the shell-bound tests —
    after extracting the live pieces (status bar, context keys); (b) one bridge contract module:
    channel string, envelope and kind union imported by `agent-window-main.ts`,
    `agent-ide-webview.ts` and the bundle, with `activeSession`/`openAgents` moved into the
    shared handler and the `.catch(() => undefined)` swallows removed; (c) fix the silent
    guarded-send (`#state.selectedModel` is never set in production, so refusals reach zero
    views) by feeding it from `refreshOmpState` and routing refusals to `showWarningMessage`;
    (d) restrict `agent-window-files.ts` `projects.writeFile` to the editor-bridge guards so no
    surface writes workspace files outside OMP turns and the guarded editor bridge. Closes when
    `rg "webview.ts|TASK_WEBVIEW_CSS"` has no users, a test asserts the two bridge-kind sets are
    equal, an inline-edit refusal is visible on screen, and the suites are green.

64. ~~**Terminal convergence receipt (decision 8's terminal pair).**~~ **Re-scoped and closed
    2026-09-23, on the owner's decision.** The item's premise was measured false in a packaged
    build: the IDE dock renders **no right dock at all** (`[data-right-dock-content]` count 0 —
    the embedded runtime gates it off by construction), its terminal transport is refused by the
    IDE bridge (which wires the `panel` surfaces `files` and `git` only, while the same frame's
    `git.status` answers, so the refusal is the terminal surface's), and neither window's Terminal
    pane uses the host PTY registry: the Agents window's pane is a per-window node-pty shell in
    the Electron main service, and the registry holds OMP's `cedia_terminal_*` checkpoints for
    iOS. So §3 decision 6's "one host PTY registry rendered by the bundle in both windows" is not
    what the build does, and the re-scope is what it is: **one PTY per window** — the
    Electron-main shell in the Agents window, the workbench's own terminal in the IDE (the user's
    private dev shell, §5) — with the host registry serving OMP's agent terminals to iOS. The
    receipt is `docs/maintenance/CEDIA-PLAN.md` §9's "Packaged receipts" entry and
    `dist/agent-ide-smoke/dock-terminal-receipt.json`, which the IDE smoke still writes on every
    run: it attempts the real transport and polls `GET /v1/sessions/:id/terminals`, so it flips
    from `resolved: false` to `true` on the day a convergence lands. If that convergence is ever
    wanted, it needs a new item, not this one.

65. **Host hardening (architecture review 2026-09-22).** Five measured gaps in `apps/host`:
    (a) the events table is **unbounded** while `store.ts:1-8` documents a "bounded journal" —
    fork hydration journals full `get_messages` payloads, so the projection drifts toward a
    second transcript on disk; add per-session retention (byte/count cap with a
    `historyTruncated` marker like `TerminalCheckpoint`) or stop journaling hydration frames,
    and fix the doc either way; (b) `service.ts` (~709 lines) owns ~10 concerns — split along
    its existing seams (OMP lifecycle / command dispatch + idempotency / permission + editor
    bridges / fork-sidechat-terminals) behind a thin `CediaHost` façade so router and tests do
    not churn; (c) wire shapes are re-declared in four places (`relay/protocol.ts` `Json` vs
    `protocol`, adapter-local `CediaProject/Session/Event/Command`, the extension-UI request
    union in four partial views) — converge on `packages/protocol` so approval shapes cannot
    drift silently; (d) the security perimeter is untested — add Origin-reject / Host-header /
    413 tests to `http.test.ts`, a host-level `remote.ts` test (pair offer, disable stops
    relay, identity file 0600), and a router test for `workspace-suggestion`; (e) iOS reads
    models/providers through the per-session command envelope while agent window and CLI use
    sessionless `/v1/models` + `/v1/providers` — converge iOS on the sessionless routes or
    record why a phone must start a session. Closes when: the journal cap lands with the doc
    matching, each shape has exactly one definition in `packages/protocol` with importers, the
    five new perimeter tests are green, and iOS lists models without a started session (or the
    deviation is recorded in §5).

66. **Split `extension.ts` (4,234 lines), only after item 63's deletions.** Extract
    host-connection/polling, the ~45 command registrations, the editor-surface
    (marks/lenses/actions), review-surface, selection-send and status/context-key code into
    modules; `activate()` becomes a ~100-line wiring root. Running it before 63 would carry the
    dead webview half across. Closes when `apps/macos/src/extension.ts` is ≤800 lines of
    wiring, the command registry is enumerable from one module, `bun run test
    apps/macos/test` + `bun run typecheck` are green, and no behavior changes (the suite is the
    receipt).

68. **The per-message revert control is disabled for ~90s after every turn.** The control now
    renders for any user message the adapter can rewind to (§10 item 1b), but in the packaged
    window it stays `disabled` for at least 90,052 ms after a send — measured 2026-09-23 while
    driving the rewind step — with the thread idle (`session.status: "ready"`, `activeTurnId:
    null`, `latestTurn.state: "completed"`), so a user who clicks it gets a dead button. The
    vendor expression is `disabled={isRevertingCheckpoint || isWorking}`; the state that keeps
    `isWorking` true through the local-dispatch marker has not been attributed, and it reproduces
    on the pre-change bundle, so it predates item 1b. Closes when the control is clickable as soon
    as the task is idle, with a packaged step that clicks it (the §10 item 1b receipt's step
    already fails over to the command the control dispatches; it should be able to use the click
    path once this is fixed).

67. **Patch-stack hygiene.** (a) Retire the three patches the README itself calls inert
    (`0019`, `0027`, `0028`) — manifest + digest work only; (b) rewrite

| [`o06-packaged-reload-boundary-2026-10-01`](evidence/o06-packaged-reload-boundary-2026-10-01/findings.md) | O06 boundary probe: the staged lock overlay loads fixture generation A through the packaged owner, but a rewritten lock does not swap on `/reload-plugins` (completes, catalog unchanged); direct `--trusted-extension` swaps fine on the same binary, and settings/discovery placements do not load for hosted sessions — packaged reload has no swappable extension path today. Specifies the implementation requirement; dev-runtime reload requalified green on 18.4.3. |
| [`o06-acp-reload-live-2026-10-01`](evidence/o06-acp-reload-live-2026-10-01/findings.md) | O06 live ACP slice on the standalone 18.4.3 binary: fixture A→B atomic swap, parking refusal with a second live session plus close→proceed recovery, throwing-candidate rollback, removal with builtins surviving — all over the ACP wire in one process, zero inference. Qualifies the ACP reload path and the shared-registry boundary live. |
| [`o06-interactive-reload-live-2026-10-01`](evidence/o06-interactive-reload-live-2026-10-01/findings.md) | O06 live interactive slice: real SDK session + InteractiveMode on VirtualTerminal, composer-driven A→B atomic swap, real-builtin candidate rollback, composer-driven removal, usage cost 0, per-phase terminal captures. Qualifies the interactive reload path live. |
| [`o06-tan-reload-live-2026-10-01`](evidence/o06-tan-reload-live-2026-10-01/findings.md) | O06 live shared-registry slice: mid-inference tan (request-observed hanging loopback) makes parent reload refuse with the exact parking error, catalog unchanged, zero spend — then a product hang: post-park `/reload-plugins` never settles while sibling commands complete. Filed as suspected bug with deterministic repro; recovery stays open. |
| [`d-packaged-panels-sweep-2026-10-01`](evidence/d-packaged-panels-sweep-2026-10-01/findings.md) | D panels sweep on a staged app with a held loopback turn plus queued follow-up: runner journal-verified running+queued at each gate; operator-recorded composer/queue/Environment/model-picker/Settings rows with per-gate window PNGs, zero spend. Closes the packaged panel-capture remainder; armed-state panels and History/Tree-with-content stay open. |
    Remaining qualification: another live session sharing the model registry still blocks reload;
    standalone interactive/ACP concurrent reload and packaged capture remain open, as do the other
    O06/F and D acceptance rows.

    Execution history (began 2026-09-24, thirty-fourth slice): a host lifetime
    handshake (`ready`/`quitting`/`stopped`, owner-only quit ACK, mutation admission fence,
    durable `stopped` receipt), an Electron-free main-process quit coordinator
    (`apps/macos/src/app-lifecycle.ts`), the `capabilities`/`lifecycle` application routes, a
    shared Mac draft owner with revision CAS, idempotent submission claims, matched clear and
    idempotent import (schema v3), and the earlier capability snapshot plus allowlisted CEDIA
    preference owner are implemented and verified with isolated fixtures. The Code-OSS
    application lifecycle is wired as well: patch `0060` joins the host shutdown into
    `lifecycleMainService.onWillShutdown`, registers the packaged login item, and keeps a
    packaged macOS login launch in the background; patch `0061` repairs the `TS7030` return
    paths `0059` introduced; and `scripts/prepare-desktop.ts` can prove an overlapping patch
    set is already applied. The whole Code-OSS client typechecks with 0 errors and the patch
    set reproduces the checkout. The host draft owner is now in the runtime path too: the
    record carries the windows' shared payload (`content`, bounded and opaque to the host),
    the main process translates the renderer's read/write into revision-checked draft routes
    with a one-time import of the pre-host file and an offline cache, and the shared bundle
    sends and adopts the host revision, keeps the local text on a real conflict and exposes
    the choose-a-side action. Both windows no longer write the local draft file as an
    authority; it is only the offline cache and the one-time import source. Both windows also
    learn a committed revision from each other: the main process publishes the revision the host
    accepted on `vscode:cedia-draft-updated` to every window but the sender, a window with nothing
    pending adopts it, a window that typed since its last confirmed revision keeps its text and
    gets the choose-a-side conflict, and a Send's authoritative clear publishes `delivered` so the
    other window starts writing the next draft from the restarted revision. Cedia's own
    preference subset has one owner as well (§6.4): the `settings` root is reachable from the
    renderer, `uiSettings` reads and writes the host record with compare-and-swap revisions and
    answers a conflict with the record that won instead of an anonymous failure, a committed record
    is published to the other window on `vscode:cedia-settings-updated`, and both ends use the renderer's
    own words for the values a control can produce. The capability
    snapshot is consumed as well: the host advertises `app.automations`, both windows read the
    snapshot through a Cedia-owned native namespace, a parsed snapshot gated by
    `capabilityGate` removes an integration-missing row from the sidebar while an unknown
    snapshot changes nothing, and Settings gained a Capability status destination listing
    available / needs-setup / not-implemented rows with the host's reason. A real Send now uses
    the shared draft owner as well: the adapter reserves the exact revision it is about to
    dispatch, dispatches with the command the host bound to that revision (so two windows
    sending one revision produce one turn), stops on a revision already sent with different
    text, and releases the draft after acceptance only while its revision still matches. R3 has
    started: the host identifies the folder a new task starts in (branch and commit, read-only
    and offline), admits one file-mutating task per folder (a concurrent Git task is refused
    with `worktree_required`, a non-repository folder with `shared_folder_busy`), records that
    identity for both modes, stamps it on every session row and answers creation with it. A
    sidechat fork is exempt from that rule because it continues the same task's folder. Archive
    and Continue are now a host-owned pair: archiving a stopped task records what it kept in
    `archive-receipt.json` (retained worktree, branch, stopped-on commit, dirty and ignored
    facts, and why nothing was removed) and leaves an immutable `refs/cedia/archive/<task>/…`
    for a managed worktree, while `PATCH {archived:false}` runs a real
    restore that resumes a surviving worktree at once and otherwise checks the archived
    revision out again - reattaching the task branch only when it still points at that commit
    and no worktree holds it, and continuing on `cedia/restore/<task>` without moving a branch
    the user changed. Occupied paths, an unavailable repository and an archive with no recorded
    revision refuse with an actionable reason and leave the task archived. Cleanup itself stays
    inactive: nothing removes a worktree, branch or file.
    A new task can also name the revision it starts from: the host resolves that label to a
    commit in the project repository before creating anything, refuses an unknown revision
    (`unknown_base_ref`) or a base revision for a task that would work in the project folder
    (`worktree_required`), gives the resolved commit to the worktree and records it as the
    task's `sourceCommit` beside the requested `baseRef`.
    R3's turn contract has started: every `prompt`/`abort_and_prompt`/`follow_up` claims a
    durable turn intent (store schema 4) linked to its command, device, incarnation and payload
    hash before dispatch, and the session row carries a bounded projection of those intents
    that only ever says what OMP proved - `prepared`, `queued` on transport acknowledgment,
    `running` from OMP's turn boundaries, `completed` with the event sequence it was read from,
    `failed`, `cancelled` on Stop - or names the gap: `needs_continue` for a turn that never
    reached OMP, `outcome_unknown` when the OMP owner vanished while the turn was dispatched.
    Recovery pauses or marks those as unknown rather than replaying them, `steer` claims no
    intent because it is input to the turn already running, and a schema-2/v1 state directory is
    migrated with a private version-named backup beside the journal.
    The OMP half of that contract is in the pinned patch as well: `prompt`, `steer`,
    `follow_up` and `abort_and_prompt` take an optional `cediaIntentId`, the session adopts the
    submission that actually starts a run (steering before follow-ups, as OMP drains them) and
    names it on `agent_start`, `turn_start`, `turn_end` and `agent_end`, and the new
    `cedia_turn_queue` operation answers which submission is running and which are waiting, in
    OMP's own order. The bridge is advertised in the ready frame and stays inert unless a client
    names a turn, so stock behavior is unchanged. `bun run smoke:omp:turn-bridge` proves all
    three against the pinned runtime with a local endpoint that never answers - no provider
    request leaves the machine - and the host now binds a named boundary to that exact intent and
    stores the queue position OMP reports. The runtime also reports the model and thinking level
    a running turn is actually using - in its queue snapshot and on its turn boundaries - and the
    host records them on that submission's turn intent (`model` as `provider/id`, schema 5), so a
    result is attributed to the model that produced it rather than to whatever the session's
    current model has become since. Pending model/effort acceptance is in the same patch: a
    revision handed to OMP is validated there, held, and committed at OMP's own dequeue/start
    boundary (the session's awaited `beforeQueuedMessageDequeue` hook and the top of `prompt()`,
    because the agent does not await its listeners), never to a turn already running. The task
    view carries the record - `awaiting` until the runtime reports committing that exact
    revision, then `in-effect` with the model OMP names, or `refused` with its reason. The same
    projection is what lets a surface say how much work OMP holds: the session's bounded turn list
    now reaches the window (the adapter projects only `turnIntentId`, `state`, `queuePosition`,
    `model` and `reason` - never the device identity or the payload hash), and Cedia's status bar
    draws `running <model> · N waiting`, or names an unproven outcome (`outcome unknown · reason`,
    `needs continue`) instead of letting the counts read as progress. The desktop
    path uses it too: a model picked while the task's turn projection shows a running turn goes to
    the pending route with a monotonic revision instead of the immediate `set_model`, a model sent
    with a plain turn still applies to that turn, and a runtime without the boundary is applied at
    once and reported as `via: "immediate"` rather than imitated. The baseline is packaged again:
    `package:mac` built `VSCode-darwin-arm64/Cedia.app` and `check:packaged` passed every check, the
    packaged Agents window and IDE window (with the AGENT dock) rendered, `Open in IDE` opened the
    second window, and a deliberate Quit stopped the host and left a durable `stopped` receipt. That
    run has since been repeated at the current 18-patch set (`086d31f75d36`) with the whole working
    tree included: the Agents window renders, a density change made in the packaged window is
    persisted by the host (`app-preferences.json` revision 1 and a live `/v1/settings` readback), and
    a deliberate Quit left the application quit for the full 30 s measurement with the host gone. The
    first packaged run also found and fixed a real defect:
    the quit path's read-only host probe returned "no host" whenever the main process had not yet
    talked to the host itself, so a Quit left the host and its OMP child running while the app
    reported `host shutdown: idle`; the probe now reads the descriptor read-only instead of
    treating an empty in-process cache as absence.
    R3 also owns the appearance the owner asked for. A new task offers the revision it starts
    from: the composer lists the project's real local branches through the existing git bridge,
    prefers the branch Git reports as the primary one, and sends the choice as `baseRef` on
    `thread.create` only for a first worktree send, so a local-folder task never carries a base
    revision the host would refuse. A non-Git task that works in the original folder shows that
    it shares that folder, and a second task in a busy Git project defaults to its own worktree
    with the reason shown; the host stays authoritative and its `worktree_required` /
    `shared_folder_busy` / `unknown_base_ref` refusals are rendered as actionable states instead
    of an empty success. The pre-quit decision is real now: patch `0062` adds
    `ILifecycleMainService.registerQuitDecider`, which runs inside `before-quit` before
    `_quitRequested` is set and before `onBeforeShutdown` fires, so `Cancel` is a cancel and not a
    half-recorded quit. The main process registers Cedia's decider, asks through a real macOS
    dialog that names the running tasks, paired-device loss and any unsaved files it can count,
    treats an unanswered dialog as a refusal rather than as consent, and reports a stop the host
    did not confirm instead of calling it a successful Quit. A packaged run found and fixed a
    real defect in that path: a cancelled quit left the base's `pendingQuitPromise` pending, so
    every later Quit returned without ever reaching the decider - no dialog, no log, no way to
    quit - and the cancel branch now resolves that promise with a veto. Packaged verification of the
    same slice found two more: the Cedia event channels were named outside the `vscode:` namespace
    the Code-OSS preload delivers, so the packaged AI window reported `Unsupported event IPC channel
    'cedia:draft-updated'` and started with no UI at all; and the settings owner was asked for
    `/v1/v1/settings` (the client resolves relative paths against `/v1/`), so a preference change
    from a window reached nothing. Both are fixed, and the second now has a fixture that runs the
    real handler against a real host. §2.6's cleanup protocol
    is implemented and stays inactive: durable workspace/cleanup metadata on the task row with an
    idempotent v6 migration, the states `retained → archive_requested → prepared → removed`,
    ordered gates (no active turn, Git ancestry proof against the recorded target commit,
    tracked/untracked/ignored data, terminals, IDE work, and an immediate recheck), an immutable
    `refs/cedia/archive/<task>/<generation>` created with an all-zero old value and verified
    before a `git worktree remove` without `--force`, a durable `prepared` receipt, crash
    reconciliation that revalidates an existing worktree instead of deleting it because state
    says `prepared`, retained orphan refs, and a typed capability that reports why removal is
    off. Nothing deletes a branch, cleans unknown files or removes a directory recursively, and
    no caller invokes cleanup automatically.
    §8.2's shared wire contract (O04/R1) exists now. The pinned runtime advertises
    `cediaCapabilitiesVersion: 1` and answers `cedia_get_capabilities` with its own descriptor
    table plus the `capabilityRevision` that identifies exactly that table, and `cedia_control`
    with one registered operation at a time. The registration table is static, so a payload can
    never name JavaScript, an executable or source text: an unknown operation is refused before
    any handler runs and each registered operation validates its own payload - in this generation
    every one of them is a read that takes none. A caller that names a revision other than the
    live one is refused rather than silently refreshed, and the table cannot advertise an
    operation that has no handler because the ready check and the dispatcher read one list.
    Registered and available: `capabilities.get`, `model.roles.get`, `auth.providers.list`,
    `turn.queue.get`, `settings.keys.list` and `settings.get`, each delegating to the same
    projection the direct `cedia_*` command already answers with. The two settings operations make
    O04's config half real: the inventory is OMP's own `SETTINGS_SCHEMA` (path, type, credential
    marker, UI presence and tab, and whether a path is project-writable - only `modelRoles` is),
    it carries no values, and a live comparison proves it equals all 498 paths in the dated audit.
    `settings.set` is the write half: it validates the path and the value against OMP's own schema,
    applies the write through OMP's own setter, flushes it and answers the readback, with a
    `settingsRevision` that a caller may name so a configuration that moved underneath it (including
    through OMP's own `config` CLI) is a typed refusal rather than a silent overwrite. Cedia's policy
    is separated from that mechanism: every one of the 498 schema paths carries a disposition -
    `editable` (366), `protected` for a credential path (8), `advanced` with no settings row of its
    own (115) or `excluded` by the plan's provider endpoint/order/enabled rule (9) - each with its
    reason, and `PATCH /v1/omp/settings` writes only an `editable` path. `settings.get` answers one
    effective value with `configured` saying whether a layer set it,
    redacts a credential path the way native `omp config` listing does, refuses a path the schema
    does not define before any handler runs, and refuses a value too large for a client instead of
    truncating it. The readback was verified to survive a real edit through the runtime's own
    `config set` CLI and two restarts in the same agent directory. Declared with their reasons but not registered: `model.roles.set`,
    `auth.api-key.set`, `auth.logout` and `model.pending`, which the direct commands still own.
    The host reads the table from a live runtime only - never by starting one - validates it
    strictly, merges it into the R1 aggregate as the one capability store, and answers the
    owner-only route with the runtime rows beside the pre-existing ones plus an `omp` state that
    carries the revision or the reason it is absent. `expectRevision` makes a table that moved
    underneath a caller a typed 409 rather than a silent refresh.
    They do not complete
    R1/R2, any D/W/N/F checkpoint, the packaged login cycle and background launch (the app is now
    built, checked, rendered and quit for real; its login item has still not been exercised),
    an observed conflict choice in a running window (the pushed revision, the conflict object
    and its resolution are fixture-tested; the on-screen control is not),
    a packaged or on-screen two-window run of the Send-versus-edit race (live two-adapter proof with a real host and runtime in [live two-renderer Send-vs-edit race](evidence/r2-send-race-live-2026-09-26/findings.md)), a packaged observation of the base-branch picker
    (the host now takes a `dirtyFiles` selection, carries exactly those paths, reports a per-path
    outcome including conflicts, and refuses a malformed list before creating anything - the
    composer UI that would offer the picker, and a packaged run of either, are still open), a
    observed-in-a-run
    §2.4 "awaiting OMP" surface (the adapter now carries the record, the store keeps and repaints
    on it, and Cedia's status bar draws awaiting / in effect / refused; no running window was
    captured), the rendered Restore surface for §2.6 (the host pair works, but
    the retention decision is now on the row that restores it - what was kept and where Continue
    resumed - but no packaged run of it was observed), an observed two-window run of the shared settings record
    (the host owns the subset, both windows project it, a committed record is published to the
    other window and a conflict re-sends once on the new revision - all fixture-tested, with no
    two windows open together), capability gating
    beyond the sidebar (settings sections, palette, search, other deep links, snapshot
    invalidation), or a packaged run of the rendered OMP settings surface. One
    renderer-boundary defect found while verifying that surface is fixed: Electron's IPC carries
    only an error's message (measured on the pinned 42.10.0), so every typed host refusal used to
    arrive with no `code` and a `Error invoking remote method '...'` message; the main process now
    tags the code into the message and the adapter restores it on one funnel, which is what makes
    the settings conflict branch and the new-task refusal copy reachable at all. The following
    O04 runtime and source work is now complete: `model.roles.set`, `auth.api-key.set`,
    `auth.logout` and `model.pending` are registered with their own payload rules; all 498 schema
    paths carry a CEDIA disposition and reason; the owner-only keys/value reads and revision-checked
    `/v1/omp/settings` write use OMP's runtime; enum values come from the validating schema; and
    each key carries source-derived apply timing. A current host-backed smoke verifies the live
    inventory, all 498 timings (`immediate` 13, `turn_boundary` 416, `new_session` 39, `reload` 30),
    an isolated editable write/readback, and stale-revision, protected-key and invalid-value
    refusals. Receipts: [registered owner writes](evidence/o03-owner-writes-registered-2026-09-24/findings.md)
    and [per-key settings timing](evidence/o04-settings-apply-classification-2026-09-24/findings.md).
    The settings introduction now accurately says apply timing is shown per row from the live
    inventory; stale copy claiming timing was unclassified is removed. The settings logic suite
    covers each published timing and the unclassified fallback.
    The packaged settings surface, an observed conflict choice in a running window, and the
    two-window settings record remain open under D/F acceptance.
    The coverage gate compares the dated audit, the adapter/host/runtime implementation side and
    a live prepared runtime. The current integrity run reports 1,041 audited records, 1,041 Cedia
    mappings, no orphan, duplicate, invalid family or stale audit source, and a live `omp/18.1.18`
    whose 498 settings paths, 50 RPC commands and 73 capability descriptors agree with the audit.
    `--require-complete`, F's source-disposition condition, passes with zero audited records lacking
    an available disposition (verified 2026-09-28). This is source coverage, not full F acceptance:
    semantic, dynamic, platform and packaged/runtime evidence remain governed by §8. The gate settles
    an SDK entry only through a source-verified link - the audited RPC command that carries the same
    operation, plus a check that Cedia's adapter still sends it - so the eight entries settled this
    way cannot become a memory of a call site that no longer exists; breaking the table makes the
    run fail (verified).
    O08 owner discovery exists for CEDIA-hosted RPC runtimes: when the host starts a runtime it
    passes `CEDIA_RPC_OWNER_BRIDGE=1`, and the patched runtime publishes a mode-0600 `owner.json`
    beside its exclusive session lock plus a mode-0600 Unix socket. The record names the session,
    incarnation, pid, bridge-issued `ownerStartedAt` marker, `processStartIdentity` and a token;
    `ownerStartedAt` remains a bridge marker, while protocol v3's process identity comes from the
    macOS process table. The host independently reads `/bin/ps -p PID -o lstart=` with fixed C locale
    and UTC, compares that value before opening the socket, then verifies it through authenticated
    identify/status replies and the controller claim. The timestamp has one-second precision, so it
    supplements but does not replace socket-token authentication or prove the same-second PID-reuse
    case. Versioned token-authenticated `identify` and `status` replies prove the live owner; the
    host returns `absent`, `attached`, `stale` or `conflict` and
    never starts, stops or deletes anything to resolve ambiguity. `GET /v1/sessions/:id/owner` is
    owner-only. Protocol v2 added a bounded persistent local read client with request-ID correlation;
    only `get_state` and `get_available_commands` are allowed. Both enter the existing
    `RpcInputDispatcher` queue and their internal replies are correlated back to that socket. The
    state response is projected to session/model/turn flags and counts plus the credit-guard state
    as a fact; the command catalog is size-bounded and marks truncation. Transcript text, paths,
    system prompt and tool schemas remain unavailable through this read client. A missing credit
    guard does not block these safe reads and never
    enables mutation. The existing owner-only `GET /v1/sessions/:id/owner/summary` remains a summary projection. The
    fixture proves concurrent out-of-order replies, guard-off inspection, unsupported-method
    refusal, socket detach and that the same owner process and primary RPC client remain live.
    The next O08 slice adds authenticated exclusive control for a CEDIA-started RPC owner after its
    primary stdin closes. OMP keeps the same session and lock, routes controller commands through
    its existing dispatcher, correlates replies and events, and assigns extension UI, host-tool and
    host-URI requests to the current lease. A stale lease cannot approve a successor's request.
    The host probes before session rotation or spawn, adopts a qualified owner without creating an
    executor, and refuses ambiguous, stale or conflicting records. The CEDIA task ID is the owner
    record's discovery identity, while OMP retains its separate transcript session ID; a live host
    reattachment test caught and fixed the initial ID mismatch. Host close detaches; explicit
    stop/delete of an attached owner is refused. Explicit spawned-owner close uses an acknowledged
    primary-only shutdown frame before EOF so the endpoint and session are disposed. Live EOF,
    sequential attach, approval fencing, shutdown/lock reuse and host/adapter suites passed on the
    attested development runtime ([controller receipt](evidence/o08-owner-controller-2026-09-28/findings.md)).
    Direct external SIGTERM initially left a stale record during development; the host refused
    it rather than spawning. This controller source slice did not prove full host-crash recovery during a turn,
    editor/native permission or host-tool/URI delivery, atomic migration, independent CLI/TUI
    discovery, application owner selection, OS process-birth identity or packaged acceptance.
    A subsequent [known-owner selection slice](evidence/o08-known-owner-selection-2026-09-28/findings.md)
    exposes an owner-only, bounded `GET /v1/owners` over persisted CEDIA tasks and a Task controls
    picker. It shows attached, absent, stale and conflict states, and explicitly attaches a selected
    live task through the host's re-probed start path before navigation. Route, adapter and renderer
    fixtures pass. This does not discover independent CLI/TUI owners or qualify packaged selection;
    O08/F remains open.
    The subsequent [SIGTERM cleanup slice](evidence/o08-owner-sigterm-cleanup-2026-09-28/findings.md)
    registered the owner bridge with OMP's shared postmortem exit path before endpoint publication
    and made the token-guarded record publication synchronous. A real child-process test and twelve
    immediate-signal repetitions removed record and socket; the refreshed source-attested runtime
    passed the host owner suite. SIGKILL/hard crashes still leave refusal evidence, and full
    crash/permission recovery, OS process-birth identity and packaged qualification remain open.
    The [qualified TUI presence slice](evidence/o08-qualified-tui-presence-2026-09-28/findings.md)
    now binds an explicit `cedia-omp --cedia-session-dir` interactive launch to a private
    host-authored task context, its transcript, the credit guard and an exclusive lifetime lock.
    The same TUI process publishes authenticated `inspect_only` identity and status; controller
    claims fail. The host listing distinguishes it and refuses adoption before spawning. A
    provider-free PTY proof exercised publication, status, refusal and SIGINT cleanup on the
    refreshed source-attested development runtime. This is presence, not interactive control.
    The qualified CLI launcher exists now: `cedia-omp`
    (`scripts/cedia-omp.ts`, decision in `apps/host/src/cli-launcher.ts`) resolves the pinned
    runtime, consults the owner endpoint for the session a caller names, brokers the one verb it
    can answer from a live owner, refuses any flag that would start or attach a second executor,
    and refuses a stale or unprovable record by name instead of adopting or deleting it. It ships
    inside the packaged runtime beside the pinned binary, and the coverage gate maps every audited
    CLI verb and launch flag to it as the handler. Full O08 remains open: owners started outside
    a CEDIA task's qualified path,
    full interactive lifecycle and owner migration, same-second process-start identity qualification,
    end-to-end permission/side-channel recovery, and discovery of uninstrumented CLI/TUI owners.
    An owner Cedia did not start remains inspect-only and requires the §2.2 verified
    exclusive-handoff path before continuation; only after the CEDIA policy guard is attested
    may it become controller-capable. Qualified TUI presence now exists for an existing CEDIA
    task; direct uninstrumented `omp` remains external.
    TUI control must wait for an OMP-owned command dispatcher shared with the interactive mode;
    the RPC dispatcher cannot be reused by spawning a second executor or automating terminal keys.
    The v3 process-start slice ([receipt](evidence/o08-process-start-identity-2026-09-28/findings.md))
    proves the host/OMP exchange on the current Darwin deployment command, with one-second
    granularity and authenticated socket confirmation. Same-second PID reuse and packaged owner
    qualification remain open; do not claim general PID-reuse resolution from the timestamp alone.
    The verbs the
    plan requires to be qualified on their own (a credential broker or forward proxy, an external
    browser relay, room join/share, remote host setup, OMP's own updater and local model downloads)
    remain `integration_missing` with their reasons rather than being claimed by shipping a path.
    The coverage checker now speaks this plan's vocabulary rather than a private pair of words:
    a record is `integrated`, `dependency unavailable`, `platform presentation equivalent`,
    `owner-only` or `explicitly excluded`, and only `integration_missing` (or no mapping at all)
    blocks F. That change exposed a mistake worth recording: treating the 51 `tuiOnly` slash records
    as out-of-scope pending an owner decision was wrong. §2.2 already settles this - TUI rendering
    and developer fixtures have CEDIA equivalents or CLI-only roles, and `tuiOnly` says nothing
    about the operation behind the command. The family is now classified per operation: settings,
    setup/providers, login, logout, hotkeys, new, resume, branch/rewind, fork and exit/quit map to
    Cedia surfaces that exist today (the settings destination, /v1/providers, the keybinding owner,
    draft creation, the archive/restore Continue pair, conversation rewind, sidechat, and the §2.7
    quit), `debug` is a developer fixture this plan keeps out of the application surface, and the
    rest stay `integration_missing` with the packet that owes them named. That pass also corrected
    three commands a first reading had called novelty - `btw`, `tan` and `omfg` are an ephemeral
    side question, a background agent and a rule-forging operation - and put them back in scope.
    The slash family is decided by the audit's own `surfaces`/`tuiOnly` metadata rather than by a
    reason string: the 143 records upstream marks reachable over `rpc` or `acp` are available
    through Cedia's prompt path, and the 51 it limits to OMP's terminal UI stay gaps, because
    Cedia runs the runtime headless and has no TUI to dispatch them from. `smoke:omp:slash` proves
    the runtime half against the pinned runtime - a builtin command, its subcommands and its
    aliases answer with `agentInvoked: false` once the virtual terminal is negotiated, which is
    what the host does before a session accepts a prompt, and not one model request is made - and
    `apps/macos/agent-window/test/omp-slash-dispatch.test.ts` proves the Cedia half, that the
    command text reaches the session exactly as typed.
    O04's timing half is a mechanism now rather than one open question: each settings key can
    carry an `apply` timing, the runtime publishes `immediate` for exactly the paths whose change
    runs one of its own declared hooks (13 keys, its own hook table as the evidence), the protocol
    refuses a timing outside the four-word vocabulary, and the gate decides each settings record
    from what that runtime actually published - so the 498 settings are no longer one all-or-nothing
    claim. The remaining 485 stay gaps, each saying Cedia has not classified when a change to that
    key takes effect.
    The gate's dispositions now come from Cedia's own declarative registries instead of literals
    it kept beside them: `UI_METHOD_CLASSES` classifies all eleven extension-UI methods next to the
    parsers that carry them, and every audited built-in, hidden and alias tool travels the one
    tool-card path, so those 38 records advanced with a fixture per name rather than a reason
    string. The six dynamic tools stay open on purpose, each naming the packet that has to register
    it (browser/computer O10, MCP/extension O06, image/speech provider surfaces). Every coverage row
    can now carry the test that proves it.
    Every one of the 28 session-event kinds the dated audit extracted is classified by name in
    `CEDIA_SESSION_EVENT_KINDS`, the three payload-free recognition gaps among them, and the gate
    reads that registry instead of the audit's dated gap list - so a kind Cedia has not classified
    is reported, and the event kind now has no gaps at all. That route is split now: `POST /v1/lifecycle/fence` closes
    admission without stopping, `POST /v1/lifecycle/resume` reopens it, and `POST
    /v1/lifecycle/quit` keeps the fence-and-stop meaning, so the owner's Stop-and-quit / Cancel
    decision happens with new local and remote work already refused and a Cancel really reopens
    admission. Active user terminals and unsaved IDE buffers do not yet
    contribute to the prompt: the lifecycle status carries running tasks, not a terminal count,
    and no main-process probe for dirty editors exists (Code-OSS still owns the Save / Don't
    Save / Cancel dialog during the window close that follows a confirmed quit). Cleanup is
    implemented but deliberately not enabled, and it has no automatic caller.

    those receipts also record the defects found by a runtime fixture and by the real Code-OSS
    typecheck, plus the pre-existing failures at revision `0676dd70d54`.
    R6's serving half is real now: `bun run --cwd apps/ios export:web` produces the existing
    Expo export, the CEDIA build stages it to `dist/remote-web` and, for a packaged build, to
    `runtime/remote-web` beside the bundled host, and the bundled host resolves that directory at
    startup and starts the loopback gateway with the owner's configured tailnet names. A build
    without the export fails by name rather than serving an empty document, and
    `verify-packaged-cedia` refuses a packaged app with no client. Running the gateway against the
    real export served the document, its 1.4 MB bundle with the right content type, an SPA deep
    link, structured API errors and a `host_forbidden` refusal for an unconfigured `Host`. The
    client half of R6 - the versioned transport discriminator, same-origin cookie/CSRF pairing in
    the web runtime and the native tailnet token - is not done; neither is any Tailscale Serve
    configuration, tailnet device or off-LAN receipt, so W is not closeable from this slice.
    The capability boundary now reaches past the sidebar row. Two rows had gone stale against
    Cedia's own code - `omp.settings` claimed its bridge was "not installed yet" while the
    inventory, disposition, revision-checked write and rendered destination all exist, and
    `remote.tailscale` claimed the gateway was "not integrated" while it is implemented - and both
    now report the state that is really missing (`dependency_unavailable`: no OMP runtime running,
    or no packaged web client) rather than `integration_missing`, which is the only state that
    removes a row from the working UI. `remote.tailscale` is decided by the process that started
    the gateway. The settings destination list, the settings search and the `?section=` deep link
    all consume the same rule, a destination whose capability only needs setup stays visible with
    its reason, and the snapshot is re-read on focus after a 30 s staleness window instead of
    being frozen for the window's life. No packaged window was captured rendering the gated list,
    and `omp.live-cli-attach` and `app.automations` stay `integration_missing` on purpose.
    O04's timing half is closed. Every one of the runtime's 498 schema paths now carries when a
    change takes effect, derived from three tables that fail loudly if they drift: a reader index of
    every literal settings read in OMP's own sources plus one explicit no-reader bucket, an authored
    lifecycle rule per source module with its rationale, and OMP's own hook table as the definition
    of `immediate`. The measured answer on the prepared pinned runtime is 0 paths without a timing,
    with `immediate` 13, `turn_boundary` 416, `new_session` 39 and `reload` 30. A new upstream key
    breaks the completeness assertion instead of publishing an unclassified timing, and the adapter
    assertion that used to require every timed key to be `immediate` now pins the vocabulary and the
    hook set. The panel renders the timing; no packaged window was captured showing a non-immediate
    one. The client half of R6 is implemented too: the gateway serves a browser as a cookie/CSRF
    principal and a native client as a Bearer principal, refuses the native JSON answer to anything
    carrying a browser `Origin`, refuses the Mac's owner credential on both paths, and `apps/ios`
    pairs through the selected Tailscale endpoint with a legacy Paseo offer preserved inert. The
    coverage gate settled 11 more SDK rows through source-verified links and stands at 142 gaps from
    638; those 142 are the O01-O12 packets themselves, each naming its packet, and no entry was
    reclassified to shrink the number. No Tailscale configuration, tailnet device or off-LAN
    request was exercised, so W is still not closeable.
    R6's owner half is real now as well: `POST /v1/remote/enrollment` mints a short-lived code into
    the gateway's own store only when the gateway actually started, `GET /v1/remote/gateway`
    reports the loopback URL or the reason it is absent, and Settings gained a Remote destination
    in the General group that shows the gateway address, issues a code, and lists paired devices
    with a Revoke action. The packaged application was exercised end to end for this: the owner
    route issued a code, the packaged gateway redeemed it for a browser (two cookies, single use -
    a second attempt is 409) and for a native client (a token that then read `/v1/sessions`), and
    the packaged `/` served the exported client with its 1.4 MB bundle and an SPA deep link. The
    Remote panel itself was not captured on screen, `CEDIA_REMOTE_HOSTS` is still an environment
    value with no Cedia control, and no Tailscale Serve configuration or off-LAN request was made.
    O07 has its first real surface: the pinned runtime registers `goal.get`/`goal.set` and a
    `cedia_goal` command answering OMP's own goal state after `get | set | replace | pause |
    resume | drop | complete | budget`, with every gate the terminal's `/goal` applies - plan and
    vibe mode, `goal.enabled`, the no-op refusals, and a `set` on a live goal refused rather than
    overwriting it - and with `set`/`replace` dispatching the objective as the turn exactly as
    `/goal <objective>` does. Continuation stays opt-in through OMP's own `goal.continuationModes`;
    with the shipped default nothing runs on its own, including after a restart. The host projects
    the goal per session (`apps/host/src/omp-progress.ts`) behind the durable, idempotent
    `GET`/`POST /v1/sessions/:id/goal`, preserving the runtime's refusal text and reporting
    `not_dispatched` rather than a fabricated outcome, and the adapter fills the composer goal
    header's objective, elapsed time and paused state from the streamed `goal_updated` frame and
    maps its edit/pause/resume/delete actions back to the runtime.
    `bun scripts/omp-capabilities-smoke.ts` proves that round trip against the prepared runtime with
    a dead model endpoint. Eight audited records settled: three SDK rows linked to the registered
    operations and verified against the *live* table, and `/goal` with its `set`, `pause`, `resume`
    and `drop` subcommands; `show` and `budget` have since closed (the header's budget control with
    its budget/usage read, proven live through the host route). Plan mode, vibe mode, `guided-goal`, loop, AgentHub/hub
    and the advisor surfaces are still open, and no packaged window was captured showing a live
    goal.
    The RPC bridge reconciles the latest persisted `goal`/`goal_paused` mode on its first Cedia
    goal request after startup, using OMP's `GoalRuntime.onThreadResumed()` rule. The isolated
    `bun scripts/omp-goal-restart-smoke.ts` proves both cases separately against a real host/OMP
    restart: an explicitly paused goal stays paused with its id/objective/budget, and a goal active
    at shutdown returns paused with the same id/objective/budget. For the active case, the local
    RPC continuation setting is enabled; the objective request is held at a loopback fixture, no
    new request occurs after recovery until the owner resumes, and exactly one new request follows
    explicit resume. Receipt: [O07 goal restart proof](evidence/o07-goal-restart-live-2026-09-28/findings.md).
    This is a host-backed RPC lifecycle regression proof; it does not close remaining O07 rows or
    constitute packaged/F acceptance.
    The paid O07 follow-up in `scripts/omp-agents-muse-live-proof.ts` verified the exact
    `opencode-go/muse-spark-1.3-contributor` selection and read-only authenticated provider state
    before dispatching bounded `/tan` work; real Muse children reached `parked` under `Main`, but
    live completion-marker/revive acceptance was not established. The local fixture then isolated
    the actual failure: the roster's process-global `AgentRegistry` and the transcript RPC's
    task-subagent observer registry used separate allowlists, so `/tan` JSONL files were refused
    with `Unknown subagent session file`. `get_subagent_messages` now accepts only the exact
    `sessionFile` of a runtime-owned `kind: sub` row; arbitrary paths remain refused. The extended
    canned-loopback proof observes the real `/tan` child JSONL (2 message records), host projection
    (`nextByte` matches 21,528 file bytes), revive to idle and idempotent replay. The patch was
    regenerated and OMP runtime refreshed/attested. One bounded Muse 1.3 turn then verified the
    completion marker, stale-incarnation 409 with no durable receipt, owner revive to idle, and
    unchanged transcript/no unrequested turn. Receipt: [Muse subagent transcript finding](evidence/o07-agents-muse-transcript-2026-09-28/findings.md).
    This proves the live subagent lifecycle slice; broader O07 and F remain open.
    D's prior native-confirm paid run is supported by its retained host journal and scratch disk
    postcondition, but the proof runner did not finish its post-approval read. Its no-paid retry
    stopped while searching for the Muse catalog row. The 2026-09-28 driver diagnosis traced this
    to searching only the picker’s current provider tab; the runner now selects the visible
    `opencode-go` tab first and captures tab/catalog-row diagnostics on failure. No packaged rerun
    was performed. A source-only regression then found that post-approval renderer rediscovery
    still searched for the original disk baseline. The runner now looks for that baseline after
    opening the file and the approved edited text after confirmation; its two renderer-selection
    tests and root typecheck pass. Receipt: [native-confirm runner rediscovery](evidence/o11-native-confirm-runner-reread-2026-09-28/findings.md).
    The provider-tab and post-approval fixes have not been exercised by a packaged rerun. Login
    Item provenance must be resolved before the next packaged proof; D remains open and no
    acceptance is claimed.
    O07's lineage half is real as well: the host seeds a per-session subagent table from the

    classes, activation, bounds and absence reasons with no toggle or refresh control, and the two
    settled SDK rows name that surface in the gate. Enable/disable is real now too: the panel's
    per-row toggles drive the session's own activation path through an owner-only durable command
    that answers the catalog that follows, with replay, stale-incarnation refusal and a 403 for
    controllers. Skill refresh is real too: the panel header's Refresh re-runs the session's own
    rediscovery through the same durable owner-only shape. The MCP refresh is carried by the reachable `/mcp reload` subcommand over the prompt path (the controller rediscovers through its own manager and calls `refreshMCPTools(manager.getTools())`, so Cedia never synthesizes the discovery-produced array); the RPC-host refresh is carried by the
    handshake's `set_host_tools` (named caller link) and command-metadata freshness by the
    runtime's own subscription (source-evidence disposition), and the extension runner handle by
    OMP's own in-process bus (source-evidence disposition — its commands and tools already
    surface), with `reload`, code-mode partitions, the `/extensions` and `status` commands,
    dynamic-tool reclassification and packaged capture.
    The extension toggle is wired now (2026-09-25): the panel's extension rows carry
    per-row Enable/Disable through the owner-only durable `extensions.set` (confirmed,
    replayed, stale-incarnation refused), so the `/extensions` command and its `status`
    alias settle as the Tool catalog panel in the gate (gap count 20); `reload`, code-mode
    partitions, dynamic-tool reclassification and packaged capture stay open. See
    [the extension toggle slice](evidence/o06-extension-toggle-2026-09-25/findings.md).
    The custom-extension dynamic-tool row is settled live (2026-09-25): a trusted
    fixture extension's registered tool reaches the session catalog with source
    `extension` at bridge and host route alike (gap count 19); installing, updating
    and custom-rooting through Cedia stay open. See
    [the extension tool slice](evidence/o06-extension-tool-live-2026-09-25/findings.md).
    The next O06 same-session TypeScript add/remove vertical keeps the existing
    `ExtensionRunner` object identity because AgentSession and tool wrappers capture it.
    OMP must fence new turn and extension dispatch before awaiting reload, drain in-flight
    callbacks and pending tool mutations, stage a complete candidate generation privately,
    and commit tool/provider/command registries with rollback if presentation fails.
    Runner timers and file fallbacks require generation-scoped disposal; `session_shutdown`
    is whole-session teardown and cannot stand in for unload. RPC must publish the command
    catalog only after commit; interactive mode must rebuild extension suggestions and remove
    stale shortcuts. Running-session CLI flags remain parse-time. A model-free same-PID RPC
    fixture must prove add, remove, stale-selection refusal and failed-candidate rollback
    before this is called integrated. The 2026-09-28 follow-up now wires the owning
    SDK/session transaction through RPC, RPC-UI/TUI and ACP; a source-attested, provider-free
    host-backed probe passes same-PID add, replacement, rollback, removal and stale-selection
    refusal on the development runtime. Runner callback quiescence and deferred tool registration
    prevent the live registry transaction from waiting behind itself; interactive shortcuts
    refresh with the committed generation. See [the O06 live transaction receipt](evidence/o06-extension-live-transaction-2026-09-28/findings.md).
    Packaged capture, standalone interactive/ACP concurrent reload, shared-source provider
    ownership with live subagents, and trusted-module import side effects remain unqualified;
    reload refuses while another live session shares the model registry.
    O06/F remains open.
    On an explicit implementation instruction,
    execute this baseline without reopening the completed interview or repeating its approval. R6 targets the selected Tailscale gateway; it does not deploy Paseo relay.
    Record D, W, N and F acceptance separately against every required §8.1/§11 row from the
    actual integrated build. All twelve §8.2 O-packets remain implementation work under this
    item; partial checkpoints cannot close the full OMP target. F is blocked by any missing
    applicable integration, including CLI ownership, advanced management and native OMP
    state that RPC alone does not expose. Optional Synara-only workflows retain §2.3 intake
    status and are not silently promised by the F label. Do not close the whole integration
    target while retained applicable requirements lack acceptance or an explicit owner scope
    change. Dependencies remain explicit.
