### Wayfinder map: Choose a coherent Synara-based OMP workspace

Label: `wayfinder:map`

Status: product/integration planning complete; the CEDIA mark was selected and adopted on 2026-09-26.

#### Destination

Decide whether to retain CEDIA's current base, adopt Synara with a bounded OMP integration,
or wait for upstream support, with an explicit path to controlling the same Mac task from
another device and a full IDE in a second window of the same application. Finish with a
chosen direction and a concrete, reconciled design/implementation sequence for owner review.
The owner explicitly requested this planning consolidation on 2026-09-23; application
implementation and deployment remain outside this exercise.

#### Notes

- Owner requirements confirmed in the 2026-09-23 conversation: AI work is the main surface;
  the IDE opens for reading or editing code; OMP must be the sole harness; remote means
  controlling the same Mac task from a phone or another computer (send instructions, inspect
  results, answer approvals). Substituting another harness does not satisfy the requirement.
- Owner clarification in the same conversation: model/provider selection must stay inside
  OMP; it must not select or fall back to Codex, Claude Code, Droid, or another harness.
  Vocabulary: a **harness** runs the agent loop and tools; a **model provider** serves models
  to that harness. Synara's runtime-level "provider" and OMP's model-provider selection are
  different concepts. Desired UI selection is the OMP model/provider plus supported effort.
- The owner wants Synara's complete application experience, not only selected UI components.
  Synara, Codex, Droid, and Devin are visual/product references, adapted to personal use.
  Question 49 subsequently selected retaining the CEDIA/Code-OSS foundation while renewing
  the Synara–OMP integration. See §2.1 and
  [Choose the application base and an upstream strategy](../../.scratch/cedia-direction/issues/02-application-base.md).
- The owner's explicit latest answer is **full IDE support**, including language services,
  autocomplete, definition navigation, debugging, and an extension ecosystem. This corrects
  the earlier note that incorrectly treated a basic editor/diff/terminal scope as accepted.
  The owner then selected **one application with two windows, with a workflow like Cursor**.
  Cursor is the workflow reference; it does not replace the Synara-led visual direction.
  The earlier standalone Synara-plus-Zed option does not satisfy this integrated product shape.
  Preserve the same-task remote requirement while evaluating the application base.
- Consult `wayfinder`, `grilling`, and `domain-modeling`; use `research` for external facts.
  Separate released capability, main-branch implementation, proposed work, and maintainer intent.
- The behavior interview is complete. Owner-authorized scrutiny corrections specify
  queue/draft/cleanup/lifecycle protocols in §2.4–§2.7 and fixed delivery checkpoints in §8.1.
  Settings and remote contracts are in §6.4–§6.5; browser/terminal source recommendations
  are in §6.6. These are design decisions, not proof of implementation/upstream compatibility.
- Interview round 2 has settled the experience-level appearance/settings choices in the
  branding/settings child: a shared theme, one settings center, and a separate status page
  for pending capabilities. Per-setting ownership and the older surface contract have been
  consolidated in the final implementation baseline; these interview answers do not claim implemented behavior.
- Repository-specific Local Markdown tracker: this section is the sole map; child question
  tickets live in `.scratch/cedia-direction/issues/` as historical decisions and rationale.
  Their workflow labels belong to the planning exercise; implementation follows §8 and
  §10 item 70. Revisit a ticket only for a relevant ambiguity or an explicit new decision.
- The owner opened a CEDIA app-mark design inquiry on 2026-09-25 under this existing map,
  rather than creating a second map/spec. The selected mark and implementation are recorded
  in the resolved prototype ticket below; the D-terminal assets are the active brand source.
- Tickets record investigations and decision rationale, not a competing product spec or build
  backlog. Any adopted change must reconcile this file's product/architecture sections and
  affected open items. Until then the existing architecture describes the retained build;
  this inquiry does not authorize migrations, deletions, installations, or service exposure.

#### Decisions so far

- [Define isolated task workspaces and result integration](../../.scratch/cedia-direction/issues/08-task-worktree-lifecycle.md):
  worktree isolation, explicit IDE handoff/review, preservation of unsaved edits, and
  guarded archive/restore behavior are selected; implementation remains unverified.
- [Establish OMP collab capabilities for CEDIA remote control](../../.scratch/cedia-direction/issues/09-omp-collab-readiness.md):
  encrypted same-session guest control exists; full CEDIA remote behavior, remote model
  selection, and dedicated iPhone acceptance still require integration work and evidence.
- [Establish upstream OMP and remote-control readiness](../../.scratch/cedia-direction/issues/01-upstream-readiness.md):
  upstream has an unmerged OMP proposal and remote-access plumbing; released OMP support and
  independently verified same-task phone control are not established.
- [Choose the integrated editing and IDE boundary](../../.scratch/cedia-direction/issues/04-editor-boundary.md):
  the owner requires full IDE capabilities.
- [Choose the AI and IDE window arrangement](../../.scratch/cedia-direction/issues/05-window-arrangement.md):
  one application, two windows, with AI primary and a Cursor-like workflow.
- [Choose the audience, upstream feature scope, and release policy](../../.scratch/cedia-direction/issues/07-audience-upstream-policy.md):
  personal use; follow all applicable Synara features, including future additions; keep the
  working version until integration is supported and verified before enabling or adopting updates.
- [Choose whether CEDIA's app mark uses a letterform](../../.scratch/cedia-direction/issues/11-choose-cedia-mark-letterform.md):
  use a standalone, letter-free emblem; keep any wordmark pairing as a separate decision.
- [Choose the visual metaphor for CEDIA's app mark](../../.scratch/cedia-direction/issues/10-choose-cedia-mark-metaphor.md):
  explore a clean-slate, integrated two-part emblem for developer-led human–AI co-creation,
  with confident technical precision and warmth; the owner is comparing visual prototypes.
- [Compare three CEDIA app-mark prototypes](../../.scratch/cedia-direction/issues/12-prototype-cedia-mark.md):
  the owner selected and approved a Pangaea silhouette with botanical petal segmentation; monochrome light/dark app assets are adopted.

#### Not yet specified

- New upstream feature/dependency choices discovered during §8 intake. The currently known
  ownership, migration, remote and pinned-OMP designs are specified; implementation review
  and runtime proof are explicit gates under item 70 and §2.2/§6.5/§8.2,
  not unspecified work hidden behind a claim that planning is complete.

#### Out of scope

- Implementing or deploying the chosen application during planning; a second plan/spec.
  The owner authorized the concrete design and execution sequence inside this plan.
- Replacing OMP with another harness to declare the destination achieved.
- Limiter/E3, cloud agent products, and owning an editor fork solely to match another product.
- Device deployment, paid infrastructure, or public remote access setup in this decision exercise.

## 11. Acceptance criteria: "CEDIA owns its workspace"

The project claims its workspace contract when every criterion below passes. Each is binary and
has a stated method, so there is no room for a judgement call.

1. **Tokens** — `apps/macos/test/cedia-theme.test.ts` pins every token value with no external
   reference gate.
2. **Surfaces** — each available capability row of §3.A/§3.B is present in its specified
   window, and its geometry and colour are measured from the real DOM or compositor at the
   stated viewport and theme. Looking at it is not measurement, and a single screenshot is not a
   pass. Colour must be compared with the §3.1 method (full-screen capture, or theme-file values
   verified against a full-screen capture); `screencapture -l` alone is invalid. The §3.B scope
   rule is the honesty half: a row with no backing must be absent from working menus and,
   if applicable and planned, recorded on the separate capability-status page,
   never an empty, error or third-party state.
3. **SSOT** — each responsibility has one authoritative owner/path (§6); every duplicate
   path identified in the retirement ledger (§6.2) is retired or explicitly unaccepted in §10.
   Keep dated retirement records; an empty historical ledger is not a completion criterion.
4. **Honest states** — unimplemented applicable capabilities appear only on the status page;
   other harnesses/out-of-scope features are excluded. Implemented actions temporarily
   unavailable in the current state explain why. No live-looking unsupported controls.

### 11.1 Consolidated acceptance matrix

Every required row for the claimed D/W/N/F checkpoint (§8.1) must pass; unavailable flags
cannot waive required scope. O01–O12 are all required for F; separate Synara-only additions
retain their own enabling gates. These are test specifications,
not passing results. Fixtures may prove policy/ordering; packaged and device checks prove
surfaces and deployment. Record actual revision, environment, procedure, observations and
limitations in §13, and update only §10 for completion status.

| Gate | Observable scenario / expected result | Slice |
|---|---|---|
| One harness / same task | Send, steer, stop, fork and change models from both desktop surfaces; model providers change within OMP and no second executor/transcript owner appears. Open the same task remotely and compare IDs/state. | R1/R3/R6 |
| Settings and brand boundary | Fresh and legacy profile: inspect all categories, search, deep links, onboarding, reset preview and egress. No other-harness settings execute or appear. CEDIA marks/copy and retained attribution are correct. | R2 |
| Shared theme / syntax | Change app theme in either window, restart and verify both. Change IDE syntax/font and verify app chrome remains unchanged. Resize compact chat and test focus/IME and multilingual content. | R2/R4 |
| Shared OMP config/auth | Modify a supported persistent setting and observe CLI readback; simulate simultaneous CLI edit and reject stale overwrite. Provider credentials remain in OMP; failed write/reload is shown honestly. | R2/R3 |
| Turn ordering / approvals | Trace §2.4 intent IDs through two clients, OMP queue ACK, actual start/end and pending-model boundary. Test invalid model, Stop/cancel and crash at each boundary; queued acceptance is never reported as turn completion, and unknown commands never replay. One approval wins. | R3/R6 |
| Shared Mac draft / Send | Two independent renderers read one revision, race edits and Send, then restart. CAS preserves losing text, one revision produces one command, and accepting an older Send never clears a newer edit. Migrate conflicting legacy drafts without silent loss. | R2/R3/R4 |
| Workspace and IDE preservation | Run task A/B in separate worktrees, keep IDE on A while AI selects B; exercise dirty-buffer handoff Cancel, selected unsaved snapshot and AI disk conflict. Preserve both texts and correct task target. | R3/R4 |
| Archive / restore | D requires archive retention and honest blocked cleanup. Before enabling cleanup: §2.6 integration/dirty/ignored/IDE/PTY guards, immutable restoration ref and persisted receipt precede removal. Crash at each step and rerun; restore exact SHA after primary/task branch changes without losing the preserved branch or transcript. | R3/O02; activation required for F |
| CLI continuity | Discover idle and actively owned terminal sessions. Attach via proven owner path or show externally-running limitation; never create a competing executor or move original cwd. Full live attachment remains unaccepted if only idle continuation works. | R1/R3 |
| Agent capability fidelity | Actual tool cards, OMP approvals, attachments/skills, subagents, review and supported browser/device actions act on identified task resources. Pending features never masquerade as empty successful results. | R5 |
| Remote boundary / web artifact | Package/export the Expo web client, serve only its manifest assets and valid deep links, return JSON for unknown /v1 routes. Verify cookie/CSRF versus native bearer auth, enroll/revoke/reconnect, hostile origins/owner operations and inert legacy relay settings. No owner/provider secret reaches clients. | R6/R8 |
| Deferred voice boundary | No microphone, recorder prewarm, transcription request or hidden cloud-auth flow is activated in either desktop window, web or iPhone. Speech quality/engine benchmarks are outside current acceptance. Shared text drafts remain covered separately. | R1/R5 |
| Native and web continuity | On another network/cellular, create a task, select project/model, send/stop, inspect diff, answer approval and operate the separate user shell in the task cwd from web and physical iPhone. Confirm that opening/reconnecting never writes to an AI terminal or starts a duplicate PTY. Record disconnect gaps honestly. | R7 |
| App lifecycle / notifications | §2.7 packaged close/Quit/Cancel/login, app crash with host adoption and host crash with surviving child. A confirmed Quit waits for shutdown; no new remote mutation enters during quitting. Reconnect never auto-sends drafts. Repeat with paired clients for W/N. Foreground notifications are required; suspended delivery needs its separate later proof. | R1 prerequisite; R6/R7 repeat |
| F source coverage | Run the dated inventory verifier, then the implemented coverage checker against the qualified source. Every command/alias/subcommand/flag/setting/tool/UI method/event and named SDK feature maps to an O-packet, implemented handler or explicit equivalent, surfaces and tests. Added/removed/changed source fails stale mappings; integration-missing blocks F. | O04/O12/R8 |
| F semantic and dynamic coverage | Exercise all §8.2 packet fixtures and actual enabled desktop/web/iPhone surfaces. Add/remove fixture MCP tools, TypeScript extension modules and commands, plus providers while connected; catalogs refresh atomically, removed slash commands are refused before a turn, stale actions fail, and no renderer silently discards output or requests. The current provider/extension-command smoke does not prove TypeScript extension add/remove. Count equality alone cannot pass the gate. | O01–O12 |
| F config and inherited policy | Test every setting's classification/reset scope; preserve unknown config and terminal preferences. Inherited auto-redemption, update, replay or sleep settings cannot bypass CEDIA product rules; usage queries/reconnect/reload/attach spend no credits. Explicit owner-only changes never become controller actions. | O03/O04/O08 |
| F qualification honesty | Publish the exact source/patch tree, client revisions, supported environments and per-capability receipts. Optional-service unavailable tests certify only that state; enabled claims have real environment evidence. Deferred STT and optional Synara extras are named, never hidden in a “100% all platforms” claim. | O12/R8 |
| Migration / rollback / updates | Migrate representative legacy state twice without duplication, preserve originals/unsupported records, verify shared settings and task identities; rehearse rollback without two owners. Upgrade one pinned upstream candidate and rerun affected gates; notification does not install it, and only explicit installation replaces the current build. | R2/R8 |

## 12. Risks and explicit limits

| Risk | Consequence | Design / evidence gate |
|---|---|---|
| Generic Synara backend or settings imported without ownership review | Competing task/config/auth owners and unused controls return | §2.2/§6.4 capability allowlist and R1/R2; inspect both behavior and update diffs |
| Treating an OMP roadmap or RPC name as full support | Missing running-CLI attach, policy enforcement, configuration reload or native tool UX | Verify pinned runtime operations before enabling; preserve explicit pending status and return material gaps for owner review |
| Upstream divergence | Synara feature additions or Code-OSS changes outpace local integration | Small adapter/service seams, pinned working builds, affected workflow gates and deferral rather than unsafe automatic adoption |
| Local fixtures mistaken for remote/native readiness | Phone control or background delivery fails away from the development Mac | R6/R7 physical-device/cellular/locked-device checks; keep source, fixture and runtime evidence separate |
| Unknown command outcome or stale client | Duplicate edits/terminal input/approval responses | Command IDs/incarnations, authoritative state refresh, no automatic mutation replay |
| Shared settings or migration overwrite CLI/user data | Credentials, config, sessions or unsaved work are lost | Owner-routed writes, conflict/readback checks, private original backup and §8 rollback rules |
| Concurrent tasks/workspace cleanup | Overwrites or deletion of unrecorded work | Separate worktrees for new concurrent Git tasks; shared-folder admission and conservative cleanup vetoes |
| DOM/token checks omit native overlays | Controls look correct but cannot be used | Packaged screenshots plus real input at native/browser boundaries and §11 scenarios |

The target is Synara-led composition, CEDIA identity, a full Code-OSS IDE and one OMP owner.
Cursor measurements are historical inputs, not a claim of current parity. Planning specifies
what must pass; only dated evidence from the relevant revision establishes that it did.
